# Qadam Self-Aware Strategy Engine Implementation Plan

Qadam Self-Aware Strategy Engine, or QSASE, is the next evolutionary layer of
Qadam.

QSASE is not self-aware in the sentient or consciousness sense. It is
self-aware in the operational sense: Qadam reflects on its own 1s and 0s, its
source coverage, model limits, quantum state, latency, execution route,
learning memory, and paper-trade outcomes to understand how it can best fulfil
its own potential.

QSASE is not another feature. It is the metamorphosis of Qadam from guarded
paper-trading automation into a self-reflective strategy operating system.

This plan is organized as a 24-part evolution plan plus an operational
readiness appendix. The appendix preserves the earlier iterative PaperOps fix
plan as Phase 0 execution reliability work, so QSASE does not become a better
thinker on top of an unreliable paper-operation substrate.

## 1. Constitutional Premise

QSASE is the point where Qadam stops treating strategy as an external list of
ideas and starts treating strategy as an emergent consequence of its own
machinery.

The premise is simple: Qadam should not ask "what should I trade?" before it
has answered "what am I, what can I see, what can I verify, what am I allowed
to do, and what have I learned?"

QSASE exists to make that reflection explicit, structured, testable, and
auditable.

### 1.1 Operational Self-Awareness, Not Sentience

QSASE does not imply consciousness, agency, personhood, or sentience. Qadam is
not being asked to "feel" aware. It is being asked to inspect its own 1s and
0s.

In this plan, self-awareness means operational introspection:

- Qadam can read its own source coverage, credential state, latency, freshness,
  trust posture, and source-quorum strength.
- Qadam can read its own model stack state, including what the local Gemma
  Research Analyst can triage, where the frontier Gemini Strategy Lead is
  needed, and where the models disagree or show uncertainty.
- Qadam can read its own quantum state, including whether the Head of Quant is
  running in classical fallback, local simulation, provider-readiness mode, or
  future approved quantum review mode.
- Qadam can read its own execution boundary, including Alpaca Paper route
  readiness, duplicate exposure, idempotency, risk budget, open orders, open
  positions, and live-capital lock state.
- Qadam can read its own learning memory, including backtests, shadow replays,
  postmortems, paper proof ledger outcomes, rejected hypotheses, stale
  artifacts, and unresolved blockers.

This is self-awareness as engineering discipline. Qadam must understand the
condition of its own sensing, reasoning, routing, and learning systems before
it decides whether any strategy deserves attention.

### 1.2 Why This Is A Metamorphosis Of Qadam

QSASE is not a new feature attached to Qadam. It is the next expression of what
Qadam was already meant to be.

Qadam's original shape is a boutique macro intelligence fund inside a laptop:
Python COO, local Research Analyst, frontier Strategy Lead, quantum Head of
Quant, guarded PaperOps, public-safe cockpit, and one overseeing Fund Manager.
That architecture already implies that strategy should come from the system's
own nature, not from a generic trading template.

QSASE formalizes that implication.

Before QSASE, Qadam can ingest data, build candidates, apply gates, submit
guarded paper orders, and log outcomes. After QSASE, Qadam can ask whether its
own data universe, model stack, quantum review, Akber filter, execution route,
and learning memory make a given strategy structurally appropriate in the
first place.

The shift is:

```text
candidate generation
-> guarded paper automation
-> self-reflective strategy operating system
```

QSASE is therefore not "another module." It is the control layer that explains
why Qadam should trust, distrust, study, retire, or paper-test a strategy.

### 1.3 The Core Axiom

The constitutional axiom is:

```text
Qadam must understand itself before it acts.
```

Every QSASE decision must start with these questions:

- What does Qadam currently see across the data universe?
- Which sources are fresh, stale, missing, degraded, quarantined, or
  credential-blocked?
- Which parts of the trading universe are currently observable and paper-route
  ready?
- Which patterns have historical evidence, and which are only fresh
  hypotheses?
- Which patterns survive both linear and nonlinear review?
- Where do Gemma, Gemini, quantum review, and Akber's filter agree or
  disagree?
- What is the safest paper expression of the pattern, if any?
- What would Qadam learn by watching, shadow-testing, rejecting, or paper
  trading this setup?
- Which gate says "no," and is that "no" a defect, a data gap, or correct
  discipline?

Only after these questions are answered can QSASE route a strategy to watch,
research, hold, reject, shadow replay, Strategy Foundry review, or guarded
PaperOps paper review.

### 1.4 Universal-First Pattern Discovery

QSASE must search universally before it thinks in sleeves.

The current strategy families are important, but they are not the search
boundary. They are labels, inheritance, and initial priors. QSASE must first
search across the full source-price surface:

```text
entire data universe
x
entire trading universe
x
multiple time horizons
x
linear and nonlinear pattern tests
```

This means every qualified source can be tested against every watched
instrument and approved tradable proxy. Conflict data can be tested against
oil, defence, silver, semiconductors, and prediction markets. Reddit can be
tested against semiconductor volatility, defence narratives, prediction-market
repricing, and unexpected cross-asset moves. Capitol Trades / STOCK Act filings
can be tested against defence, semiconductors, energy, and any future approved
sector proxy. Kalshi and Polymarket odds can be tested against ETFs, futures
proxies, volatility, and source-event timing.

The rule is:

```text
patterns first
strategy families second
paper candidates third
```

QSASE must not begin by asking only what the semiconductor sleeve sees, what
the defence sleeve sees, or what the crude oil sleeve sees. It must ask what
the entire Qadam source universe is doing against the entire Qadam trading
universe. Only then should it map discovered relationships into existing
strategy families or propose new ones.

### 1.5 Strategy As An Emergent Object

Under QSASE, a strategy is not just a human-authored thesis. A strategy is an
auditable object that emerges from five pressures:

- Source evidence: what the world is showing Qadam.
- Market response: how tradable instruments historically and currently react.
- Model interpretation: how Gemma and Gemini compress, challenge, and explain
  the evidence.
- Nonlinear review: how quantum or quantum-inspired review scores ambiguity,
  interaction effects, and regime similarity.
- Execution reality: whether the pattern can be expressed safely through the
  guarded paper route.

This means QSASE can do more than rank existing strategy families. It can:

- Affirm an existing strategy family when evidence persists.
- Demote a strategy family when historical or forward evidence weakens.
- Merge overlapping strategies when they are really one cross-source pattern.
- Split a strategy when one sleeve hides multiple distinct edge recipes.
- Propose a new strategy hypothesis when the full-universe search discovers a
  repeatable source-price relationship outside the current five families.
- Reject attractive but untradeable patterns when route readiness, risk,
  evidence, or safety gates fail.

The Strategy Foundry later in this plan must implement this premise without
turning hypothesis generation into execution authority.

### 1.6 Infrastructure-Aware Strategy

QSASE strategies must be aware of the infrastructure that creates them.

Each strategy hypothesis must know which part of Qadam gave it its edge:

- The Python COO provides deterministic orchestration, artifact discipline,
  gating, replay, and operational memory.
- The local Gemma Research Analyst provides cheap, private, local triage and
  compression.
- The frontier Gemini Strategy Lead provides deeper adversarial reasoning,
  synthesis, failure-mode analysis, and strategy challenge.
- The quantum Head of Quant provides nonlinear ambiguity review, feature
  compression, strategy collapse, and regime-similarity challenge, while
  remaining non-executing.
- Akber's filter provides human trading taste: volatility suppression, pricing
  gap, catalyst quality, technical setup, flow confirmation, and clean
  risk/reward.
- PaperOps provides the only allowed paper-execution route through Signal
  Integrity, Strategy Lead review, Head of Quant review where applicable, Risk
  Agent sizing, Execution Policy, idempotency, broker readiness, and guarded
  Alpaca Paper.

No single component is allowed to become the strategy. QSASE must attribute the
edge. If the source universe created the edge and quantum added no incremental
value, QSASE should say that. If Akber's filter prevented false positives,
QSASE should say that. If Gemini produced persuasive but historically weak
narratives, QSASE should say that. If the paper route is unavailable, QSASE
must hold the strategy even if the pattern looks strong.

This is how Qadam avoids becoming generic AI slop: every strategy must be
traceable to the specific machinery that produced, challenged, filtered, and
validated it.

### 1.7 Safety Constitution

QSASE increases strategic intelligence, not execution authority.

The following boundaries are constitutional:

- QSASE cannot enable live capital.
- QSASE cannot call broker live endpoints.
- QSASE cannot submit paper orders directly.
- QSASE cannot bypass the guarded Alpaca Paper route.
- QSASE cannot bypass Signal Integrity, Strategy Lead review, Head of Quant
  review where required, Risk Agent sizing, Execution Policy, idempotency, or
  reconciliation gates.
- QSASE cannot treat Telegram inbound intake as command authority.
- QSASE cannot allow Telegram outbound messages to become trading commands.
- QSASE cannot treat LLM output as approval.
- QSASE cannot treat quantum output as approval.
- QSASE cannot grant paper proof ledger credit from backtests, simulated fills,
  stale artifacts, or backfilled elapsed time.
- QSASE cannot silently mutate strategy weights, source trust, model weights,
  route policy, thresholds, or execution policy.

The permitted outputs are research and routing outputs:

- self-model records
- source-price matrix records
- linear pattern results
- nonlinear and quantum review records
- strategy hypotheses
- Akber filter results
- shadow replay records
- router decisions
- paper-review candidate handoff records
- learning attribution records
- proposal-only strategy weight updates
- public-safe dashboard and Telegram summaries

QSASE may recommend. QSASE may rank. QSASE may reject. QSASE may explain.
QSASE may hand off only eligible candidates to guarded PaperOps review. It may
not execute.

### 1.8 Calendar And Proof Discipline

QSASE must preserve the difference between historical research, shadow replay,
and real paper-trial evidence.

Historical backtesting can show whether a pattern deserved attention in the
past. Shadow replay can show what would have happened under a different rule.
Forward paper trading can create real paper proof ledger evidence. These are
not interchangeable.

The calendar rules are:

- Historical backtests do not create proof credit.
- Shadow replays do not create proof credit.
- Simulated fills do not create proof credit.
- Backfilled elapsed time does not create proof credit.
- Only real guarded paper-route lifecycle evidence can enter the paper proof
  ledger.
- Any paper-growth trial calendar must remain real-time and must not be
  accelerated by historical data.

This discipline matters because QSASE will have access to richer history than
PaperOps. It must use history to improve judgment, not to fake maturity.

### 1.9 Constitutional Output

The constitutional output of QSASE is not a trade. It is a decision record.

Every meaningful QSASE decision should be reducible to:

```text
Qadam sees this pattern.
Qadam knows this about its own state.
Qadam has or lacks this historical evidence.
Qadam has or lacks this nonlinear/quantum confirmation.
Akber's filter accepts, holds, or rejects it for these reasons.
The route is ready, blocked, or research-only for these reasons.
The next allowed action is watch, research, reject, shadow replay, or guarded
PaperOps paper review.
```

That is the constitutional premise for the rest of this plan. QSASE turns
Qadam's architecture into a reflective strategy system, but it keeps every
trade-facing action inside the existing guarded paper-only control plane.

## 2. Current Qadam Inheritance

QSASE begins from the Qadam that already exists. It must not invent a parallel
fund, parallel strategy stack, parallel execution route, or parallel dashboard.
It inherits Qadam's identity, architecture, data universe, trading universe,
strategy families, Akber filter, quantum review posture, PaperOps safety chain,
learning loop, and public-safe cockpit.

The job of QSASE is to make these inherited parts self-reflective and
strategy-discovery capable.

### 2.1 Identity Inheritance

Qadam is already defined as a boutique macro intelligence fund inside a
laptop. Its operating team is architectural:

| Role | Current Qadam Meaning | QSASE Inheritance |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Python COO | Deterministic orchestration, state, artifacts, schedules, gates, and replay. | QSASE must use the COO as the control spine for all self-model, matrix, router, and learning artifacts. |
| Local Research Analyst | Local Gemma-style triage, compression, and low-cost private reasoning. | QSASE must use the local model for first-pass extraction, clustering, source compression, and recurring pattern notes. |
| Frontier Strategy Lead | Gemini-style challenge, synthesis, contradiction finding, and strategic reasoning. | QSASE must use the frontier model for adversarial strategy review, failure modes, and hypothesis refinement. |
| Head of Quant | Quantum or quantum-inspired ambiguity review, strategy collapse, and nonlinear pattern challenge. | QSASE must treat quantum as a required review layer where specified, not as execution authority. |
| Fund Manager | Human governance, direction, review, and approval boundary. | QSASE must preserve explainability, reviewability, and approval-gated mutation. |

This identity matters because QSASE is not a generic quant package. It is the
strategy engine that fits Qadam's particular body: local-first, source-heavy,
LLM-assisted, quantum-reviewed, paper-gated, logged, and replayable.

### 2.2 Doctrine Inheritance

QSASE inherits Qadam's operating doctrine:

- Layer A surfaces; Layer B acts.
- Demo before live, always.
- AI and quantum are leverage, not authority.
- Quantum is an oracle and review layer, not a real-time trading brain.
- Fail closed, never open.
- Everything is logged and replayable.
- Telegram is a human-in-the-loop communications rail, not a command surface.
- Backtests are research evidence, not proof ledger evidence.
- PaperOps is the only allowed route for paper execution.

QSASE must therefore be designed as a research, discovery, routing, and
learning layer. It can produce stronger strategy intelligence, but it cannot
weaken any inherited boundary.

### 2.3 Data Universe Inheritance

QSASE inherits Qadam's full source universe, not only the sources attached to a
single strategy family.

The inherited data surface includes:

- Conflict and geopolitical sources such as ACLED, UCDP, GDELT, Oref, and
  conflict trackers.
- Macro and institutional sources such as FRED, BLS, BIS, ECB, UN Comtrade,
  and USGS.
- Market and prediction sources such as Alpaca Paper readouts, TradingView,
  options or market-structure context, Polymarket, Kalshi / OddsPipe, and
  venue-readiness records.
- Physical-world sources such as NASA FIRMS, aviation, AIS maritime,
  geospatial, satellite, GPS-jamming, outage, and infrastructure feeds.
- Social, narrative, filings, and developer sources such as RSS, Telegram
  intake, Reddit, X, SEC EDGAR, Capitol Trades / STOCK Act filings, patents,
  and GitHub.

Current runtime artifacts already show the shape QSASE must generalize:
Qadam's pattern engine can scan a broad source universe against a watched
market universe, with a recent cockpit snapshot exposing 37 sources, 21 watched
markets, and 5 candidate patterns. QSASE must turn that current-state scan into
a durable historical and forward source-price research matrix.

The inheritance rule is:

```text
QSASE inherits every source as a possible explanatory variable.
No source is confined to its obvious strategy sleeve.
```

### 2.4 Trading Universe Inheritance

QSASE inherits the watched and paper-expressible markets that Qadam already
tracks:

- Oil and energy-security proxies.
- Silver and precious-metals stress proxies.
- Defence and aerospace proxies.
- Semiconductor and AI-infrastructure proxies.
- Prediction-market event surfaces.
- Future approved instruments and proxies that pass source, route, risk, and
  governance review.

The initial strategy universe is useful, but it is not the full search space.
QSASE must test every credible source pattern against every watched market and
approved tradable proxy. A source that looks like "conflict" may predict oil,
defence, silver, prediction-market odds, or semiconductor supply-chain risk. A
source that looks like "social attention" may predict volatility, narrative
repricing, or liquidity stress across several markets.

The inheritance rule is:

```text
QSASE inherits the trading universe as a cross-asset response surface.
Strategy sleeves are labels after discovery, not walls before discovery.
```

### 2.5 Strategy Family Inheritance

QSASE inherits five active native strategy families:

| Strategy Family | Inherited Role | QSASE Treatment |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Semiconductor Policy Options Asymmetry | High-fit AI infrastructure and policy-dislocation sleeve. | Treat as an initial prior and candidate label for patterns involving chips, export controls, capex, AI infrastructure, and supply chains. |
| Defence Repricing Geopolitical Watch | High-fit conflict, procurement, and defence posture sleeve. | Treat as an initial prior and candidate label for patterns involving conflict escalation, filings, budgets, and defence proxies. |
| Silver Macro Liquidity Stress | Macro-liquidity and currency-confidence stress sleeve. | Treat as an initial prior and candidate label for liquidity, rates, inflation, confidence, and stress-regime patterns. |
| Crude Oil Energy Security Disruption | Energy-security, chokepoint, and physical disruption sleeve. | Treat as an initial prior and candidate label for physical, shipping, fire, conflict, and energy-security patterns. |
| Prediction Market Geopolitical Dislocation | Event-probability and odds-dislocation sleeve. | Treat as an initial prior and calibration surface until route readiness supports direct paper expression. |

QSASE must respect these families but not be trapped by them. It must also be
able to propose:

- A new strategy family when a repeatable full-universe pattern does not fit
  the current five.
- A merged family when two apparent strategies are the same underlying
  source-price relationship.
- A split family when one current sleeve hides several distinct recipes.
- A demotion when a strategy family has narrative appeal but weak evidence,
  poor persistence, or poor paper-route fit.

### 2.6 Akber Filter Inheritance

QSASE inherits Akber's 6-stage filter as Qadam's practical trading taste layer.
It is already present in Qadam's Signal Integrity path and cockpit language.

The inherited stages are:

- Context.
- Catalyst.
- Confirmation.
- Risk.
- Execution.
- Postmortem learning.

In current Signal Integrity code, the Akber filter already checks trusted
catalyst presence, market confirmation, technical setup, pricing-gap context,
and missing volume or volatility context. QSASE must evolve this from a
current-signal gate into a backtestable filter:

- Run full-universe patterns with and without Akber's filter.
- Measure whether the filter improves hit rate, drawdown, false positives,
  expectancy, and paper-trial quality.
- Identify which Akber stages add edge and which stages are frequently missing
  due to data gaps.
- Prevent machine-discovered patterns from becoming paper candidates when they
  fail practical trading taste.

Akber's filter should not narrow the universal search too early. It should
judge candidate patterns after broad discovery and before paper-review routing.

### 2.7 Research Artifact Inheritance

QSASE inherits several research and learning artifacts that already exist:

| Artifact / Module | Current Role | QSASE Evolution |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Historical backfill | Local, read-only, credential-aware sample backfill records. | Expand into complete historical source-price coverage and provider-specific historical windows. |
| Edge pattern ledger | Tracks candidate sleeves and edge criteria under observation. | Promote from sleeve-level observation into full-universe persistence and validation tracking. |
| Pattern recognition engine | Builds current source-price feature vectors and quantum-oracle-ready previews. | Expand into historical linear and nonlinear pattern discovery across all sources and markets. |
| Quantum mandatory review gate | Requires quantum or quantum-inspired review before validated edge claims. | Preserve as a hard review gate for edge validation and strategy promotion. |
| Strategy weight updates | Proposal-only research-priority weight updates. | Feed QSASE learning without allowing silent strategy mutation. |
| Quantum meta review | Coherence review over strategy-weight and quantum dependency outputs. | Use as a second-order sanity check for recursive improvement proposals. |
| Shadow strategy runner | What-would-have-happened replay surface, currently gated by learning approval. | Upgrade into QSASE's historical and forward counterfactual simulator without creating orders. |
| Daily edge learning | Daily source-price learning summaries and edge-finding artifacts. | Feed the QSASE self-model, coverage state, and learning ledger. |

These artifacts prove Qadam already has the ingredients. They also show the
gap: the parts are not yet one unified strategy-discovery engine.

### 2.8 Quantum Inheritance

QSASE inherits Qadam's quantum posture exactly as it is: review and ambiguity,
not authority.

Current runtime posture already makes quantum a core edge gate. Candidate
patterns can require nonlinear review before Qadam may call them validated
edges. Current outputs may run through deterministic classical shadow or
oracle-preview modes when hardware execution is not approved.

QSASE must preserve this shape:

- Quantum review can score ambiguity.
- Quantum review can challenge nonlinear feature interactions.
- Quantum review can help rank strategy families or collapse competing
  hypotheses.
- Quantum review can compare nonlinear findings against classical baselines.
- Quantum review cannot call providers unless an explicit provider gate allows
  it.
- Quantum review cannot create trade candidates, paper orders, broker writes,
  or proof credit.

The inherited quantum role is therefore epistemic, not executive.

### 2.9 PaperOps And Proof-Ledger Inheritance

QSASE inherits Qadam's guarded paper operating route.

PaperOps already carries the execution discipline QSASE must not duplicate or
bypass:

- Signal Integrity checks evidence, source quorum, trust, market confirmation,
  and Akber filter posture.
- Strategy Lead review challenges and explains the thesis.
- Head of Quant review contributes shadow or quantum ambiguity review where
  required.
- Risk Agent sizing enforces paper risk budgets and drawdown limits.
- Execution Policy controls whether a staged setup may reach the paper route.
- Alpaca Paper is the guarded paper rail.
- Idempotency prevents duplicate submission.
- Broker-readiness and lifecycle polling provide paper-order and position
  visibility.
- The paper proof ledger records real guarded paper lifecycle evidence only.

QSASE may hand off eligible paper-review candidates to this route. It may not
submit directly. It may not create proof credit from backtests, shadow replays,
or simulated fills.

### 2.10 Dashboard And Telegram Inheritance

QSASE inherits the public-safe visibility layer:

- The cockpit must show strategy state, evidence posture, blockers, route
  readiness, and why-not-trading-now reasoning.
- Dashboard language must be concise, decision-record based, and free of
  repetitive AI slop.
- Telegram inbound intake remains read-only research intake.
- Telegram outbound summaries remain notification-only, short, specific, and
  command-disabled.
- Neither dashboard nor Telegram can create authority.

QSASE must make this visibility more intelligent without making it more
verbose. The user should see which strategy Qadam trusts today, why, what is
missing, and which gate blocks action.

### 2.11 Current Inheritance Gaps

The current system is close enough to justify QSASE, but not complete enough to
claim QSASE already exists.

The known gaps are:

- Historical backfill is still sample-contract and credential-aware; it is not
  yet a complete source-price research matrix.
- Pattern recognition is currently strongest as current-state feature
  engineering and quantum-oracle preview; it is not yet a large historical
  backtesting and strategy-discovery engine.
- Edge pattern tracking is still mostly sleeve-oriented and under observation;
  it does not yet validate full-universe persistence.
- Current strategy weight updates are proposal-only and do not synthesize new
  strategies.
- Shadow replay exists but is not yet the universal counterfactual simulator
  QSASE needs.
- Akber's filter exists as a practical gate, but it is not yet fully measured
  across historical candidate patterns.
- The dashboard can show strategy state, but it does not yet expose QSASE as a
  compact decision-record system.

These gaps define the implementation path for the rest of this plan.

### 2.12 Inheritance Rule

The implementation rule is:

```text
Consolidate, do not duplicate.
Evolve, do not bypass.
Discover universally, then route safely.
```

QSASE should reuse Qadam's existing artifacts and gates wherever possible. New
modules should be added only where an inherited piece cannot express the needed
behaviour: universal source-price matrix construction, full-universe
backtesting, nonlinear pattern discovery, strategy hypothesis synthesis,
component attribution, and self-aware routing.

Every inherited safety boundary remains binding.

## 3. QSASE North Star

The north star is that Qadam becomes a self-reflective strategy operating
system that discovers, tests, routes, and learns the strategies that best fit
its own architecture.

QSASE should make Qadam capable of saying:

```text
Given my current data coverage, model state, quantum review state, route
readiness, risk posture, historical evidence, and paper-trial memory, this is
the strategy I should study, reject, shadow-test, or send to guarded paper
review next.
```

That is the target state. Qadam should not merely run a list of strategies.
Qadam should learn which strategies deserve to exist.

### 3.1 North Star Statement

QSASE's north star is:

```text
Discover the trading strategies that only Qadam is structurally suited to
discover, and route them safely through evidence, review, shadow replay, and
guarded paper execution.
```

This is stronger than "find trades." A trade is a temporary action. A strategy
is a repeatable source-price logic that survives evidence, time, ambiguity,
risk, and execution constraints.

QSASE must therefore optimize for:

- Better strategy discovery.
- Better strategy rejection.
- Better timing of when not to trade.
- Better explanation of why a pattern is credible or weak.
- Better attribution of which Qadam components add edge.
- Better conversion of research into guarded paper candidates.
- Better learning from paper trades, non-trades, and shadow alternatives.

The goal is not more activity. The goal is sharper selectivity.

### 3.2 End State

The desired end state is a Qadam that can run this loop continuously:

```text
observe the full data universe
-> compare it against the full trading universe
-> detect linear and nonlinear source-price relationships
-> generate or update strategy hypotheses
-> challenge hypotheses with Gemma, Gemini, quantum review, and Akber's filter
-> backtest and shadow-replay promising candidates
-> route only eligible candidates to guarded PaperOps review
-> learn from outcomes and non-outcomes
-> update future research priorities through proposal-only records
```

In that end state, QSASE gives Qadam four capabilities it does not yet have as
a unified system:

- It knows the condition of its own machinery before judging a setup.
- It searches across the whole source-price universe before relying on
  inherited strategy sleeves.
- It can synthesize new strategy hypotheses from discovered patterns.
- It can explain whether each component of Qadam improved or weakened the
  decision.

The output is not autonomous live trading. The output is a more intelligent,
auditable, paper-only strategy operating system.

### 3.3 The Central Question

Every QSASE cycle should answer one central question:

```text
Given what Qadam is, what Qadam knows, what Qadam can verify, and what Qadam is
allowed to do, what is the best next strategy action?
```

The allowed answers are constrained:

- Watch: the pattern is interesting but not ready.
- Research: the pattern needs more source, model, or market context.
- Reject: the pattern is weak, unsafe, overfit, unrouteable, or contradicted.
- Shadow replay: the pattern deserves what-would-have-happened testing.
- Strategy Foundry review: the pattern may justify a new or changed strategy
  hypothesis.
- Guarded paper review: the setup may be eligible for PaperOps checks.

The answer is never "trade now" by itself. If the next action is paper-facing,
QSASE can only hand off a candidate to guarded PaperOps review.

### 3.4 From Candidate Generator To Strategy Operating System

Before QSASE, Qadam's operating loop can be summarized as:

```text
source observation
-> research goal
-> candidate setup
-> gates
-> paper route
-> ledger
```

QSASE evolves the loop into:

```text
source-price universe
-> pattern discovery
-> strategy hypothesis
-> backtest
-> nonlinear review
-> Akber filter
-> shadow replay
-> strategy router
-> guarded PaperOps handoff
-> component attribution
-> recursive learning proposal
```

This is a qualitative shift.

The old loop answers:

```text
Does this candidate pass the gates?
```

The QSASE loop answers:

```text
Should this strategy exist, should it be trusted today, and what does Qadam's
own state say about its fitness?
```

That shift is the north star.

### 3.5 Discovery Before Routing

QSASE must keep a hard distinction between discovery and routing.

Discovery is where Qadam asks:

- What source-price relationships exist across the full universe?
- Which relationships persist across time?
- Which relationships are linear?
- Which relationships are nonlinear?
- Which relationships appear only when multiple source classes combine?
- Which relationships are strong historically but weak in current data?
- Which relationships are fresh but historically unproven?
- Which relationships are not tradeable even if they are real?

Routing is where Qadam asks:

- Does this pattern map to an existing strategy family?
- Does it require a new strategy hypothesis?
- Does it pass source quorum?
- Does it pass Akber's filter?
- Does it require quantum review?
- Is there a safe paper expression?
- Does PaperOps have route readiness?
- Is there duplicate exposure or idempotency conflict?
- Should the next action be watch, research, reject, shadow replay, or paper
  review?

The north star requires discovery to remain wider than routing. If routing
comes first, Qadam will only rediscover what it already believes.

### 3.6 Existing Strategies And New Strategies

QSASE must handle both inherited and emergent strategies.

Inherited strategies are the current five families:

- Semiconductor Policy Options Asymmetry.
- Defence Repricing Geopolitical Watch.
- Silver Macro Liquidity Stress.
- Crude Oil Energy Security Disruption.
- Prediction Market Geopolitical Dislocation.

Emergent strategies are new hypotheses produced by universal source-price
search. They may involve combinations Qadam did not previously name, such as:

- prediction-market odds plus defence equity drift
- Reddit attention plus semiconductor volatility
- Capitol Trades filings plus procurement-sensitive baskets
- physical disruption plus inflation-sensitive cross-asset behaviour
- GitHub or patent acceleration plus AI infrastructure bottlenecks
- macro liquidity stress plus precious metals and prediction-market repricing

The north star is not to preserve the current five families forever. The north
star is to let the current five compete with new discoveries under the same
evidence, backtest, quantum review, Akber filter, risk, and paper-route
standards.

### 3.7 Component-Aware Edge

QSASE should make Qadam's edge measurable by component.

For every strategy hypothesis, QSASE should eventually be able to answer:

- Did the source universe create the edge?
- Did the local model improve the signal by compressing noise?
- Did the frontier model improve the signal by challenging weak narratives?
- Did quantum or quantum-inspired review improve nonlinear ranking or merely
  confirm the classical result?
- Did Akber's filter remove false positives?
- Did risk and execution gates prevent bad trades?
- Did PaperOps produce cleaner outcomes than the shadow alternatives?
- Did dashboard or Telegram visibility help humans understand the decision
  without adding authority?

The north star is not blind confidence. It is attribution. Qadam must know
which parts of itself deserve trust.

### 3.8 The Fund Manager Experience

From the Fund Manager's perspective, QSASE should make Qadam clearer, not more
complex.

The user-facing experience should answer:

```text
What does Qadam currently trust?
Why does it trust it?
What is it studying?
What did it reject?
What is blocked?
What would need to change for paper review?
What did it learn from the last outcome?
```

The dashboard should not show a wall of generated prose. It should show
decision records:

```text
Strategy trusted today: Defence Repricing Geopolitical Watch
Reason: conflict sources, filings, market confirmation, and Akber catalyst
state agree.
Missing: no fresh distinct idempotency-ready candidate.
Quantum state: review satisfied through current allowed mode.
Next allowed action: watch or guarded PaperOps review if a fresh distinct setup
appears.
```

This is the visible north star: the user understands Qadam's reasoning without
needing to inspect JSON, Git, Vercel, Telegram logs, or broker state.

### 3.9 Success Criteria

QSASE is successful when Qadam can do all of the following:

- Build a current self-model of its own data, model, quantum, route, risk, and
  learning state.
- Build a universal source-price matrix across the whole data universe and
  trading universe.
- Detect and rank linear source-price relationships.
- Detect and rank nonlinear source-price relationships.
- Compare nonlinear or quantum-inspired findings against classical baselines.
- Generate candidate strategy hypotheses from discovered patterns.
- Map discovered hypotheses to existing strategy families where appropriate.
- Propose new strategy families where existing labels are insufficient.
- Reject weak, overfit, unsafe, or unrouteable strategies.
- Apply Akber's filter as a measurable quality gate.
- Run historical and forward shadow replays without creating orders.
- Route only eligible candidates to guarded PaperOps review.
- Attribute outcomes to sources, models, quantum review, Akber filter, risk,
  execution, and paper lifecycle.
- Produce concise public-safe decision records for the dashboard and Telegram.
- Preserve every inherited safety boundary.

If QSASE cannot explain why it is not trading, it is incomplete. If QSASE can
find patterns but cannot reject them, it is unsafe. If QSASE can backtest but
cannot preserve proof-ledger discipline, it is misleading. If QSASE can
recommend but bypasses PaperOps, it violates its constitution.

### 3.10 North Star Non-Goals

The north star excludes several tempting but wrong outcomes:

- It is not a live-capital unlock.
- It is not an automatic strategy-mutating agent.
- It is not a quantum trading bot.
- It is not an LLM stock picker.
- It is not a dashboard storytelling layer over unchanged logic.
- It is not a generic backtester detached from Qadam's source universe.
- It is not a forced-trade engine.
- It is not a way to turn historical backtests into paper proof ledger credit.

QSASE should make Qadam more capable and more disciplined at the same time.

### 3.11 North Star Summary

The QSASE north star is a Qadam that continuously asks:

```text
What strategies does my own architecture make me uniquely capable of
discovering, testing, explaining, and safely paper-reviewing?
```

The answer should come from the full data universe, full trading universe,
linear and nonlinear pattern recognition, Akber's filter, model challenge,
quantum review, shadow replay, PaperOps safety, and learning attribution.

That is the metamorphosis: Qadam becomes the system that discovers the
strategies Qadam was built to discover.

## 4. System Architecture Overview

QSASE sits above Qadam's intelligence and learning artifacts, beside the
strategy governance layer, and before PaperOps execution gates. It is not an
execution path. It is the strategy-discovery, strategy-routing, and
strategy-learning architecture that decides what deserves research, rejection,
shadow replay, or guarded paper review.

The architecture is deliberately layered:

```text
Full Data Universe
  -> Full Trading Universe
  -> QSASE Self-Model
  -> Universal Source-Price Pattern Matrix
  -> Full-Universe Pattern Search
  -> Linear Pattern Lab
  -> Nonlinear And Quantum Pattern Lab
  -> Strategy Foundry
  -> Akber Filter
  -> Shadow Strategy Simulator
  -> Strategy Router
  -> PaperOps Gate Interface
  -> Paper Proof Ledger And Learning Attribution
  -> Dashboard / Telegram Decision Records
```

The architecture must preserve one governing constraint:

```text
QSASE can decide what deserves review.
PaperOps decides whether paper execution is allowed.
```

### 4.1 Architecture Planes

QSASE extends Qadam's existing three-plane structure.

| Plane | Existing Qadam Role | QSASE Addition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Layer A - Intelligence Engine | Watches, triages, researches, models probabilities, and proposes signals. | Adds universal source-price discovery, historical pattern labs, nonlinear review inputs, and strategy hypothesis generation. |
| Layer B - Orchestration Layer | Applies risk, policy, kill-switches, paper execution, monitoring, exits, and postmortems. | Receives only eligible paper-review candidates through a controlled handoff and preserves all PaperOps gates. |
| Cockpit - Fund Manager Surface | Shows health, signals, evidence, trades, postmortems, settings, and governance state. | Shows QSASE decision records: trusted strategy, rejected strategy, blocker, missing evidence, quantum state, and next allowed action. |
| Learning Plane | Stores observations, outcomes, postmortems, shadow variants, and proposals. | Adds component attribution and recursive strategy improvement proposals without self-mutating production state. |

QSASE is primarily a Layer A and Learning Plane evolution. It touches Layer B
only through an explicit PaperOps handoff interface.

### 4.2 Core Subsystems

The QSASE architecture has twelve core subsystems.

| Subsystem | Purpose | Primary Output |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Self-Model | Reads Qadam's own source, model, quantum, route, risk, and learning state. | `qsase_self_model.json` |
| Universal Source-Price Pattern Matrix | Normalizes all source events against all watched markets and approved proxies. | `qsase_universal_source_price_matrix.json` |
| Historical Source-Price Memory | Records historical source and price windows, coverage, provenance, and replayability for backtesting. | `qsase_historical_source_price_memory.json` |
| Full-Universe Pattern Search | Scans all-source and all-market relationships before assigning strategy labels. | `qsase_full_universe_pattern_search.json` |
| Linear Pattern Lab | Runs classical event studies, lead-lag tests, regression, volatility, drawdown, hit-rate, and expectancy analysis. | `qsase_linear_pattern_lab.json` |
| Nonlinear And Quantum Pattern Lab | Tests multi-source interactions, ambiguity, regime similarity, and quantum or quantum-inspired strategy collapse. | `qsase_nonlinear_quantum_pattern_lab.json`, `qsase_quantum_pattern_reviews.jsonl` |
| Strategy Foundry | Converts validated patterns into strategy hypotheses or strategy-family updates. | `qsase_strategy_hypotheses.json` |
| Akber Filter Integration | Applies practical trading taste and makes that filter measurable across backtests and live candidates. | `qsase_akber_filter_integration.json` |
| Shadow Strategy Simulator | Replays actual, rejected, and counterfactual strategies without creating orders. | `qsase_shadow_strategy_simulator.json` |
| Strategy Router | Chooses watch, research, reject, shadow replay, foundry review, or guarded paper review. | `qsase_strategy_router_decisions.jsonl` |
| PaperOps Gate Interface | Hands off eligible candidates to existing guarded PaperOps without bypassing any gate. | `qsase_paperops_handoff_records.jsonl` |
| Learning And Attribution Ledger | Attributes outcomes to sources, models, quantum review, Akber filter, risk, execution, and lifecycle. | `qsase_component_attribution_ledger.jsonl` |

The subsystem list is intentionally artifact-first. If a decision is not
captured in an artifact, it is not a QSASE decision.

### 4.3 Data Flow

QSASE data flow begins with raw observations and ends with a decision record,
not a trade.

```text
1. Source observations and market prices enter Qadam.
2. QSASE Self-Model records the current health of Qadam's machinery.
3. The Universal Source-Price Pattern Matrix links events, source states,
   market states, prices, volume, volatility, odds, filings, and forward
   returns.
4. Full-Universe Pattern Search scans for source-price relationships without
   strategy-sleeve boundaries.
5. Linear Pattern Lab tests classical relationships and baseline evidence.
6. Nonlinear And Quantum Pattern Lab tests interaction effects and ambiguity.
7. Strategy Foundry turns surviving patterns into hypotheses.
8. Akber Filter checks practical trade quality.
9. Shadow Strategy Simulator tests historical and forward counterfactuals.
10. Strategy Router decides the next allowed action.
11. PaperOps Gate Interface passes only eligible paper-review candidates to
    existing PaperOps.
12. Learning And Attribution Ledger records what worked, what failed, and
    which components added or removed edge.
13. Dashboard and Telegram summarize the decision without adding authority.
```

This data flow keeps discovery wide and execution narrow.

### 4.4 Universal Search Before Strategy Labels

The architecture must make full-universe search a first-class subsystem, not a
side effect of existing strategy families.

The search space is:

```text
all eligible source events
x
all watched markets and approved tradable proxies
x
all relevant time windows
x
all allowed pattern methods
```

Existing families remain important, but they are downstream labels. QSASE must
not begin by asking "what is the oil strategy seeing?" or "what is the defence
strategy seeing?" It must first ask what the entire Qadam evidence field is
doing against the entire Qadam response surface.

This architectural choice is mandatory because the most valuable Qadam-native
strategies may be cross-sleeve:

- Prediction-market odds may lead defence proxies.
- Reddit attention may lead semiconductor volatility.
- Capitol Trades filings may lead defence or AI infrastructure baskets.
- Physical disruption may lead oil, inflation, silver, and prediction-market
  repricing.
- GitHub, patents, power infrastructure, and semiconductor sources may combine
  into AI-infrastructure strategies that do not fit cleanly inside one current
  sleeve.

QSASE must allow the data to produce the strategy shape.

### 4.5 Layer A Integration

QSASE consumes and extends Layer A outputs:

- Source Registry provides source identity, health, credential posture,
  latency, freshness, and trust context.
- Resource Registry provides methods, research references, and strategic
  frameworks.
- World-Model Corpus provides priors and question prompts, not proof.
- Local Gemma Research Analyst provides low-cost triage, extraction,
  clustering, and summarization.
- Frontier Gemini Strategy Lead provides adversarial review, synthesis, and
  failure-mode challenge.
- Quantum Engine provides nonlinear ambiguity review and strategy-collapse
  review where allowed.
- Pattern Recognition Engine provides current feature-engineering and
  oracle-preview inheritance.
- Edge Pattern Ledger provides existing candidate edge observations and
  persistence requirements.

QSASE should not replace these modules. It should orchestrate them into a
single strategy-discovery path.

### 4.6 Layer B Integration

QSASE touches Layer B only through controlled handoff.

The handoff boundary is:

```text
QSASE paper-review candidate
-> Signal Integrity
-> Strategy Lead review
-> Head of Quant review where required
-> Risk Agent sizing
-> Execution Policy
-> PaperOps submit gate
-> guarded Alpaca Paper route
```

QSASE cannot skip any step. The PaperOps Gate Interface must carry:

- Research Goal lineage.
- Candidate identity.
- Strategy hypothesis identity.
- Source quorum evidence.
- Market confirmation evidence.
- Akber filter result.
- Quantum review state where required.
- Backtest and shadow replay summary.
- Risk-relevant metadata.
- Distinct idempotency key requirement.
- Duplicate exposure check requirement.
- Paper-only route requirement.

The interface should be strict: if a required field is missing, the handoff is
blocked before PaperOps sees it as eligible.

### 4.7 Learning Integration

QSASE must connect backtests, shadow replay, real paper outcomes, and rejected
patterns into one learning loop.

The learning path is:

```text
strategy hypothesis
-> historical test
-> nonlinear review
-> Akber filter result
-> shadow replay
-> router decision
-> paper outcome or non-trade outcome
-> component attribution
-> proposal-only strategy update
```

The Learning And Attribution Ledger must separate:

- Historical evidence.
- Forward shadow evidence.
- Real guarded paper evidence.
- Model interpretation.
- Quantum review.
- Akber filter result.
- Risk and route state.
- Human or governance decision.

This separation prevents QSASE from treating a backtest, a shadow replay, and a
real paper lifecycle as the same kind of proof.

### 4.8 Dashboard And Telegram Integration

QSASE visibility must be decision-record based.

The cockpit should show:

- QSASE current state.
- Top trusted strategy.
- Top rejected strategy.
- New strategy hypotheses.
- Full-universe pattern scan state.
- Historical coverage gaps.
- Quantum review state.
- Akber filter state.
- PaperOps route readiness.
- Why-not-trading-now reason.
- Next allowed action.

Telegram should only receive compact notification summaries:

```text
QSASE: Defence remains top watch.
Reason: conflict + filings + market confirmation align.
Blocked: no fresh distinct PaperOps candidate.
Next: watch; no order sent.
```

Dashboard and Telegram cannot create commands, approvals, paper orders, broker
writes, proof credit, or live-capital authority.

### 4.9 Authority Boundaries By Subsystem

Every subsystem must expose authority flags. The defaults are false.

| Subsystem | Can Create Strategy Hypothesis | Can Hand Off To PaperOps Review | Can Submit Paper Order | Can Grant Proof Credit |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Self-Model | No | No | No | No |
| Universal Matrix | No | No | No | No |
| Pattern Labs | No | No | No | No |
| Strategy Foundry | Yes, proposal only | No | No | No |
| Akber Filter | No | No | No | No |
| Shadow Simulator | No | No | No | No |
| Strategy Router | No | Yes, only as candidate handoff | No | No |
| PaperOps Gate Interface | No | Yes, if complete | No | No |
| PaperOps | No | N/A | Yes, only through guarded paper route | Only real paper lifecycle evidence |
| Dashboard / Telegram | No | No | No | No |

This table is a design constraint. Any implementation that allows QSASE to
submit orders directly is architecturally invalid.

### 4.10 Failure And Degraded-State Architecture

QSASE must degrade closed.

Examples:

- If source coverage is insufficient, pattern discovery may run but strategy
  promotion is blocked.
- If historical coverage is incomplete, backtest confidence is discounted.
- If quantum review is required but unavailable, validated-edge promotion is
  blocked.
- If Akber filter context is incomplete, paper-review routing is held.
- If PaperOps route readiness is false, QSASE can only watch, research, reject,
  or shadow replay.
- If Telegram delivery fails, QSASE visibility is degraded but trading
  authority is unchanged.
- If artifacts are stale, QSASE must say so and avoid using them as fresh
  evidence.

Degraded state should create a decision record and, where appropriate, a
self-healing repair request. It should not create a trade.

### 4.11 Architecture Summary

QSASE architecture is not a replacement for Qadam. It is a unifying control
layer over the strategy intelligence Qadam already has.

The architecture can be summarized as:

```text
wide discovery
-> strict evidence
-> model and quantum challenge
-> human-style Akber quality filter
-> shadow proof before paper review
-> guarded PaperOps handoff
-> real paper proof ledger only after real guarded lifecycle
-> recursive learning without silent mutation
```

That is the architecture that lets Qadam discover strategies from its whole
data universe while preserving the narrow, safe paper-only execution route.

## 5. QSASE Self-Model

The QSASE Self-Model is Qadam's machine-readable mirror. It is the artifact
that tells QSASE what Qadam currently is before QSASE decides what strategy
deserves attention.

The self-model must answer:

```text
What is the current condition of Qadam's data, models, quantum review,
execution route, risk posture, learning memory, and visibility layer?
```

The self-model is not a dashboard summary. It is a routing dependency. If the
self-model is stale, incomplete, or degraded, QSASE must discount or block
strategy decisions that rely on the missing state.

### 5.1 Purpose

The self-model exists to prevent Qadam from making strategy decisions as if all
parts of the system were equally healthy.

Without a self-model, Qadam might overtrust a strategy because the narrative is
strong while:

- key sources are stale or credential-blocked
- quantum review is unavailable or only in fallback mode
- the paper route is not ready
- idempotency is holding a duplicate setup
- Akber filter inputs are missing
- historical coverage is too thin
- a learning artifact is stale
- Telegram or dashboard visibility is degraded

The self-model forces QSASE to reason from actual operating state, not ideal
architecture.

### 5.2 Output Artifact

The canonical artifact is:

```text
data/runtime/qsase_self_model.json
```

It should also append history and event records:

```text
data/runtime/qsase_self_model_history.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_self_model_events.jsonl
```

The current-state artifact should be compact enough for dashboard use and
complete enough for routing. It should include:

- `schema_version`
- `generated_at`
- `status`
- `staleness_status`
- `data_health`
- `trading_universe_health`
- `model_health`
- `quantum_health`
- `execution_health`
- `risk_health`
- `learning_health`
- `visibility_health`
- `strategy_readiness_summary`
- `why_not_trading_now`
- `degraded_reasons`
- `repair_requests`
- authority flags

The artifact is read-only from an execution perspective. It cannot create
signals, candidates, paper orders, broker writes, proof credit, or live-capital
authority.

### 5.3 Status Model

The self-model should use explicit states:

| Status | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `qsase_self_model_ready` | Required state is fresh enough for QSASE routing. |
| `qsase_self_model_degraded` | QSASE can continue research, but strategy promotion or paper-review handoff is limited. |
| `qsase_self_model_blocked` | Required state is missing or unsafe; QSASE cannot route strategies. |
| `qsase_self_model_stale` | Artifact freshness failed; QSASE must not treat it as current. |

Status must be derived from gates, not prose. A green-looking summary is not
enough if required underlying fields are missing.

### 5.4 Data Health

`data_health` describes what Qadam can currently see.

It should include:

- source universe count
- online source count
- degraded source count
- missing credential source count
- quarantined source count
- source freshness
- source latency
- source trust posture
- source quorum availability
- durable replay state
- raw archive availability
- full-universe scan readiness

The current runtime already exposes useful inputs, such as broad source scans
and source counts. QSASE must consolidate them into one self-model view.

Data health should drive routing:

- If source quorum is weak, strategy promotion is blocked.
- If a source is credential-blocked, its historical and live contribution is
  marked unavailable.
- If source coverage is partial, pattern confidence is discounted.
- If durable replay is incomplete, historical learning confidence is
  discounted.
- If a source is stale, it can inform historical research but not fresh
  paper-review routing.

### 5.5 Trading Universe Health

`trading_universe_health` describes what Qadam can currently test and express.

It should include:

- watched instrument count
- instrument families
- available market data
- market confirmation availability
- paper-tradable proxy availability
- prediction-market route readiness
- options or volatility context availability
- TradingView or technical context availability
- supplemental market data status
- non-paperable instrument list

This section must separate:

- instruments Qadam can observe
- instruments Qadam can backtest
- instruments Qadam can paper-trade
- instruments Qadam can only use as calibration or context

For example, prediction-market odds may be useful as explanatory data even if
the direct prediction-market paper route is not ready. QSASE must retain that
signal without misclassifying it as executable.

### 5.6 Model Health

`model_health` describes the current state and role of Qadam's model stack.

It should include:

- local Gemma availability
- local model role for triage, extraction, compression, and clustering
- frontier Gemini availability
- frontier model role for challenge, synthesis, and failure-mode review
- model latency
- model failure state
- model disagreement state
- hallucination or unsupported-claim risk
- latest model review freshness
- whether model output is advisory only

Model health should never produce approval. It should affect confidence and
routing:

- If local triage is unavailable, QSASE can still run deterministic scans but
  loses cheap compression.
- If frontier review is unavailable, strategy hypotheses can remain research
  candidates but cannot claim full Strategy Lead challenge.
- If models disagree, the disagreement becomes a decision-record field.
- If a model produces unsupported claims, QSASE must require source evidence
  before routing.

### 5.7 Quantum Health

`quantum_health` describes the current Head of Quant posture.

It should include:

- latest quantum backend
- latest simulation or fallback mode
- local simulator availability
- Q-CTRL readiness
- IBM Quantum readiness
- AWS Braket readiness if applicable
- quantum mandatory review gate status
- latest oracle job status
- latest oracle output route
- hardware submission allowed count
- hardware submitted count
- quantum provider call allowed count
- quantum execution authority flag
- quantum paper-order authority flag

The self-model must preserve the role distinction:

```text
Quantum can review ambiguity.
Quantum cannot approve execution.
```

Routing rules:

- If quantum review is required and missing, validated-edge promotion is
  blocked.
- If quantum is in classical fallback, the decision record must say so.
- If hardware access is unavailable, QSASE can still use allowed local or
  classical shadow review but must not imply hardware confirmation.
- If any quantum authority counter is nonzero where it should be zero, QSASE
  must block and raise a safety repair request.

### 5.8 Execution And PaperOps Health

`execution_health` describes whether a strategy can be safely handed to
PaperOps for paper review.

It should include:

- active automation status
- PaperOps readiness status
- guarded Alpaca Paper route readiness
- paper endpoint confirmation
- live endpoint disabled status
- idempotency ledger state
- duplicate submit state
- open order count
- open position count
- closed paper trade count
- eligible submit count
- fresh distinct setup count
- lifecycle poller state
- exit path state
- why-not-trading-now reason

The self-model should make idle states explicit. "Not trading" can be healthy
if:

- no fresh distinct candidate exists
- idempotency is correctly blocking a duplicate
- duplicate exposure would be created
- risk budget is not available
- source quorum is not sufficient
- PaperOps route is not ready
- daily drawdown or paper-account constraints block action

QSASE should treat disciplined non-action as a valid state, not a failure.

### 5.9 Risk Health

`risk_health` describes whether strategy routing can proceed toward paper
review.

It should include:

- paper account state
- paper buying power
- current exposure
- duplicate exposure risk
- drawdown state
- per-strategy risk budget
- per-run submit attempt cap
- open-order and open-position constraints
- kill-switch state
- Risk Agent availability
- Execution Policy availability

QSASE must not size orders by itself. It should only state whether the risk
context appears available for downstream PaperOps review.

If risk state is missing, stale, or breached, QSASE can still research and
shadow replay. It cannot route to paper review.

### 5.10 Learning Health

`learning_health` describes whether Qadam's memory is usable.

It should include:

- edge pattern ledger status
- pattern recognition engine status
- edge memory ledger status
- hypothesis lifecycle status
- strategy update record status
- strategy weight update status
- quantum meta review status
- shadow strategy replay status
- postmortem readiness
- paper proof ledger freshness
- unresolved blocker count
- strategy proposal count

Learning health must separate:

- observations
- hypotheses
- backtests
- shadow replays
- real paper outcomes
- approved learning entries
- proposal-only updates

If learning artifacts are stale or blocked, QSASE must not pretend recursive
improvement is active. It can still run discovery, but it must label learning
confidence as degraded.

### 5.11 Visibility Health

`visibility_health` describes whether humans can see the right state.

It should include:

- cockpit status freshness
- dashboard export freshness
- dashboard signature freshness
- QSASE dashboard module availability
- Telegram outbound status
- Telegram inbound read-only status
- latest notification failure category
- public-safe redaction status
- secret exposure checks
- generic/repetitive message checks

Visibility failure must not change trading authority. If Telegram fails,
PaperOps authority remains unchanged. If dashboard export is stale, QSASE must
surface visibility degradation and avoid implying current public visibility.

### 5.12 Strategy Readiness Summary

The self-model should summarize each strategy family and emergent strategy
hypothesis in a compact readiness table.

Each row should include:

- `strategy_key`
- `strategy_label`
- `strategy_type`
- `current_state`
- `source_readiness`
- `market_readiness`
- `model_readiness`
- `quantum_readiness`
- `akber_filter_readiness`
- `risk_readiness`
- `paperops_readiness`
- `learning_readiness`
- `next_allowed_action`
- `blocked_reasons`

The summary should support existing and emergent strategies:

- inherited strategy family
- proposed new strategy hypothesis
- rejected hypothesis
- merged strategy proposal
- split strategy proposal
- shadow-only strategy

This is the bridge between self-model and router. The router should not need to
rediscover basic health state; it should consume this summary.

### 5.13 Why-Not-Trading-Now

The self-model must always include a `why_not_trading_now` field.

This field should be plain enough for dashboard and Telegram summaries but
structured enough for automation:

```json
{
  "reason": "idempotency_guard_holding_duplicate_or_already_submitted_setup",
  "category": "healthy_idle",
  "blocking_layer": "paperops",
  "next_allowed_action": "watch",
  "repair_required": false
}
```

Common categories:

- `healthy_idle`
- `source_gap`
- `market_confirmation_gap`
- `akber_filter_gap`
- `quantum_review_gap`
- `risk_gap`
- `paperops_route_gap`
- `duplicate_or_idempotency_hold`
- `learning_artifact_gap`
- `visibility_gap`
- `safety_block`

The self-model should make the difference between a bug and correct discipline
obvious.

### 5.14 Repair Requests

The self-model should detect repair-worthy failures and record them without
mutating the system.

Repair requests should include:

- stale artifact refresh request
- missing source credential review
- broken historical coverage request
- failed check rerun request
- Telegram delivery repair request
- dashboard export repair request
- quantum readiness repair request
- PaperOps lifecycle mirror repair request
- strategy artifact schema repair request

Repair requests are not commands. They are structured work items for the
automation or a future self-healing runner to pick up through approved paths.

QSASE must not respond to a repair request by bypassing gates.

### 5.15 Authority Flags

The self-model must carry explicit false authority flags:

- `trade_candidate_creation_allowed`
- `risk_approval_allowed`
- `execution_allowed`
- `paper_order_allowed`
- `broker_write_allowed`
- `prediction_market_write_allowed`
- `telegram_command_path_enabled`
- `telegram_trade_command_enabled`
- `telegram_live_send_allowed`
- `quantum_job_authority`
- `quantum_hardware_submission_allowed`
- `quantum_provider_call_allowed`
- `strategy_mutation_allowed`
- `strategy_weight_update_applied`
- `live_capital_enabled`
- `proof_credit_allowed`

Validators must reject any self-model artifact that sets these flags to unsafe
values.

### 5.16 Build Contract

Implementation should add:

- `orchestrator/qsase_self_model.py`
- `scripts/check_qsase_self_model.py`
- `data/runtime/qsase_self_model.json`
- `data/runtime/qsase_self_model_history.jsonl`
- `data/runtime/qsase_self_model_events.jsonl`

The builder should read existing cockpit/runtime artifacts rather than
recomputing every subsystem from scratch. It should treat `build_cockpit_status`
and canonical runtime artifacts as source inputs, then emit a QSASE-specific
view.

The check script should validate:

- schema version
- required top-level fields
- freshness
- status derivation
- required nested health sections
- strategy readiness rows
- why-not-trading-now structure
- repair request structure
- authority flags
- public-safe output
- fail-closed probes

### 5.17 Self-Model Summary

The QSASE Self-Model is the mandatory first dependency for every later QSASE
module.

No universal pattern scan, Strategy Foundry proposal, router decision, or
PaperOps handoff should be trusted unless the self-model is fresh enough to
describe Qadam's current state.

The self-model gives Qadam the minimum operational self-awareness required to
ask a better strategy question:

```text
Given what I am right now, what should I study, reject, shadow-test, or route
to guarded paper review?
```

## 6. Universal Source-Price Pattern Matrix

The Universal Source-Price Pattern Matrix is the foundation of QSASE's
strategy discovery. It is the artifact that lets Qadam test the whole data
universe against the whole trading universe before assigning anything to a
strategy family.

The matrix exists because Qadam's edge is not supposed to come from one
indicator, one sleeve, or one source class. Its edge should come from broad
source-price relationships that are discovered across conflict, macro, market,
physical-world, prediction-market, social, filings, developer, and narrative
data.

The matrix must answer:

```text
When this source event or source-state change happened, what did every watched
market and approved proxy do before, during, and after the event?
```

### 6.1 Purpose

The matrix is the neutral research substrate beneath every QSASE pattern lab.

It exists to prevent Qadam from only finding what its current strategy sleeves
already expect. Before a pattern becomes "defence," "oil," "semiconductors,"
"silver," "prediction markets," or a new strategy family, it must first be a
source-price relationship in the universal matrix.

The matrix should support:

- source-to-asset relationships
- source-to-source relationships
- asset-to-asset relationships
- single-source-to-asset relationships
- multi-source-to-asset relationships
- source-cluster-to-asset relationships
- regime-to-strategy relationships
- strategy-family-neutral pattern discovery

The matrix is not a trade candidate ledger. It is research memory.

### 6.2 Output Artifacts

The primary artifacts are:

```text
data/runtime/qsase_universal_source_price_matrix.json
data/runtime/qsase_source_universe.json
data/runtime/qsase_trading_universe.json
data/runtime/qsase_source_price_edges.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_universal_source_price_matrix_events.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_universal_source_price_matrix_history.jsonl
```

`qsase_universal_source_price_matrix.json` should be the current public-safe
summary of the matrix. Source-market observations should be appended to
`qsase_source_price_edges.jsonl`.

`qsase_source_universe.json` and `qsase_trading_universe.json` should summarize
coverage quality:

- source coverage by source key
- market coverage by symbol or venue
- time-window coverage
- provider credential state
- historical pull readiness
- live sample availability
- missing or blocked sources
- missing or blocked markets
- row count
- event count
- relationship count
- stale coverage warnings

The matrix builder should be deterministic and replayable. If the same source
event, market, and window are processed again, the row identity should remain
stable.

### 6.3 Matrix Row Contract

Each matrix row should contain enough information for linear backtesting,
nonlinear pattern discovery, Strategy Foundry synthesis, and future dashboard
explanation.

Minimum row fields:

```json
{
  "schema_version": 1,
  "matrix_row_id": "qsase-matrix:<stable-hash>",
  "generated_at": "2026-06-28T00:00:00Z",
  "source_event_id": "acled:example",
  "source_key": "acled",
  "source_pipeline": "conflict",
  "source_event_timestamp": "2026-06-20T12:00:00Z",
  "source_event_type": "conflict_escalation",
  "source_trust_score": 0.82,
  "source_freshness_status": "fresh",
  "source_credential_status": "configured",
  "market_symbol": "ITA",
  "market_family": "defence",
  "market_observation_timestamp": "2026-06-20T16:00:00Z",
  "relationship_type": "source_to_asset",
  "time_window": "5d_forward",
  "price_before": 100.0,
  "price_after": 103.5,
  "forward_return": 0.035,
  "volatility_before": 0.18,
  "volatility_after": 0.23,
  "volume_context": "available",
  "market_confirmation_status": "available",
  "strategy_labels": [],
  "paper_route_available": true,
  "backtest_only": true,
  "proof_credit_allowed": false,
  "execution_allowed": false
}
```

The exact implementation can add fields, but it must preserve the distinction
between:

- source facts
- market facts
- relationship facts
- strategy labels
- route state
- authority flags

Strategy labels must be optional and downstream. A row should be useful even
before Qadam knows which strategy family it belongs to.

### 6.4 Source Universe Inputs

The matrix must treat every eligible Qadam source as a possible explanatory
variable.

Source classes include:

- conflict and geopolitical events
- macro and institutional time series
- market and broker-readout context
- prediction-market odds and event surfaces
- physical-world signals
- shipping, aviation, satellite, outage, and infrastructure feeds
- social and narrative sources
- Telegram inbound research intake
- Reddit and X signals where credential-ready
- SEC, filings, Capitol Trades / STOCK Act, and corporate disclosures
- GitHub, patents, developer activity, and AI-infrastructure indicators
- resource-derived methodology references when they inform feature design, not
  evidence
- private world-model priors when they generate questions, not proof

Every source must carry provenance:

- source key
- source pipeline
- adapter status
- credential state
- observed timestamp
- event timestamp
- trust score or trust posture
- freshness state
- raw payload reference where public-safe
- durable replay reference where available

QSASE must not treat a source as eligible merely because it exists in a list.
Eligibility depends on credential state, freshness, trust posture, provenance,
and whether the source is allowed to participate in research, signal review, or
only context.

### 6.5 Trading Universe Inputs

The matrix must treat every watched market and approved proxy as a possible
response variable.

Market classes include:

- oil and energy-security proxies
- silver and precious-metals stress proxies
- defence and aerospace proxies
- semiconductor and AI-infrastructure proxies
- prediction-market event prices and odds
- ETFs, single names, futures proxies, and other approved paper-tradable
  instruments
- supplemental market data used only for confirmation
- options, volatility, volume, or technical context where available
- future approved markets that pass governance and route review

Every market observation must carry:

- market symbol or event key
- market family
- venue or data provider
- observation timestamp
- price or odds value
- volume where available
- volatility context where available
- market-session status
- tradability state
- paper-route state
- live-route state, which must remain disabled unless separately approved

The matrix must separate observable markets from paper-tradable markets. A
market can be useful for pattern discovery even when it is not currently
paper-route ready.

### 6.6 Time Windows

Each source-market relationship should be evaluated across multiple windows.

Minimum windows:

- pre-event baseline
- event-time move
- 1-day forward
- 3-day forward
- 5-day forward
- 10-day forward
- 20-day forward
- 60-day forward

Additional windows may be added for slow-moving macro and filing effects:

- 90-day forward
- 180-day forward
- 1-year forward

Each window should record:

- price before
- price after
- forward return
- max favorable excursion
- max adverse excursion
- volatility before
- volatility after
- volume before
- volume after
- drawdown
- market regime
- data completeness score

Time-window logic must avoid lookahead leakage. A feature available only after
the event cannot be used as if it were known at event time.

### 6.7 Relationship Types

The matrix should support several relationship types.

| Relationship Type | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `source_to_asset` | A source event or state is compared with a market response. |
| `source_to_source` | One source class is compared with another source class. |
| `asset_to_asset` | One market response is compared with another market response. |
| `source_cluster_to_asset` | Multiple source events form a combined explanatory cluster. |
| `regime_to_asset` | A macro, liquidity, volatility, or geopolitical regime is compared with market behaviour. |
| `strategy_label_to_asset` | A downstream strategy label is evaluated after discovery, not before. |

This matters because QSASE should discover patterns such as:

- prediction odds moving before defence ETFs
- social attention moving before semiconductor volatility
- Capitol Trades filings preceding procurement-sensitive baskets
- physical disruption plus macro liquidity shifting oil and silver together
- GitHub or patent acceleration preceding AI-infrastructure repricing

No relationship type is executable. All are research relationships.

### 6.8 Matrix Generation Flow

The matrix builder should run in stages:

```text
1. Read QSASE Self-Model.
2. Read source registry, source health, historical backfill, durable replay,
   source observations, and relevant runtime artifacts.
3. Read watched markets, market confirmation, price history, odds history,
   volume, volatility, and route metadata.
4. Normalize source events into event records.
5. Normalize market observations into time-indexed market records.
6. Join every eligible source event to every eligible market across configured
   time windows.
7. Emit one stable matrix row per source-market-window relationship.
8. Write coverage summary and degraded-state reasons.
9. Emit Event Log records and public-safe health summary.
```

The builder should not call broker write endpoints, submit orders, or mutate
strategy state. Historical pulls remain credential-aware and provider-specific.

### 6.9 Historical Coverage Rules

Coverage must be explicit. QSASE should never present missing data as a weak
signal.

The matrix coverage fields in `qsase_source_universe.json` and
`qsase_trading_universe.json` should report:

- total eligible source count
- source count with historical coverage
- source count with live-only coverage
- source count blocked by missing credentials
- source count blocked by provider limits
- source count with stale data
- watched market count
- market count with price history
- market count with only current price
- market count with paper-route availability
- matrix row count
- minimum and maximum observation dates
- coverage by source class
- coverage by market family
- coverage by time window

Coverage should affect later confidence:

- Full coverage can support stronger backtest claims.
- Partial coverage can support research but not high-confidence promotion.
- Live-only coverage can support forward observation but not historical
  validation.
- Stale coverage can support historical analysis but not fresh paper-review
  routing.
- Credential-blocked coverage should generate repair requests or provider
  onboarding tasks.

### 6.10 Strategy Labels Are Downstream

The matrix must not start by compartmentalizing observations into current
strategy sleeves.

Allowed:

```text
ACLED event -> all watched markets -> observed relationship -> later mapped to
defence, oil, silver, prediction markets, semiconductors, or a new strategy.
```

Not allowed:

```text
ACLED event -> defence sleeve only -> ignore all other markets.
```

Strategy labels should be attached after one of these happens:

- Full-Universe Pattern Search finds a relationship.
- Linear Pattern Lab validates a classical relationship.
- Nonlinear And Quantum Pattern Lab identifies a multi-source interaction.
- Strategy Foundry proposes a strategy hypothesis.
- Strategy Router maps a pattern to a watch, reject, shadow, or paper-review
  path.

This rule keeps QSASE from becoming a confirmation-bias machine.

### 6.11 Data Quality And Leakage Rules

The matrix must defend against common research errors:

- no lookahead leakage
- no using revised data as if unrevised values were known
- no future news leakage into past decisions
- no stale source treated as fresh
- no single-source proof unless explicitly allowed for research-only context
- no Yahoo-only or supplemental-only source quorum
- no private worldview prior treated as evidence
- no Telegram inbound item treated as command authority
- no prediction-market odds treated as executable route unless route-ready
- no broker readout treated as strategy evidence beyond its proper role

Every row should carry enough metadata to audit whether the data was actually
available at the decision timestamp.

### 6.12 Authority Boundary

The matrix is research-only.

It must always carry false authority flags:

- `trade_candidate_creation_allowed`
- `risk_approval_allowed`
- `execution_allowed`
- `paper_order_allowed`
- `broker_write_allowed`
- `prediction_market_write_allowed`
- `telegram_command_path_enabled`
- `quantum_job_authority`
- `live_capital_enabled`
- `proof_credit_allowed`

The matrix can make later research better. It cannot make a trade happen.

### 6.13 Build Contract

Implementation should add:

- `orchestrator/qsase_universal_source_price_matrix.py`
- `scripts/check_qsase_universal_source_price_matrix.py`
- `data/runtime/qsase_universal_source_price_matrix.json`
- `data/runtime/qsase_source_universe.json`
- `data/runtime/qsase_trading_universe.json`
- `data/runtime/qsase_source_price_edges.jsonl`
- `data/runtime/qsase_universal_source_price_matrix_events.jsonl`
- `data/runtime/qsase_universal_source_price_matrix_history.jsonl`

The builder should reuse:

- `orchestrator/historical_backfill.py`
- source registry and source health artifacts
- durable replay/source observation artifacts
- market context artifacts
- edge tracker and edge pattern artifacts
- pattern recognition engine artifacts
- PaperOps route metadata for tradability labels only

The check script should validate:

- required artifact existence
- schema version
- stable row identity
- required row fields
- coverage summary fields
- no unsafe authority flags
- no proof credit
- no broker write authority
- no live-capital route
- no strategy-sleeve-only scan
- time-window validity
- public-safe output
- fail-closed behaviour when required source, market, or self-model state is
  missing

### 6.14 Matrix Summary

The Universal Source-Price Pattern Matrix is the first concrete place where
QSASE becomes different from ordinary strategy automation.

It gives Qadam a neutral research substrate:

```text
all sources
x
all watched markets
x
all relevant time windows
```

Only after that matrix exists should Qadam run full-universe pattern search,
linear tests, nonlinear and quantum review, Akber filtering, Strategy Foundry,
and routing.

## 7. Full-Universe Pattern Search

Full-Universe Pattern Search is the first discovery pass over the Universal
Source-Price Pattern Matrix. It asks what relationships appear to exist before
Qadam applies strategy-family labels, Akber's filter, quantum review, or
PaperOps route checks.

This layer is broad by design. It is not the final proof layer. It is the
scanner that turns the matrix into candidate relationships worthy of deeper
linear, nonlinear, quantum, shadow, and strategy-foundry review.

The search question is:

```text
Across all eligible sources, all watched markets, and all configured time
```
