```text
windows, which relationships look persistent, unusual, explanatory, or
potentially tradable enough to study further?
```

### 7.1 Purpose

The purpose of Full-Universe Pattern Search is to stop Qadam from seeing only
the patterns it already expects.

It should discover:

- obvious relationships, such as conflict escalation leading defence or oil
  proxies
- non-obvious relationships, such as prediction-market odds leading ETF
  repricing
- cross-sleeve relationships, such as macro liquidity affecting silver,
  semiconductors, and prediction markets together
- source-cluster relationships, such as Reddit attention plus options context
  plus policy news preceding volatility expansion
- strategy-invalidating relationships, such as patterns that look strong but
  fail outside one regime
- dead relationships, where the data shows that an intuitive strategy has no
  durable evidence

This layer should produce candidates and rejections. A good search system is
valuable not only because it finds possible edges, but because it kills weak
ones early.

### 7.2 Output Artifact

The primary artifact is:

```text
data/runtime/qsase_full_universe_pattern_search.json
```

The scan should also append:

```text
data/runtime/qsase_candidate_patterns.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_rejected_patterns.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_full_universe_pattern_search_history.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_full_universe_pattern_search_events.jsonl
```

The current artifact should include:

- `schema_version`
- `generated_at`
- `status`
- `self_model_status`
- `matrix_status`
- `matrix_row_count`
- `source_count`
- `market_count`
- `relationship_count`
- `candidate_pattern_count`
- `rejected_pattern_count`
- `strategy_label_count`
- `new_strategy_candidate_count`
- `scan_methods`
- `candidate_patterns`
- `rejected_patterns`
- `coverage_gaps`
- `degraded_reasons`
- authority flags

The scan is public-safe research output. It cannot create source quorum,
candidate trades, paper orders, broker writes, proof credit, or live-capital
authority.

### 7.3 Pattern Candidate Contract

Each candidate pattern should be structured enough to feed later modules.

Minimum candidate fields:

```json
{
  "pattern_id": "qsase-pattern:<stable-hash>",
  "generated_at": "2026-06-28T00:00:00Z",
  "pattern_state": "candidate_for_validation",
  "pattern_type": "source_cluster_to_asset",
  "source_keys": ["kalshi", "acled", "gdelt"],
  "source_pipelines": ["market", "conflict"],
  "market_symbols": ["ITA", "XAR", "LMT"],
  "market_families": ["defence"],
  "time_windows": ["1d_forward", "5d_forward", "20d_forward"],
  "relationship_summary": "Prediction odds and conflict escalation tend to precede defence proxy repricing.",
  "sample_size": 42,
  "persistence_score": 0.71,
  "effect_size_score": 0.64,
  "directional_consistency_score": 0.68,
  "drawdown_penalty": 0.18,
  "false_positive_risk": 0.31,
  "regime_sensitivity_score": 0.44,
  "source_quorum_score": 0.82,
  "novelty_score": 0.57,
  "paper_route_fit": "paper_proxy_available",
  "initial_strategy_labels": ["defence_repricing_geopolitical_watch"],
  "new_strategy_candidate": false,
  "next_required_review": ["linear_pattern_lab", "nonlinear_quantum_pattern_lab", "akber_filter"],
  "proof_credit_allowed": false,
  "execution_allowed": false
}
```

The scores are preliminary. They are not final edge validation. Later modules
must test, challenge, reject, or promote the pattern.

### 7.4 Rejected Pattern Contract

Rejected patterns matter. They stop Qadam from repeatedly chasing bad ideas.

Each rejected pattern should include:

- `pattern_id`
- `rejection_state`
- `source_keys`
- `market_symbols`
- `tested_windows`
- `sample_size`
- `rejection_reasons`
- `failed_metrics`
- `overfit_risk`
- `data_quality_issues`
- `next_allowed_action`

Common rejection reasons:

- insufficient sample size
- unstable direction
- low persistence
- high false-positive rate
- excessive drawdown
- regime-only effect
- source coverage too thin
- market history too thin
- source quorum impossible
- route unavailable
- duplicate of existing pattern
- future-data leakage risk
- strategy label too weak

Rejected patterns should be retained for learning. A rejected relationship can
become useful later if source coverage, market route, or regime context changes.

### 7.5 Search Methods

Full-Universe Pattern Search should use several first-pass methods. These are
broad discovery methods, not final statistical proof.

Allowed scan methods:

- frequency scan: how often a source event and market move appear together
- lead-lag scan: whether source events consistently precede market moves
- divergence scan: whether source pressure rises while market price lags
- co-movement scan: whether source clusters and market clusters move together
- volatility expansion scan: whether source events precede volatility increase
- drawdown scan: whether candidate patterns carry unacceptable downside
- regime split scan: whether the pattern works only in specific regimes
- novelty scan: whether the relationship is new relative to existing strategy
  families
- route-fit scan: whether the market expression is observable, backtestable,
  or paper-route ready
- source-quorum scan: whether the relationship has independent corroboration

These scans should produce candidate scores and rejection reasons. They should
not create final confidence or paper eligibility.

### 7.6 Ranking Model

The scan should rank candidate patterns with a transparent preliminary score.

Suggested score:

```text
pattern_scan_score =
  persistence_score
  + effect_size_score
  + directional_consistency_score
  + source_quorum_score
  + novelty_score
  + route_fit_score
  - drawdown_penalty
  - false_positive_risk
  - regime_fragility_penalty
  - data_quality_penalty
```

The score should be normalized to a 0-1 range and decomposed into its parts.
The decomposition matters more than the final number because later modules
need to know why the pattern was ranked.

Ranked states:

| State | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `candidate_for_linear_review` | Pattern has enough sample and structure for classical testing. |
| `candidate_for_nonlinear_review` | Pattern may depend on multi-source interactions or regime effects. |
| `candidate_for_strategy_foundry` | Pattern may justify a new or changed strategy hypothesis. |
| `watch_only` | Pattern is interesting but too thin for validation. |
| `rejected` | Pattern fails enough criteria that it should not move forward. |
| `blocked_by_coverage` | Pattern cannot be judged until source or market history improves. |

### 7.7 Strategy Label Handling

The scan may attach provisional labels, but it must not force labels.

Allowed label states:

- `unlabeled_pattern`
- `maps_to_existing_strategy`
- `maps_to_multiple_existing_strategies`
- `candidate_new_strategy`
- `candidate_strategy_merge`
- `candidate_strategy_split`
- `rejected_strategy_mapping`

Examples:

- Kalshi odds plus conflict escalation leading defence proxies may map to
  Defence Repricing Geopolitical Watch.
- Reddit AI-infrastructure attention plus semiconductor volatility may map to
  Semiconductor Policy Options Asymmetry.
- Capitol Trades plus procurement-sensitive baskets may map to defence, or it
  may justify a separate policy-flow strategy.
- Physical disruption plus macro liquidity plus oil and silver moves may
  suggest a cross-commodity stress strategy instead of one sleeve.

The scan must preserve the underlying relationship even if the label changes
later.

### 7.8 Multi-Source Pattern Discovery

The scan must test combinations, not only individual sources.

Examples of multi-source recipes:

- ACLED + GDELT + Kalshi odds -> defence and oil repricing
- Reddit + options context + AI policy news -> semiconductor volatility
- Capitol Trades + SEC filings + procurement news -> defence basket drift
- NASA FIRMS + AIS maritime + crude futures -> energy disruption proxy
- FRED liquidity + ECB/BIS stress + silver prices -> macro stress pattern
- GitHub activity + patents + semiconductor capex news -> AI infrastructure
  bottleneck pattern

The search should track whether multi-source combinations add incremental
signal over the best single source. If a three-source pattern does not improve
over one source, QSASE should prefer the simpler explanation.

### 7.9 Novelty And Redundancy

The scan should score novelty and redundancy.

Novelty asks:

- Is this relationship new relative to current strategy families?
- Does it connect source classes Qadam has not previously linked?
- Does it identify a tradable proxy Qadam has not emphasized?
- Does it expose a hidden timing gap?

Redundancy asks:

- Is this just another version of an existing pattern?
- Does it add anything beyond a current strategy label?
- Is it already captured by the edge pattern ledger?
- Does it create duplicate exposure if routed?

Novelty should not automatically promote a pattern. Redundancy should not
automatically reject a pattern. Both should affect the next review path.

### 7.10 Persistence And Regime Rules

Every candidate pattern should include persistence and regime context.

Persistence checks:

- number of observations
- number of distinct event days
- number of distinct market sessions
- number of time windows where direction holds
- consistency across source providers
- consistency across market instruments
- consistency across forward windows

Regime checks:

- liquidity regime
- volatility regime
- rates regime
- conflict regime
- risk-on / risk-off regime
- sector rotation regime
- prediction-market participation regime

QSASE should distinguish:

- a durable pattern
- a regime-specific pattern
- a one-off event
- an unproven fresh hypothesis
- an overfit historical artifact

Only durable or clearly regime-conditioned patterns should move toward
Strategy Foundry. One-off events may still be watched, but they should not be
treated as reusable strategy logic.

### 7.11 Route-Aware But Not Route-Limited

The scan should record route fit, but it should not discard useful research
just because the route is not ready.

Route states:

- `paper_proxy_available`
- `observable_not_paper_route_ready`
- `prediction_market_route_blocked`
- `supplemental_context_only`
- `not_approved_instrument`
- `route_unknown`

This allows QSASE to discover relationships such as Kalshi odds leading market
proxies even before direct prediction-market paper routing is ready.

Route state affects downstream action:

- paper-route ready patterns may proceed to deeper paper-review readiness
  checks after validation
- observable but non-route-ready patterns may remain research or calibration
- blocked route patterns may generate infrastructure repair or provider
  onboarding requests
- not-approved instruments remain outside execution and paper review

### 7.12 Search Output To Later Modules

The scan should explicitly route each candidate to the next module.

Next review options:

- `linear_pattern_lab`
- `nonlinear_quantum_pattern_lab`
- `strategy_foundry`
- `akber_filter`
- `shadow_strategy_simulator`
- `strategy_router`
- `coverage_repair`
- `reject_and_store`

Typical routing:

- strong sample, clear direction -> Linear Pattern Lab
- weak individual sources but strong combinations -> Nonlinear And Quantum
  Pattern Lab
- new relationship outside current families -> Strategy Foundry
- practical trade quality uncertain -> Akber Filter
- pattern historically interesting but current route uncertain -> Shadow
  Strategy Simulator
- coverage missing -> Coverage Repair
- failed metrics -> Reject And Store

No candidate should jump directly from full-universe scan to PaperOps.

### 7.13 Degraded-State Behaviour

The scan must degrade closed.

Examples:

- If `qsase_self_model.json` is stale, the scan is blocked.
- If the universal matrix is missing, the scan is blocked.
- If historical coverage is partial, the scan can run with degraded confidence.
- If source provenance is missing, affected relationships are rejected or held.
- If market data is unavailable, affected market relationships are excluded.
- If time-window data is incomplete, the candidate is blocked by coverage.
- If unsafe authority flags appear, the scan fails validation.

Degraded scans can still be useful, but they must label themselves as
degraded. A degraded scan cannot promote patterns toward paper-review routing.

### 7.14 Authority Boundary

Full-Universe Pattern Search is research-only.

It must always keep these flags false:

- `source_quorum_credit_allowed`
- `trade_candidate_creation_allowed`
- `risk_approval_allowed`
- `execution_allowed`
- `paper_order_allowed`
- `broker_write_allowed`
- `prediction_market_write_allowed`
- `telegram_command_path_enabled`
- `telegram_trade_command_enabled`
- `quantum_job_authority`
- `live_capital_enabled`
- `proof_credit_allowed`

It can say:

```text
This pattern deserves deeper review.
```

It cannot say:

```text
This pattern is a trade.
```

### 7.15 Build Contract

Implementation should add:

- `orchestrator/qsase_full_universe_pattern_search.py`
- `scripts/check_qsase_full_universe_pattern_search.py`
- `data/runtime/qsase_full_universe_pattern_search.json`
- `data/runtime/qsase_candidate_patterns.jsonl`
- `data/runtime/qsase_rejected_patterns.jsonl`
- `data/runtime/qsase_full_universe_pattern_search_history.jsonl`
- `data/runtime/qsase_full_universe_pattern_search_events.jsonl`

The builder should read:

- `data/runtime/qsase_self_model.json`
- `data/runtime/qsase_universal_source_price_matrix.json`
- `data/runtime/qsase_source_price_edges.jsonl`
- `data/runtime/qsase_historical_coverage_map.json`
- existing edge tracker and edge pattern ledger artifacts
- pattern recognition engine artifact for current feature inheritance
- strategy family definitions for provisional labels only

The check script should validate:

- required fields
- candidate pattern schema
- rejected pattern schema
- scan method list
- score decomposition
- no strategy-sleeve-only scan
- no candidate with unsafe authority
- no paper-order permission
- no proof credit
- no direct PaperOps eligibility
- degraded-state handling
- public-safe output
- stable pattern ids

### 7.16 Search Summary

Full-Universe Pattern Search is the layer where QSASE first becomes curious.

It looks across:

```text
all sources
all markets
all windows
all first-pass relationship types
```

It returns:

```text
candidate patterns
rejected patterns
coverage gaps
next review paths
```

That is enough to feed the Linear Pattern Lab, Nonlinear And Quantum Pattern
Lab, Strategy Foundry, Akber Filter, Shadow Strategy Simulator, and Strategy
Router without prematurely turning research into trading authority.

## 8. Historical Source-Price Memory

Historical Source-Price Memory is the time-aware memory layer that sits above
the Universal Source-Price Pattern Matrix.

The matrix answers:

```text
What source event, market state, and forward outcome belong together?
```

Historical Source-Price Memory answers:

```text
What did Qadam know at that exact point in time, what was only knowable later,
and what would a strategy have been allowed to decide without lookahead?
```

This distinction matters because QSASE must learn from the past without lying
to itself. Historical data can be backfilled for research, but the 30-day paper
growth trial cannot be backfilled, accelerated, or simulated. Backtests can
support strategy discovery. They cannot create paper proof ledger credit.

### 8.1 Purpose

The purpose of Historical Source-Price Memory is to give QSASE a durable,
replayable, point-in-time archive of source events, market states, derived
features, and later outcomes.

It exists so Qadam can ask:

```text
If I had been running then, with only the sources available then, what would I
have seen, what would I have inferred, what would I have ignored, and what
would have happened next?
```

This layer is not only a data warehouse. It is a memory discipline.

It must preserve:

- The source event as originally observed.
- The market state as tradable at the decision timestamp.
- The feature set that was actually available at that timestamp.
- The outcome window that became knowable only later.
- The exact difference between historical research, forward shadow replay, and
  real paper-order evidence.

Without this layer, QSASE risks optimizing against hindsight instead of
building strategies that would have survived real-time operation.

### 8.2 Core Principle

Every historical memory record must separate four things:

```text
event time
source availability time
decision time
outcome availability time
```

These times are not interchangeable.

An ACLED event date, a news publication timestamp, a Reddit post timestamp, a
Kalshi odds snapshot, a Capitol Trades filing timestamp, and an Alpaca market
bar timestamp all have different availability rules. A strategy may only use
the data that was available before or at its decision timestamp.

Forward returns, later volatility, later drawdown, later options movement, and
later cross-asset reaction are labels for learning. They are not permitted
features for the historical decision.

### 8.3 Output Artifacts

QSASE should implement Historical Source-Price Memory as a small family of
runtime and durable artifacts:

```text
data/runtime/qsase_historical_source_price_memory.json
data/runtime/qsase_historical_source_price_memory.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_historical_source_price_memory_history.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_historical_source_price_memory_events.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_point_in_time_replay_index.json
```

The summary artifact should answer:

```text
What historical memory is available?
What sources are covered?
What markets are covered?
What windows are replayable?
What parts are leakage-safe?
What gaps remain?
What can be used for research only?
What can be used for shadow replay?
What can never be counted as paper proof ledger evidence?
```

The JSONL artifacts should preserve append-only record history so later
strategy evaluation can reference the exact historical memory snapshot that
created a result.

### 8.4 Memory Record Contract

Each memory record should have a schema like:

```json
{
  "memory_record_id": "qsase-memory-2024-03-12-acled-oil-xle-001",
  "source_event_id": "acled-2024-03-12-example",
  "matrix_row_ids": [
    "matrix-acled-xle-2024-03-12-1d",
    "matrix-acled-xle-2024-03-12-5d"
  ],
  "as_of_timestamp": "2024-03-12T14:30:00Z",
  "event_timestamp": "2024-03-12T08:15:00Z",
  "source_available_at": "2024-03-12T09:05:00Z",
  "market_available_at": "2024-03-12T14:30:00Z",
  "decision_timestamp": "2024-03-12T14:31:00Z",
  "outcome_available_at": "2024-04-11T20:00:00Z",
  "source_snapshot": {
    "source_name": "acled",
    "source_type": "conflict_event",
    "event_type": "regional_escalation",
    "region": "middle_east",
    "trust_score": 0.82,
    "freshness_seconds": 3300,
    "payload_ref": "data/historical/acled/2024-03-12.jsonl"
  },
  "market_snapshot": {
    "instrument": "XLE",
    "asset_class": "equity_etf",
    "price": 91.42,
    "volume": 1842200,
    "volatility_state": "expanding",
    "market_session": "regular",
    "tradable": true,
    "provider": "alpaca_paper_route_context"
  },
  "feature_availability": {
    "source_features_available": true,
    "market_features_available": true,
    "macro_features_available": false,
    "options_features_available": false,
    "prediction_market_features_available": false,
    "forbidden_future_features_detected": false
  },
  "features": {
    "source_intensity_score": 0.71,
    "cross_source_confirmation_score": 0.44,
    "market_reaction_lag_score": 0.62,
    "regime_similarity_score": 0.58
  },
  "forward_outcomes": {
    "return_1d": 0.0062,
    "return_3d": 0.0141,
    "return_5d": 0.0198,
    "return_10d": -0.0045,
    "return_20d": 0.0314,
    "return_60d": 0.0521,
    "max_drawdown_20d": -0.0186,
    "volatility_expansion_5d": 0.23
  },
  "strategy_labels": [
    "geopolitical_event_reaction",
    "macro_catalyst_momentum"
  ],
  "backtest_eligible": true,
  "shadow_replay_eligible": true,
  "paper_proof_ledger_eligible": false,
  "lookahead_safe": true,
  "execution_allowed": false,
  "proof_credit_allowed": false
}
```

The important fields are not the example values. The important fields are the
boundaries.

A memory record may be used for:

- Historical pattern search.
- Linear and nonlinear feature testing.
- Strategy hypothesis generation.
- Counterfactual replay.
- Shadow evaluation.
- Future routing calibration.

A memory record must not be used for:

- Creating paper proof ledger credit.
- Pretending the 30-day paper growth trial already elapsed.
- Submitting paper orders.
- Bypassing source quorum, risk budget, Q-CTRL review, or the guarded Alpaca
  Paper route.
- Creating live-capital authority.

### 8.5 Source Snapshot Rules

Every source snapshot must preserve provenance, availability, and confidence.

For each source event, QSASE should record:

- Source name.
- Source family.
- Raw payload reference.
- Normalized payload reference.
- Event timestamp.
- Ingestion timestamp.
- Availability timestamp.
- Provider status.
- Credential status.
- Source trust score.
- Source freshness score.
- Source revision status.
- Whether the source was known before the decision timestamp.

Source families include:

- Market data.
- Broker paper-account context.
- News.
- Geopolitical events.
- Macro data.
- Weather and disaster feeds.
- SEC filings.
- Prediction markets.
- Social attention.
- Reddit.
- Capitol Trades.
- Kalshi.
- Local LLM observations.
- Frontier LLM reviews.
- Quantum review outputs.
- PaperOps state.

The source snapshot is not a strategy explanation. It is the evidence record
from which strategy explanations can later be derived.

### 8.6 Market Snapshot Rules

Every market snapshot must preserve the tradable state as of the decision
timestamp.

For each market or instrument, QSASE should record:

- Symbol or market identifier.
- Asset class.
- Provider.
- Session state.
- Last price.
- Open, high, low, close, and volume where available.
- Spread or liquidity proxy where available.
- Volatility context.
- Technical context.
- Options context where available.
- Prediction-market odds where relevant.
- Borrow or shortability context where relevant.
- Tradability under the paper route.
- Exposure conflicts at that point in time.
- Risk-budget state at that point in time.

The market snapshot must not silently use a later bar, later close, later
revision, or later execution state. If the 20-day return is being evaluated,
that return belongs in the outcome field, not the decision field.

### 8.7 Required Outcome Windows

Historical Source-Price Memory should standardize forward outcome windows so
different strategy candidates can be compared cleanly.

Minimum outcome windows:

- Event time.
- Same-session close.
- 1-day.
- 3-day.
- 5-day.
- 10-day.
- 20-day.
- 60-day.

Optional outcome windows:

- 30-minute.
- 1-hour.
- 4-hour.
- Overnight.
- 2-day.
- 30-day.
- 90-day.
- Until catalyst resolution.
- Until invalidation trigger.
- Until risk stop.

For each window, the memory layer should preserve:

- Directional return.
- Maximum favorable excursion.
- Maximum adverse excursion.
- Volatility change.
- Volume change.
- Liquidity change.
- Benchmark-relative return.
- Sector-relative return where relevant.
- Cross-asset confirmation where relevant.

This makes later strategy evaluation more precise. A setup may look good on a
20-day horizon and bad on a 1-day horizon. QSASE should not flatten those into
one generic score.

### 8.8 Backfilled, Forward Shadow, And Paper Evidence

QSASE must maintain three evidence classes.

Historical backfill evidence:

```text
Useful for research, pattern discovery, and hypothesis generation.
Not proof of live or paper operational skill.
Cannot create paper proof ledger credit.
```

Forward shadow evidence:

```text
Useful for checking whether a strategy would have acted correctly after it was
defined.
Still not broker-executed evidence.
Cannot create paper proof ledger credit.
```

Real paper evidence:

```text
Created only when a fresh eligible setup passes the guarded route, is submitted
to Alpaca paper, and is tracked through the paper lifecycle.
Can contribute to the paper proof ledger if all proof rules pass.
```

This distinction is constitutional. Historical memory lets Qadam become
smarter. It does not let Qadam claim operational proof it did not earn on the
actual calendar.

### 8.9 Replay Modes

Historical Source-Price Memory should support several replay modes.

`historical_backtest_replay`:

```text
Replays old events and old prices to test whether a strategy rule would have
produced positive expectancy. Research-only.
```

`point_in_time_replay`:

```text
Replays only data that would have been available at the decision timestamp.
Used for leakage-safe evaluation.
```

`forward_shadow_replay`:

```text
Runs a strategy from a fixed definition date forward without broker submission.
Used to evaluate live-like behavior before paper execution.
```

`counterfactual_strategy_replay`:

```text
Tests how newly discovered strategy logic would have behaved historically.
Useful for rejection, calibration, and sample-size analysis.
```

`paper_lifecycle_replay`:

```text
Reconstructs real paper orders, fills, open positions, closes, and PnL from
actual paper lifecycle artifacts. This can support paper proof ledger review
only when it refers to real paper events, not simulated fills.
```

Each replay mode must write its mode into the result artifact. A dashboard,
Telegram note, or later strategy selector must never blur these categories.

### 8.10 Feature Store Boundary

Historical Source-Price Memory should include a feature store boundary, but it
should not become an execution engine.

Allowed feature types:

- Source intensity.
- Source freshness.
- Source trust.
- Source rarity.
- Source disagreement.
- Cross-source confirmation.
- Event clustering.
- Market reaction lag.
- Volatility compression and expansion.
- Liquidity shift.
- Trend state.
- Mean-reversion pressure.
- Prediction-market odds shift.
- Options skew and implied-volatility shift where available.
- Macro regime.
- Sector regime.
- Risk budget state.
- Route readiness state.
- Quantum ambiguity score.

Every feature must include:

- Feature name.
- Feature version.
- Input source references.
- Window.
- Availability timestamp.
- Decision timestamp.
- Missing-data behavior.
- Leakage check status.

The feature store exists to make later labs reproducible. It must not create
trade candidates by itself.

### 8.11 Memory Indices

QSASE should build indices over the memory so later modules can search quickly
without re-normalizing every artifact.

Required indices:

- By source family.
- By source name.
- By instrument.
- By asset class.
- By event type.
- By region.
- By sector.
- By time window.
- By outcome direction.
- By volatility regime.
- By liquidity regime.
- By strategy label.
- By candidate pattern id.
- By rejected pattern id.
- By route readiness state.
- By source-quorum state.
- By paper-route state.

These indices are not just performance aids. They are also interpretability
aids. They let Qadam explain whether a candidate edge was found across the
whole universe or only inside one narrow compartment.

### 8.12 Coverage And Quality Checks

Historical memory must report its coverage honestly.

Coverage checks should include:

- Eligible source count.
- Covered source count.
- Missing source count.
- Eligible instrument count.
- Covered instrument count.
- Missing instrument count.
- Matrix row count.
- Memory record count.
- Point-in-time safe record count.
- Leakage-rejected record count.
- Window-complete record count.
- Window-incomplete record count.
- Revision-aware macro record count.
- Credential-blocked source count.
- Provider-stale source count.
- Public-safe summary status.

Quality checks should include:

- No future feature timestamps before decision time.
- No outcome values inside decision features.
- No revised macro values without vintage metadata.
- No broker execution assumptions inside historical backtests.
- No simulated fills counted as paper proof.
- No live-capital route.
- No command authority from Telegram inbound intake.

The memory artifact should degrade loudly when these checks fail. A quiet
historical memory failure is dangerous because it can contaminate every
downstream pattern score.

### 8.13 Relationship To Existing Qadam Modules

Historical Source-Price Memory should not duplicate existing modules. It should
index them and make them replayable.

It should consume:

- Historical backfill plans and samples.
- Universal Source-Price Pattern Matrix rows.
- Source intelligence observations.
- Market context snapshots.
- Pattern recognition outputs.
- Edge memory ledger entries.
- Strategy weight updates.
- Shadow strategy results.
- PaperOps cycle summaries.
- Paper proof ledger references.

It should produce:

- Point-in-time replay records.
- Leakage-safe feature records.
- Forward outcome labels.
- Memory coverage summaries.
- Replay indices.
- Evidence-class labels.
- Research-ready training sets for the Linear Pattern Recognition Lab.
- Research-ready training sets for the Nonlinear And Quantum Pattern Lab.

The relationship is:

```text
historical backfill and runtime observations
  -> universal source-price matrix
  -> historical source-price memory
  -> linear and nonlinear pattern labs
  -> strategy foundry
  -> Akber filter
  -> shadow replay
  -> guarded paper route only if all later gates pass
```

### 8.14 Safety And Authority Boundary

Historical Source-Price Memory is research infrastructure.

It must not:

- Create Telegram commands.
- Create trade candidates by itself.
- Create risk approvals.
- Create execution approvals.
- Submit paper orders.
- Write to broker endpoints.
- Bypass Q-CTRL review.
- Bypass the guarded Alpaca Paper route.
- Enable live capital.
- Grant proof credit.
- Backfill the 30-day paper growth trial.
- Simulate elapsed time.

It may:

- Improve strategy discovery.
- Improve feature engineering.
- Improve replay quality.
- Improve rejection discipline.
- Improve explanation quality.
- Improve dashboard clarity.
- Improve future PaperOps setup selection after later modules approve the
  strategy path.

### 8.15 Dashboard Representation

The dashboard should represent this layer as memory state, not as trading
performance.

Recommended fields:

- Historical memory status.
- Covered source families.
- Covered trading universe.
- Point-in-time safe records.
- Leakage rejected records.
- Backtest replay windows.
- Forward shadow windows.
- Paper proof ledger eligibility boundary.
- Latest memory refresh.
- Current blockers.

The language should stay explicit:

```text
Historical memory informs research. It is not paper proof ledger credit.
```

This prevents users from confusing a strong backtest with a real paper-trial
result.

### 8.16 Build Contract

Implementation should add a focused module:

```text
orchestrator/qsase_historical_source_price_memory.py
```

The module should expose:

```text
build_historical_source_price_memory(settings)
load_historical_source_price_memory(settings)
validate_historical_source_price_memory(memory)
write_historical_source_price_memory(memory, settings)
build_point_in_time_replay_index(memory)
```

It should add a check script:

```text
scripts/check_qsase_historical_source_price_memory.py
```

The check should validate:

- Required artifacts exist.
- Records have event, availability, decision, and outcome timestamps.
- Features do not use future data.
- Outcomes are not included as decision features.
- Evidence class is explicit.
- Backtest records cannot produce paper proof ledger credit.
- Replay mode is explicit.
- Authority flags are false for execution and live capital.
- Public-safe dashboard summary can be generated.

The first implementation does not need to backfill every source perfectly. It
does need to create the right memory contract so future source additions do not
force another redesign.

### 8.17 Historical Memory Summary

Historical Source-Price Memory is the layer that lets QSASE learn from history
without corrupting itself with hindsight.

It turns the Universal Source-Price Pattern Matrix into a replayable,
point-in-time archive. It gives the Linear Pattern Recognition Lab and
Nonlinear And Quantum Pattern Lab clean training material. It preserves the
difference between backfilled research, forward shadow replay, and real paper
evidence. It makes Qadam more intelligent without allowing it to fake proof,
skip the actual paper calendar, or confuse historical discovery with trading
authority.

## 9. Linear Pattern Recognition Lab

The Linear Pattern Recognition Lab is QSASE's classical statistics layer.

It consumes Historical Source-Price Memory and asks:

```text
Which source-price relationships are visible using transparent, reproducible,
linear methods before Qadam asks nonlinear models or quantum review to
interpret the harder interactions?
```

This lab is deliberately not glamorous. Its job is to build the classical
baseline that every more complex claim must beat.

If a nonlinear model or quantum review later says a pattern is interesting,
QSASE needs to know whether the same pattern was already visible through simple
event studies, correlations, regressions, lead-lag analysis, hit-rate testing,
or factor-controlled returns. Otherwise Qadam cannot tell whether the advanced
layer created real insight or merely renamed a basic relationship.

### 9.1 Purpose

The purpose of the Linear Pattern Recognition Lab is to test full-universe
source-price relationships using methods that are:

- Point-in-time safe.
- Reproducible.
- Explainable.
- Comparable across sources and instruments.
- Strong enough to reject weak patterns.
- Simple enough to become the benchmark for nonlinear review.

It should answer:

```text
Which sources historically led which markets?
Which market reactions were immediate, delayed, or absent?
Which relationships survived transaction-cost and drawdown checks?
Which patterns were just broad market beta?
Which source combinations improved expectancy?
Which candidate edges deserve nonlinear review?
Which candidate edges should be rejected before consuming more compute?
```

The lab must run across the entire available data universe and trading
universe, not only the current named strategy families.

### 9.2 Inputs

The lab should consume:

- Historical Source-Price Memory records.
- Universal Source-Price Pattern Matrix rows.
- Full-Universe Pattern Search candidate patterns.
- Full-Universe Pattern Search rejected patterns for regression testing.
- Source health and coverage metadata.
- Trading universe metadata.
- Market snapshots.
- Forward outcome windows.
- Existing edge memory ledger entries.
- Existing edge pattern ledger entries.
- Shadow strategy runner outcomes where available.
- Paper proof ledger references for later comparison only.

The lab should not require all possible sources to be perfect before it starts.
It should start with coverage-aware testing and mark confidence accordingly.

Incomplete coverage should produce a discounted result, not a false pass.

### 9.3 Output Artifacts

QSASE should create:

```text
data/runtime/qsase_linear_pattern_lab.json
data/runtime/qsase_linear_backtest_results.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_linear_rejected_patterns.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_linear_pattern_lab_history.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_linear_pattern_lab_events.jsonl
```

The summary artifact should expose:

```text
status
generated_at
source_count
instrument_count
tested_relationship_count
accepted_linear_pattern_count
rejected_linear_pattern_count
inconclusive_linear_pattern_count
leakage_rejected_count
coverage_blocked_count
candidate_for_nonlinear_review_count
candidate_for_strategy_foundry_count
execution_allowed
proof_credit_allowed
live_capital_enabled
```

`execution_allowed`, `proof_credit_allowed`, and `live_capital_enabled` must be
false. The Linear Pattern Recognition Lab is research infrastructure, not a
trading route.

### 9.4 Linear Pattern Result Contract

Each tested relationship should produce a result record like:

```json
{
  "linear_pattern_id": "linear-acled-xle-5d-20240601",
  "source_recipe": {
    "source_family": "geopolitical_events",
    "source_names": ["acled"],
    "event_type": "regional_escalation",
    "region": "middle_east",
    "feature_names": [
      "source_intensity_score",
      "cross_source_confirmation_score"
    ]
  },
  "market_expression": {
    "instrument": "XLE",
    "asset_class": "equity_etf",
    "horizon": "5d",
    "direction": "long"
  },
  "sample": {
    "memory_record_count": 184,
    "train_record_count": 128,
    "validation_record_count": 56,
    "start_date": "2021-01-04",
    "end_date": "2024-06-01",
    "point_in_time_safe": true,
    "coverage_score": 0.82
  },
  "tests": {
    "event_study": {
      "mean_forward_return": 0.0124,
      "median_forward_return": 0.0069,
      "hit_rate": 0.57,
      "max_drawdown_median": -0.018
    },
    "lead_lag": {
      "best_lag": "1d",
      "lag_score": 0.61
    },
    "correlation": {
      "pearson": 0.18,
      "spearman": 0.23
    },
    "regression": {
      "coefficient": 0.0091,
      "t_stat": 2.34,
      "p_value": 0.019,
      "r_squared": 0.07
    },
    "factor_control": {
      "benchmark": "SPY",
      "sector_neutral_alpha": 0.0062,
      "beta_adjusted": true
    }
  },
  "risk": {
    "expectancy": 0.0048,
    "win_loss_ratio": 1.21,
    "max_adverse_excursion_median": -0.014,
    "tail_loss_95": -0.039,
    "liquidity_penalty": 0.12
  },
  "decision": {
    "linear_status": "candidate_for_nonlinear_review",
    "reason": "positive expectancy with modest factor-adjusted alpha and acceptable drawdown",
    "reject_reason": null,
    "candidate_for_strategy_foundry": true,
    "candidate_for_akber_filter": true,
    "candidate_for_paper_route": false
  },
  "authority": {
    "backtest_only": true,
    "execution_allowed": false,
    "proof_credit_allowed": false,
    "live_capital_enabled": false
  }
}
```

The result schema should be stable enough that later modules can compare old
and new test runs without changing interpretation.

### 9.5 Test Families

The first implementation should support a compact but serious statistical
toolkit.

Event studies:

```text
Measure market behavior before, during, and after source events.
```

Lead-lag tests:

```text
Measure whether source movement consistently precedes price movement, and at
which horizon.
```

Correlation tests:

```text
Measure linear association between source features and market outcomes.
```

Rank correlation tests:

```text
Measure monotonic relationships that may not be cleanly linear in raw values.
```

Regression tests:

```text
Estimate whether source features explain forward returns, volatility changes,
drawdown, or volume changes.
```

Factor-controlled tests:

```text
Separate true source-specific edge from broad market, sector, rate, dollar,
oil, volatility, or liquidity exposure.
```

Hit-rate and expectancy tests:

```text
Measure whether the setup would have produced positive average outcome after
losses, costs, and invalidations.
```

Drawdown and adverse excursion tests:

```text
Measure whether the pattern is operationally tolerable, not just directionally
right.
```

Regime diagnostics:

```text
Measure whether the pattern survives across volatility, liquidity, macro,
geopolitical, market-session, and risk-budget regimes.
```

Multiple-testing controls:

```text
Discount results when Qadam scanned many source-market combinations and a
relationship may be a false discovery.
```

### 9.6 Full-Universe Testing

The lab must preserve the QSASE rule:

```text
Search the whole data universe against the whole trading universe before
settling into named strategy families.
```

It should test relationships such as:

- Geopolitical event intensity against defence, energy, shipping, commodity,
  volatility, and regional ETF proxies.
- Kalshi odds changes against oil, rates, election-sensitive baskets, sector
  ETFs, and volatility instruments.
- Reddit attention against single-name equities, semiconductor proxies, meme
  liquidity, short-interest proxies, and volatility expansion.
- Capitol Trades disclosures against single names, sectors, factor baskets, and
  delayed drift windows.
- SEC filings against single-name drift, gap risk, liquidity, and volatility.
- Macro surprises against rates, dollar, gold, equity indices, sector rotation,
  and volatility.
- Weather and disaster feeds against energy, agriculture, insurance, shipping,
  utilities, and regional exposures.
- News sentiment against sector-relative returns and volatility changes.
- Prediction-market odds against correlated asset prices and later event
  resolution outcomes.
- PaperOps and route-readiness state against missed opportunities, idempotency
  holds, and operational latency.

The examples are not hardcoded strategy families. They are search spaces.

If a relationship is found outside the existing strategy families, the lab
should preserve it as a candidate for Strategy Foundry instead of forcing it
into the nearest existing label.

### 9.7 Sample Eligibility

Each test must state whether the sample is large enough and clean enough to be
interpretable.

Eligibility checks should include:

- Minimum memory record count.
- Minimum unique event count.
- Minimum instrument coverage.
- Minimum time-window coverage.
- Maximum missing-feature ratio.
- Maximum stale-source ratio.
- Maximum leakage-rejected ratio.
- Minimum point-in-time safe record count.
- Minimum out-of-sample validation count.
- Sufficient regime diversity.
- Sufficient liquidity coverage.

If a pattern has a strong result but weak sample coverage, it should be marked:

```text
inconclusive_sample_too_small
```

not:

```text
validated_edge
```

QSASE should prefer honest uncertainty over false precision.

### 9.8 Train, Validation, And Walk-Forward Discipline

The lab should not run one full-sample test and call it truth.

It should support:

- Chronological train and validation splits.
- Walk-forward validation.
- Rolling-window performance.
- Expanding-window performance.
- Regime-split validation.
- Source-availability split validation.
- Pre-defined strategy-definition dates for shadow replay.

The core rule is:

```text
The model cannot learn from records that would not yet have existed at the
decision point being tested.
```

This protects QSASE from a common failure mode: using a beautiful backtest that
could not have been known in real time.

### 9.9 Factor And Benchmark Controls

Linear tests must separate actual source-price edge from broad exposure.

For each relevant pattern, the lab should compare raw returns against:

- Broad market benchmark.
- Sector benchmark.
- Equal-weight sector basket where available.
- Volatility benchmark.
- Rates proxy.
- Dollar proxy.
- Commodity proxy.
- Liquidity proxy.
- Market-cap or factor proxy where relevant.

Example:

```text
If ACLED conflict intensity appears to lead an energy ETF, the lab must check
whether the result survives oil-price exposure, broad market beta, and sector
momentum.
```

Without controls, QSASE may mistake a market regime for a source-specific
edge.

### 9.10 Costs, Slippage, And Operational Friction

The lab should include conservative research assumptions for costs and
friction, even though it must not simulate paper proof.

Research-only friction checks should include:

- Spread proxy.
- Slippage proxy.
- Liquidity penalty.
- Delayed-entry penalty.
- Missed-bar penalty.
- Order-size sensitivity.
- Overnight gap risk.
- Halt or non-tradable session risk.
- Idempotency and duplicate-exposure hold frequency.
- Paper-route availability at the decision time.

The goal is not to pretend historical orders were submitted. The goal is to
reject patterns that only work under impossible execution assumptions.

### 9.11 Relationship To Akber's Filter

The Linear Pattern Recognition Lab does not replace Akber's filter.

It prepares the evidence Akber's filter needs to become measurable.

For each candidate pattern, the lab should export:

- Catalyst strength.
- Price reaction quality.
- Volume confirmation.
- Volatility behavior.
- Drawdown profile.
- Risk/reward profile.
- Clean invalidation possibility.
- Time horizon fit.
- Liquidity fit.
- False-positive history.

Akber's filter can later ask:

```text
Does this statistically interesting pattern also look like a trade that a
disciplined human operator would actually want to take?
```

That question belongs after the linear evidence exists, not before the
universe has been searched.

### 9.12 Relationship To Nonlinear And Quantum Review

The Linear Pattern Recognition Lab produces the baseline for the next lab.

It should route patterns into three broad paths:

`linear_accept`:

```text
The pattern is interpretable, statistically useful, and may already be strong
enough for Strategy Foundry review.
```

`linear_reject`:

```text
The pattern fails sample, expectancy, drawdown, factor-control, or leakage
checks and should not consume nonlinear or quantum review unless there is a
specific audit reason.
```

`linear_inconclusive_candidate_for_nonlinear_review`:

```text
The simple linear tests are weak or mixed, but the pattern may depend on
interaction effects, regimes, thresholds, clustering, or source combinations.
```

Nonlinear and quantum review should not be used as a vague rescue mechanism for
bad linear results. It should be used when there is a concrete reason to
believe linear methods are insufficient.

### 9.13 Rejection Discipline

The lab should make rejection as valuable as acceptance.

Every rejected pattern should record:

- Tested source recipe.
- Tested market expression.
- Tested window.
- Sample count.
- Failure reason.
- Leakage status.
- Factor-control result.
- Drawdown failure if applicable.
- Inconclusive coverage if applicable.
- Whether future retest is allowed.

Common rejection reasons:

- `sample_too_small`
- `coverage_too_sparse`
- `lookahead_leakage_detected`
- `no_positive_expectancy`
- `drawdown_too_large`
- `factor_control_failed`
- `hit_rate_unstable`
- `regime_fragile`
- `liquidity_unworkable`
- `duplicate_of_stronger_pattern`
- `not_explainable_enough`

Rejected patterns should remain searchable so Qadam does not rediscover the
same weak idea every week.

### 9.14 Ranking Model

The lab should rank accepted and inconclusive patterns with a transparent
linear score.

Recommended score components:

- Point-in-time safety.
- Sample size.
- Coverage quality.
- Effect size.
- Statistical confidence.
- Out-of-sample survival.
- Walk-forward survival.
- Factor-adjusted alpha.
- Hit rate.
- Expectancy.
- Drawdown quality.
- Liquidity quality.
- Regime durability.
- Explanation quality.
- Novelty versus existing patterns.

The output should include both the total score and component scores. A high
score should be explainable without reading code.

### 9.15 Integration With Existing Qadam Modules

This lab should connect to current Qadam modules instead of becoming a separate
research toy.

It should consume or reference:

- `orchestrator/pattern_recognition_engine.py`
- `orchestrator/edge_memory_ledger.py`
- `orchestrator/edge_pattern_ledger.py`
- `orchestrator/strategy_update_record.py`
- `orchestrator/phase6_shadow_strategy_runner.py`
- Historical backfill outputs.
- PaperOps summary outputs.

It should produce inputs for:

- Nonlinear And Quantum Pattern Lab.
- Strategy Foundry.
- Akber Filter Integration.
- Shadow Strategy Simulator.
- Strategy Router.
- Dashboard QSASE panels.

The key integration rule:

```text
Existing edge and pattern ledgers record what Qadam is already observing.
The Linear Pattern Recognition Lab tests whether those observations have
historical, point-in-time, statistically defensible support.
```

### 9.16 Dashboard Representation

The dashboard should represent the lab as a research decision table, not a wall
of prose.

Recommended fields:

- Linear lab status.
- Tested relationship count.
- Accepted linear pattern count.
- Rejected linear pattern count.
- Inconclusive pattern count.
- Top accepted pattern.
- Top rejected pattern reason.
- Leakage rejection count.
- Coverage gap count.
- Candidate for nonlinear review count.
- Candidate for Strategy Foundry count.
- Last run timestamp.

Recommended decision language:

```text
Linear result: candidate for nonlinear review
Reason: positive 5-day expectancy, survives sector control, sample still thin.
Authority: research only.
```

This keeps the dashboard specific and prevents another generic strategy blob.

### 9.17 Authority Boundary

The Linear Pattern Recognition Lab must never:

- Create Telegram commands.
- Create trade candidates directly.
- Create risk approvals.
- Create execution approvals.
- Submit paper orders.
- Write broker records.
- Bypass Q-CTRL review.
- Bypass the guarded Alpaca Paper route.
- Enable live capital.
- Create paper proof ledger credit.
- Simulate elapsed time in the 30-day paper growth trial.

It may:

- Promote patterns to nonlinear review.
- Promote patterns to Strategy Foundry review.
- Improve strategy rejection.
- Improve source weighting.
- Improve dashboard explanations.
- Improve future PaperOps setup selection after later gates pass.

### 9.18 Build Contract

Implementation should add:

```text
orchestrator/qsase_linear_pattern_lab.py
scripts/check_qsase_linear_pattern_lab.py
```

The module should expose:

```text
build_linear_pattern_results(settings)
load_linear_pattern_results(settings)
validate_linear_pattern_results(payload)
write_linear_pattern_results(payload, settings)
rank_linear_patterns(results)
```

The check script should validate:

- Required artifacts exist.
- Historical Source-Price Memory input is present.
- Records are point-in-time safe.
- Tests state sample size and windows.
- Accepted, rejected, and inconclusive patterns are all represented.
- Factor controls are present where required.
- Leakage-rejected records cannot be accepted.
- Execution authority is false.
- Proof credit authority is false.
- Live capital is false.
- Dashboard summary is public-safe.

The first implementation can use deterministic classical calculations and
simple statistical methods. It should prioritize clean contracts, leakage
discipline, and rejection logging over complex modelling.

### 9.19 Linear Lab Summary

The Linear Pattern Recognition Lab is QSASE's first serious truth test.

It turns historical memory into interpretable evidence. It finds source-price
relationships that simple methods can defend. It rejects weak patterns before
they waste nonlinear or quantum review. It creates the classical baseline that
advanced QSASE layers must beat.

Most importantly, it keeps research separate from authority. A strong linear
backtest can move a pattern into nonlinear review, Strategy Foundry, Akber's
filter, and shadow replay. It cannot submit a trade, grant proof credit, or
pretend the 30-day paper growth trial has already happened.

## 10. Nonlinear And Quantum Pattern Lab

The Nonlinear And Quantum Pattern Lab is the layer that asks whether Qadam's
source-price universe contains relationships that linear methods cannot
properly explain.

It consumes the Linear Pattern Recognition Lab output and asks:

```text
Which patterns depend on thresholds, regimes, source interactions, delayed
effects, ambiguity, clustering, or strategy-choice collapse rather than a
simple linear relationship?
```

This lab is where Qadam becomes more self-aware of its own architecture. The
Python orchestrator handles deterministic replay and validation. Local models
score interaction effects without needing external authority. Frontier model
review can challenge explanations. The quantum oracle can review pattern
ambiguity and strategy collapse through the bounded Qadam quantum contract.

The lab must not overclaim. Quantum review is a core review layer where
required, but it is advisory, bounded, public-safe, and non-executing.

### 10.1 Purpose

The purpose of this lab is to identify patterns that are invisible, unstable,
or under-explained under classical linear testing.

It should answer:

```text
Which source combinations matter only together?
Which regimes change whether a pattern works?
Which thresholds flip a relationship from noise to edge?
Which patterns look weak linearly but strong conditionally?
Which strategy families should collapse into one route?
Which strategy families should remain separate?
Which candidates are too ambiguous for paper trading?
Which advanced findings actually beat the linear baseline?
```

The lab should not exist to make weak ideas sound sophisticated. It exists to
challenge the classical baseline and only preserve the cases where nonlinear or
quantum review adds measurable value.

### 10.2 Inputs

The lab should consume:

- Historical Source-Price Memory records.
- Universal Source-Price Pattern Matrix rows.
- Full-Universe Pattern Search candidates and rejects.
- Linear Pattern Recognition Lab results.
- Linear rejects that are eligible for nonlinear audit.
- Source health and coverage metadata.
- Trading universe metadata.
- Edge memory ledger entries.
- Edge pattern ledger entries.
- Quantum oracle status and local simulator status.
- Q-CTRL / IBM readiness status where available.
- Strategy weight update records.
- Shadow strategy outcomes where available.
- Paper proof ledger references for comparison only.

The lab should not run on records that failed point-in-time or leakage checks.
It may inspect rejected records for audit and regression purposes, but it must
not promote leakage-tainted records into candidate strategy evidence.

### 10.3 Output Artifacts

QSASE should create:

```text
data/runtime/qsase_nonlinear_quantum_pattern_lab.json
data/runtime/qsase_nonlinear_pattern_results.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_quantum_pattern_reviews.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_nonlinear_quantum_pattern_lab_history.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_nonlinear_quantum_pattern_lab_events.jsonl
```

The nonlinear summary artifact should expose:

```text
status
generated_at
candidate_input_count
tested_interaction_count
accepted_nonlinear_pattern_count
rejected_nonlinear_pattern_count
inconclusive_nonlinear_pattern_count
linear_baseline_beat_count
linear_baseline_not_beat_count
candidate_for_quantum_review_count
candidate_for_strategy_foundry_count
execution_allowed
proof_credit_allowed
live_capital_enabled
```

The quantum summary artifact should expose:

```text
status
generated_at
reviewed_pattern_count
quantum_backend
quantum_mode
local_validation_status
hardware_submission_allowed
hardware_submitted
provider_call_allowed
execution_allowed
paper_order_allowed
trade_candidate_authority
proof_credit_allowed
recommendation_authority
```

All authority fields must remain false unless a later explicit policy gate
changes a status-only provider-readiness field. Even then, this lab must not
gain trading authority.

### 10.4 Nonlinear Pattern Result Contract

Each nonlinear result should use a schema like:

```json
{
  "nonlinear_pattern_id": "nonlinear-acled-kalshi-xle-20240601",
  "source_recipe": {
    "source_families": [
      "geopolitical_events",
      "prediction_markets",
      "market_data"
    ],
    "source_names": ["acled", "kalshi", "alpaca_market_bars"],
    "feature_names": [
      "source_intensity_score",
      "odds_shift_24h",
      "market_reaction_lag_score"
    ],
    "interaction_type": "threshold_regime_interaction"
  },
  "market_expression": {
    "instrument": "XLE",
    "asset_class": "equity_etf",
    "horizon": "5d",
    "direction": "long"
  },
  "baseline": {
    "linear_pattern_id": "linear-acled-xle-5d-20240601",
    "linear_score": 0.58,
    "linear_status": "candidate_for_nonlinear_review"
  },
  "nonlinear_tests": {
    "interaction_gain": 0.17,
    "threshold_stability": 0.71,
    "regime_dependence_score": 0.64,
    "conditional_expectancy": 0.0078,
    "walk_forward_survival": 0.66,
    "false_discovery_penalty": 0.19
  },
  "sample": {
    "memory_record_count": 184,
    "point_in_time_safe": true,
    "coverage_score": 0.82,
    "regime_count": 4,
    "walk_forward_windows": 8
  },
  "decision": {
    "nonlinear_status": "candidate_for_quantum_review",
    "reason": "interaction improves expectancy over linear baseline and remains stable across walk-forward windows",
    "reject_reason": null,
    "candidate_for_strategy_foundry": true,
    "candidate_for_akber_filter": true,
    "candidate_for_paper_route": false
  },
  "authority": {
    "research_only": true,
    "execution_allowed": false,
    "proof_credit_allowed": false,
    "live_capital_enabled": false
  }
}
```

The core field is `baseline`. Every nonlinear claim must be compared against
the linear baseline. If the nonlinear result does not improve explanation,
ranking, stability, or rejection quality, QSASE should not preserve it as an
advanced insight.

### 10.5 Quantum Pattern Review Contract

Each quantum review should use a schema like:

```json
{
  "quantum_review_id": "quantum-review-nonlinear-acled-kalshi-xle-20240601",
  "source_pattern_id": "nonlinear-acled-kalshi-xle-20240601",
  "job_type": "pattern_recognition",
  "strategy_collapse_job_required": true,
  "backend": "classical_fallback",
  "backend_status": "ok",
  "local_simulation_mode": "deterministic_classical_shadow",
  "local_validation_status": "passed",
  "hardware_submission_allowed": false,
  "hardware_submitted": false,
  "provider_call_allowed": false,
  "input_contract": {
    "feature_count": 12,
    "source_count": 3,
    "instrument_focus": "XLE",
    "evidence_item_count": 184,
    "input_fingerprint": "sha256-example"
  },
  "scores": {
    "pattern_score": 0.67,
    "ambiguity_score": 0.41,
    "confidence_delta": 0.06,
    "strategy_collapse_score": 0.58
  },
  "recommendation": "upgrade_shadow_confidence",
  "required_next_steps": [
    "strategy_foundry_review",
    "akber_filter_review",
    "shadow_strategy_simulation"
  ],
  "authority": {
    "execution_allowed": false,
    "paper_order_allowed": false,
    "trade_candidate_created": false,
    "proof_credit_allowed": false,
    "live_capital_enabled": false
  }
}
```

The review can adjust research confidence. It cannot become execution truth.

### 10.6 Nonlinear Method Families

The lab should start with deterministic, interpretable nonlinear methods before
adding heavier modelling.

Interaction scans:

```text
Measure whether two or more source features matter together more than they
matter separately.
```

Threshold scans:

```text
Find whether a pattern only works after intensity, odds movement, volatility,
volume, or trust crosses a threshold.
```

Regime-conditioned tests:

```text
Measure whether a pattern works only in specific macro, volatility, liquidity,
geopolitical, or market-session regimes.
```

Tree-style rule extraction:

```text
Find simple branching rules that explain conditional relationships without
turning the lab into an opaque model zoo.
```

Cluster and nearest-regime matching:

```text
Find historical environments that resemble the current environment and test
whether similar source-price behavior followed.
```

Anomaly and divergence detection:

```text
Find when source activity and market pricing disagree strongly enough to
suggest delayed repricing.
```

Lagged state transitions:

```text
Model whether a pattern moves through recognizable states before the market
reacts.
```

The first version should prioritize methods whose decisions can be summarized
as short decision records. If a method cannot explain why it ranked a pattern,
it should not feed Strategy Foundry.

### 10.7 Quantum Review Roles

Quantum review should serve four bounded roles.

Pattern recognition review:

```text
Challenge whether a nonlinear source-price relationship is coherent or likely
noise.
```

Ambiguity scoring:

```text
Estimate whether the evidence is too ambiguous to promote, even if the pattern
looks promising.
```

Feature compression review:

```text
Compress a high-dimensional source recipe into a bounded oracle input so Qadam
can compare competing candidate patterns consistently.
```

Strategy collapse review:

```text
Compare multiple candidate strategy routes and decide whether they are really
separate strategies or variants of the same underlying pattern.
```

These roles match Qadam's current quantum oracle shape: pattern recognition
and strategy collapse / ambiguity review. They do not include execution,
broker routing, proof credit, or live-capital authority.

### 10.8 Backend Discipline

The lab must report the backend used for every quantum review.

Allowed backend states:

- Deterministic classical fallback.
- Optional Qiskit Aer local simulator when local dependencies are available.
- Explicitly gated hardware/device probe status where a later policy allows it.

Disallowed assumptions:

- Treating hardware access as present when only fallback ran.
- Treating Q-CTRL or IBM readiness as execution truth.
- Persisting raw provider responses into public artifacts.
- Exposing secret shapes or credential values.
- Submitting hardware jobs from this lab.
- Creating autonomous quantum schedulers from this lab.

If a provider is unavailable, blocked by policy, missing credentials, or missing
local validation, the lab should degrade to local deterministic review and
write the degraded backend state explicitly.

### 10.9 Comparison Against Linear Baseline

Every nonlinear or quantum result must answer:

```text
What did this add beyond the Linear Pattern Recognition Lab?
```

Valid improvements include:

- Higher walk-forward expectancy.
- Better rejection of false positives.
- Better drawdown filtering.
- Better regime identification.
- Better source-combination ranking.
- Better strategy-family collapse.
- Better ambiguity detection.
- Better explanation of delayed market reaction.

Invalid improvements include:

- Higher in-sample score only.
- More complex explanation with no predictive gain.
- Quantum score that contradicts leakage checks.
- Model output that cannot be explained.
- Pattern that only works after ignoring costs, slippage, or route state.

If the advanced layer does not beat the linear baseline, the result should be:

```text
advanced_review_not_incremental
```

That is a valid and useful outcome.

### 10.10 Multi-Source Pattern Recipes

The lab should be especially focused on recipes that require several source
families at once.

Examples:

- ACLED escalation plus Kalshi odds drift plus energy ETF non-response.
- Reddit attention spike plus options volatility compression plus single-name
  liquidity expansion.
- Capitol Trades filing cluster plus sector underreaction plus improving
  technical trend.
- SEC filing surprise plus frontier model risk summary plus delayed volume
  confirmation.
- Macro liquidity stress plus dollar strength plus gold or silver divergence.
- Weather disruption plus regional energy exposure plus shipping or insurance
  repricing lag.
- Prediction-market probability jump plus equity market disbelief.
- PaperOps idempotency hold plus repeated missed setup class.

These are not final strategies. They are candidate recipes that Strategy
Foundry may later turn into strategy hypotheses.

### 10.11 Model Self-Awareness

This lab should explicitly account for Qadam's own stack.

Python orchestrator:

```text
Best for deterministic replay, validation, feature construction, artifact
writing, authority gates, and reproducibility.
```

Local model:

```text
Best for low-cost summarization, clustering support, explanation drafts, and
local-first review where privacy or cost matters.
```

Frontier model:

```text
Best for adversarial critique, alternative causal explanations, narrative
compression, and identifying weak assumptions in a candidate pattern.
```

Quantum oracle:

```text
Best for bounded ambiguity review, nonlinear feature compression, pattern
recognition challenge, and strategy collapse review.
```

QSASE should not ask every component to do the same job. The lab should route
work to the component whose nature fits the task, then write the result back
into one coherent decision record.

### 10.12 Rejection Discipline

Nonlinear and quantum rejection must be first-class.

Common nonlinear rejection reasons:

- `no_incremental_gain_over_linear`
- `interaction_unstable`
- `threshold_overfit`
- `regime_too_sparse`
- `walk_forward_failed`
- `false_discovery_penalty_too_high`
- `explanation_not_stable`
- `costs_destroy_edge`
- `route_state_not_actionable`

Common quantum review hold reasons:

- `ambiguity_too_high`
- `pattern_score_too_low`
- `strategy_collapse_unclear`
- `input_contract_invalid`
- `local_validation_failed`
- `backend_degraded`
- `hardware_claim_not_supported`
- `authority_boundary_violation`

Rejected patterns must remain searchable. A rejected advanced pattern is not a
failure; it is memory that stops Qadam from re-consuming compute on the same
weak structure.

### 10.13 Ranking Model

The advanced ranking model should combine:

- Linear baseline score.
- Incremental nonlinear gain.
- Walk-forward survival.
- Regime durability.
- Interaction stability.
- Source diversity.
- Coverage quality.
- False-discovery penalty.
- Cost and slippage robustness.
- Quantum pattern score.
- Quantum ambiguity score.
- Strategy collapse clarity.
- Explanation quality.
- Route readiness.

The ranking should not allow quantum score to override a failed leakage check,
failed authority check, or failed risk boundary.

Recommended final statuses:

- `nonlinear_rejected`
- `nonlinear_inconclusive`
- `candidate_for_quantum_review`
- `quantum_hold`
- `quantum_review_passed_for_research`
- `candidate_for_strategy_foundry`
- `advanced_review_not_incremental`

### 10.14 Relationship To Strategy Foundry

This lab should not create strategies directly.

It should send Strategy Foundry a compact decision packet:

```text
source recipe
market expression
linear baseline
nonlinear improvement
quantum ambiguity review
regime conditions
failure modes
sample quality
route constraints
recommended hypothesis path
```

Strategy Foundry then decides whether the pattern should become:

- A new strategy hypothesis.
- A modification to an existing strategy family.
- A rejection record.
- A shadow-only monitoring rule.
- A dashboard-only research observation.

The separation matters because pattern discovery and strategy design are not
the same function.

### 10.15 Relationship To PaperOps

The Nonlinear And Quantum Pattern Lab can improve future PaperOps selection,
but it does not participate in execution.

It may inform:

- Which setup classes deserve more attention.
- Which sources should receive higher research priority.
- Which strategy families should be demoted.
- Which candidate patterns deserve shadow replay.
- Which ambiguity holds should stop a setup before risk review.

It must not:

- Create a paper order.
- Mark a setup eligible by itself.
- Override idempotency.
- Override duplicate exposure checks.
- Override daily drawdown checks.
- Override Q-CTRL paper consultation holds.
- Override the guarded Alpaca Paper route.
- Create paper proof ledger credit.

### 10.16 Dashboard Representation

The dashboard should represent this lab as a short advanced-review decision
record, not a technical essay.

Recommended fields:

- Nonlinear lab status.
- Tested interaction count.
- Linear baseline beat count.
- Quantum review status.
- Quantum backend.
- Quantum mode.
- Top advanced pattern.
- Top ambiguity hold.
- Candidate for Strategy Foundry count.
- Authority state.
- Latest blocker.

Recommended decision language:

```text
Advanced review: candidate for Strategy Foundry
Why: nonlinear interaction beat the 5-day linear baseline and quantum ambiguity is tolerable.
Backend: deterministic classical shadow
Authority: research only
```

The dashboard must not imply confirmed hardware execution unless a real
hardware-backed run is explicitly recorded.

### 10.17 Authority Boundary

The Nonlinear And Quantum Pattern Lab must never:

- Create Telegram commands.
- Create trade candidates directly.
- Create risk approvals.
- Create execution approvals.
- Submit paper orders.
- Write broker records.
- Call live broker endpoints.
- Enable live capital.
- Treat quantum output as execution truth.
- Treat Q-CTRL / IBM readiness as proof.
- Submit hardware jobs without an explicit approved hardware gate.
- Persist secret values or raw provider responses.
- Create paper proof ledger credit.
- Simulate elapsed time in the 30-day paper growth trial.

It may:

- Promote patterns to Strategy Foundry.
- Promote patterns to Akber Filter review.
- Increase or decrease research confidence.
- Add ambiguity holds.
- Improve strategy-family ranking.
- Improve source weighting.
- Improve dashboard explanation.
- Improve future PaperOps setup selection after later gates pass.

### 10.18 Build Contract

Implementation should add:

```text
orchestrator/qsase_nonlinear_quantum_pattern_lab.py
scripts/check_qsase_nonlinear_quantum_pattern_lab.py
```

The module should expose:

```text
build_nonlinear_pattern_results(settings)
build_quantum_pattern_reviews(settings, nonlinear_results)
load_nonlinear_pattern_results(settings)
load_quantum_pattern_reviews(settings)
validate_nonlinear_pattern_results(payload)
validate_quantum_pattern_reviews(payload)
write_nonlinear_pattern_results(payload, settings)
write_quantum_pattern_reviews(payload, settings)
```

The check script should validate:

- Linear Pattern Recognition Lab input exists.
- Historical Source-Price Memory input exists.
- Nonlinear records are point-in-time safe.
- Every nonlinear claim references a linear baseline.
- Every advanced pass states incremental value over linear.
- Rejected and inconclusive records are preserved.
- Quantum reviews use allowed job types.
- Quantum reviews report backend and mode.
- Hardware submission flags remain false unless explicitly gated elsewhere.
- Execution authority is false.
- Paper order authority is false.
- Proof credit authority is false.
- Live capital is false.
- Public dashboard summary does not overclaim hardware execution.

The first implementation should prefer deterministic calculations and the
existing quantum oracle contract over adding opaque dependencies. It should be
boring, auditable, and difficult to misinterpret.

### 10.19 Nonlinear And Quantum Lab Summary

The Nonlinear And Quantum Pattern Lab is where QSASE tests whether Qadam's full
source-price universe contains interaction effects that linear methods miss.

It does not replace the Linear Pattern Recognition Lab. It stands on top of it.
It asks whether advanced review adds real incremental value, whether ambiguity
is tolerable, and whether strategy routes should collapse or separate.

The lab turns Qadam's unusual stack into an advantage: deterministic Python for
replay, local inference for cheap review, frontier inference for adversarial
critique, and the quantum oracle for bounded ambiguity and strategy-collapse
review. But it keeps the boundary intact. Advanced pattern review can improve
research confidence. It cannot trade, approve risk, grant proof credit, or fake
the actual paper calendar.

## 11. Strategy Foundry

Strategy Foundry is the layer that turns discovered patterns into strategy
hypotheses.

It does not start with a list of strategies and search for evidence to justify
them. It starts with universal pattern discovery, historical memory, linear
testing, nonlinear review, and quantum ambiguity review. Only then does it ask:

```text
Does this pattern deserve to become a strategy hypothesis, a modification to
an existing strategy family, a new strategy family, a shadow-only monitor, or a
rejection record?
```

This is one of the most important QSASE boundaries. A strategy hypothesis is
not a trade candidate. A strategy family is not execution approval. The Foundry
is a strategy-design layer, not a PaperOps route.

### 11.1 Purpose

The purpose of Strategy Foundry is to convert vetted source-price patterns into
auditable strategy hypotheses.

It should answer:

```text
What repeatable market behavior did Qadam find?
What data sources created the edge?
What instrument expression could represent the pattern?
What catalyst class triggers it?
What entry logic would express it?
What invalidates it?
What risk shape does it create?
Which existing strategy family, if any, does it belong to?
Does it require a new strategy family?
What gates must pass before it can even become a paper-review candidate?
What would Qadam learn by shadowing or paper-testing it later?
```

The Foundry is where strategy becomes explicit. It forces Qadam to turn a
pattern into a defined object with lineage, boundaries, assumptions, and
failure modes.

### 11.2 Inputs

Strategy Foundry should consume:

- Full-Universe Pattern Search candidates.
- Full-Universe Pattern Search rejects.
- Historical Source-Price Memory records.
- Linear Pattern Recognition Lab results.
- Nonlinear Pattern Lab results.
- Quantum Pattern Review records.
- Existing Phase 4 candidate strategy universe records.
- Existing strategy research intake records.
- Existing strategy update records.
- Strategy Lead challenge context.
- Akber filter requirements as pending downstream checks.
- Shadow strategy outcomes where available.
- Paper proof ledger references for comparison only.
- PaperOps route state for route-fit assessment only.

The Foundry should only consider patterns that have enough evidence to be
translated into a strategy hypothesis. It may record weak patterns, but weak
patterns should become rejection records or watch-only records, not strategy
hypotheses.

### 11.3 Output Artifacts

QSASE should create:

```text
data/runtime/qsase_strategy_hypotheses.json
data/runtime/qsase_strategy_hypotheses.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_strategy_hypotheses_history.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_strategy_hypotheses_events.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_strategy_family_map.json
data/runtime/qsase_rejected_strategy_hypotheses.jsonl
```

The summary artifact should expose:

```text
status
generated_at
input_pattern_count
strategy_hypothesis_count
existing_family_match_count
new_family_proposal_count
strategy_modification_proposal_count
shadow_only_monitor_count
rejected_pattern_count
paper_review_candidate_count
execution_allowed
paper_order_allowed
proof_credit_allowed
live_capital_enabled
```

`paper_review_candidate_count` does not mean paper orders are allowed. It means
the hypothesis is structured enough to be considered later by the Strategy
Router and PaperOps gate interface.

### 11.4 Strategy Hypothesis Contract

Each hypothesis should use a schema like:

```json
{
  "strategy_hypothesis_id": "qsase-strategy-energy-event-probability-lag-001",
  "status": "strategy_hypothesis_recorded_not_approved",
  "name": "Energy Event Probability Lag",
  "hypothesis_type": "new_strategy_family_candidate",
  "lineage": {
    "source_pattern_ids": [
      "pattern-acled-kalshi-xle-5d-001"
    ],
    "linear_pattern_ids": [
      "linear-acled-xle-5d-20240601"
    ],
    "nonlinear_pattern_ids": [
      "nonlinear-acled-kalshi-xle-20240601"
    ],
    "quantum_review_ids": [
      "quantum-review-nonlinear-acled-kalshi-xle-20240601"
    ],
    "historical_memory_refs": [
      "qsase-memory-2024-03-12-acled-oil-xle-001"
    ]
  },
  "source_recipe": {
    "source_families": [
      "geopolitical_events",
      "prediction_markets",
      "market_data"
    ],
    "source_names": ["acled", "kalshi", "alpaca_market_bars"],
    "required_source_groups": ["conflict", "prediction_market", "market_price"],
    "minimum_source_quorum": 2
  },
  "market_expression": {
    "primary_instrument": "XLE",
    "asset_class": "equity_etf",
    "allowed_proxy_set": ["XLE", "USO", "energy_equities"],
    "excluded_instruments": [],
    "paper_route_required": true
  },
  "strategy_logic": {
    "catalyst_class": "geopolitical_probability_dislocation",
    "entry_logic_summary": "Watch for conflict intensity and prediction-market odds moving before energy prices respond.",
    "exit_logic_summary": "Exit after catalyst resolution, failed confirmation, stop condition, or time window expiry.",
    "invalidation_logic_summary": "Invalidate if source quorum fails, odds reverse, market confirms in the opposite direction, or route state becomes unsafe.",
    "time_horizon": "3d_to_10d",
    "no_trade_conditions": [
      "source quorum missing",
      "market already repriced",
      "duplicate exposure conflict",
      "quantum ambiguity hold",
      "paper route unavailable"
    ]
  },
  "evidence": {
    "historical_sample_size": 184,
    "linear_score": 0.58,
    "nonlinear_score": 0.67,
    "quantum_ambiguity_score": 0.41,
    "walk_forward_survival": 0.66,
    "factor_control_status": "survived_sector_control",
    "coverage_score": 0.82
  },
  "family_mapping": {
    "mapped_existing_family": null,
    "proposed_new_family": "energy_event_probability_lag",
    "overlaps_existing_families": [
      "crude_oil_energy_security_disruption",
      "prediction_market_geopolitical_dislocation"
    ],
    "strategy_collapse_review_required": true
  },
  "route_readiness": {
    "strategy_router_eligible": true,
    "akber_filter_required": true,
    "shadow_replay_required": true,
    "paperops_gate_required": true,
    "paper_review_candidate": false,
    "paper_order_allowed": false
  },
  "learning_value": {
    "expected_learning_class": "cross_source_event_probability_lag",
    "what_qadam_would_learn": "Whether prediction-market repricing plus conflict data leads energy-sector paper setups better than either source alone.",
    "postmortem_questions": [
      "Did the source recipe lead the market?",
      "Did Akber's filter remove false positives?",
      "Did quantum ambiguity correctly hold weak cases?"
    ]
  },
  "authority": {
    "strategy_approved": false,
    "trade_candidate_created": false,
    "risk_handoff_allowed": false,
    "execution_allowed": false,
    "paper_order_allowed": false,
    "broker_write_allowed": false,
    "proof_credit_allowed": false,
    "live_capital_enabled": false
  }
}
```

This schema has to be explicit because later modules will treat it as a
contract. A vague strategy thesis is not enough. The Foundry must record what
the strategy is, where it came from, why it might work, how it fails, and why
it is still not execution authority.

### 11.5 Foundry Decision Types

Every input pattern should become one of these decision types:

`existing_family_match`:

```text
The pattern strengthens or refines an existing strategy family.
```

`existing_family_modification`:

```text
The pattern suggests a change to an existing family, such as a new source
weight, stricter catalyst requirement, new invalidation rule, or demotion.
```

`new_strategy_family_candidate`:

```text
The pattern is repeatable and coherent but does not fit the current five
strategy families cleanly.
```

`shadow_only_monitor`:

```text
The pattern is interesting but not ready to become a strategy hypothesis.
```

`rejected_pattern`:

```text
The pattern is weak, unsafe, overfit, non-paperable, non-explainable, or
otherwise not useful enough to promote.
```

The Foundry must preserve all five outcomes. A system that only records
promoted hypotheses will relearn the same bad ideas.

### 11.6 Mapping To Existing Strategy Families

Qadam already has inherited strategy families across prediction markets, crude
oil, defence, silver, and semiconductors. Strategy Foundry should respect that
inheritance without being trapped by it.

Mapping should compare:
