- Source recipe.
- Catalyst class.
- Instrument universe.
- Historical response window.
- Invalidation logic.
- Risk shape.
- Existing source weights.
- Existing model weights.
- Existing quantum role.
- Existing no-trade conditions.
- Existing market-confirmation requirements.

If the pattern fits an existing family, the Foundry should produce a
family-update proposal, not a new family.

Examples:

- Conflict escalation plus oil proxy lag may map to
  `crude_oil_energy_security_disruption`.
- Conflict escalation plus defence procurement repricing may map to
  `defence_repricing_geopolitical_watch`.
- Macro liquidity stress plus silver divergence may map to
  `silver_macro_liquidity_stress`.
- Policy shock plus semiconductor volatility may map to
  `semiconductor_policy_options_asymmetry`.
- Event odds dislocation may map to
  `prediction_market_geopolitical_dislocation`.

The Foundry should avoid duplicate strategy families that are only renamed
versions of existing sleeves.

### 11.7 Creating New Strategy Families

The Foundry may propose a new strategy family only when the pattern cannot be
honestly mapped to an existing family.

New family requirements:

- Distinct source recipe.
- Distinct catalyst class or market behavior.
- Distinct instrument expression.
- Sufficient historical sample.
- Point-in-time safe evidence.
- Linear or nonlinear support.
- Quantum ambiguity review where required.
- Clear invalidation logic.
- Clear no-trade conditions.
- Paper-route expression possible in principle.
- Measurable learning value.
- No duplicate exposure conflict with an existing family.

New family proposals should be conservative. Qadam should not create a new
strategy family because a pattern has an interesting story. It should create
one only when the pattern represents a distinct repeatable behavior that the
existing families would distort or hide.

### 11.8 Strategy Logic Requirements

Every strategy hypothesis must define:

- Name.
- Hypothesis type.
- Source recipe.
- Catalyst class.
- Market expression.
- Entry logic summary.
- Exit logic summary.
- Invalidation logic summary.
- No-trade conditions.
- Required gates.
- Historical sample size.
- Linear evidence.
- Nonlinear evidence where available.
- Quantum ambiguity score where required.
- Akber filter requirements.
- Shadow replay requirements.
- PaperOps gate requirements.
- Route readiness state.
- Learning value.

The Foundry should reject any hypothesis that cannot define these fields in a
specific way. Generic strategy language is not acceptable.

### 11.9 Required Gates

Strategy hypotheses should carry required gates forward, but they should not
pass those gates themselves.

Required gates may include:

- Source quorum.
- Source freshness.
- Historical memory coverage.
- Linear pattern result.
- Nonlinear review.
- Quantum ambiguity review.
- Strategy Lead challenge.
- Akber filter review.
- Shadow replay.
- Signal Integrity.
- Risk budget.
- Duplicate exposure check.
- Daily drawdown check.
- Execution policy.
- Paper route readiness.
- Alpaca Paper route only.

The Foundry can say:

```text
This strategy would need these gates.
```

It cannot say:

```text
These gates have passed for a paper order.
```

That distinction prevents strategy design from bypassing PaperOps.

### 11.10 Rejection Discipline

Strategy Foundry should reject aggressively.

Common rejection reasons:

- `overfit_pattern`
- `sample_too_small`
- `source_quorum_weak`
- `point_in_time_leakage`
- `linear_evidence_failed`
- `nonlinear_not_incremental`
- `quantum_ambiguity_too_high`
- `not_distinct_from_existing_family`
- `no_clear_invalidation`
- `no_clean_paper_expression`
- `risk_shape_unacceptable`
- `duplicate_exposure_conflict`
- `unexplainable_thesis`
- `route_unavailable`
- `learning_value_too_low`
- `live_only_expression_not_allowed`

Each rejection should record:

- Source pattern id.
- Proposed family or existing family.
- Rejection reason.
- Evidence summary.
- Whether retest is allowed.
- Retest condition.
- Authority flags.

Rejected records should stay in `qsase_rejected_strategy_hypotheses.jsonl` so
Qadam does not repeatedly regenerate the same weak strategy.

### 11.11 Strategy Lead And Model Roles

The Foundry should route different work to different parts of Qadam.

Python orchestrator:

```text
Builds deterministic hypothesis records, validates schemas, writes artifacts,
and enforces authority flags.
```

Local Research Analyst:

```text
Compresses candidate evidence, identifies missing source context, and drafts
local-first summaries.
```

Frontier Strategy Lead:

```text
Challenges the hypothesis, tests alternative causal explanations, identifies
failure modes, and checks whether the strategy is coherent enough to preserve.
```

Head of Quant:

```text
Reviews ambiguity, nonlinear sensitivity, and strategy-collapse questions
through the bounded quantum contract.
```

Akber filter:

```text
Comes after Foundry creation. It checks whether the hypothesis resembles a
tradeable setup with catalyst quality, pricing gap, volatility behavior,
technical confirmation, flow, and clean risk/reward.
```

The Foundry should not let model prose become strategy truth. Model output is
evidence and challenge context, not authority.

### 11.12 Relationship To Strategy Update Record

Existing strategy update records document proposal-only adjustments from edge
memory and pattern-recognition evidence. Strategy Foundry should extend that
idea.

The relationship should be:

```text
edge memory and pattern evidence
-> strategy update proposal
-> QSASE foundry hypothesis
-> shadow and filter review
-> router decision
```

Strategy Foundry may convert a strategy update proposal into:

- A refined existing-family hypothesis.
- A demotion or hold recommendation.
- A source-weight proposal.
- A new invalidation rule proposal.
- A rejected update record.

It must not apply the update automatically. Strategy mutation remains
proposal-first and auditable.

### 11.13 Relationship To Akber Filter

Akber's filter should not limit the universal search before the Foundry runs.

The order is:

```text
universal discovery
-> linear and nonlinear evidence
-> Strategy Foundry hypothesis
-> Akber filter
```

This matters because Akber's filter is a practical trading-quality filter, not
a universal pattern-discovery engine. It should veto weak strategy hypotheses,
not prevent Qadam from seeing unexpected patterns in the first place.

The Foundry should prepare Akber-filter inputs:

- Catalyst quality.
- Pricing gap.
- Volatility behavior.
- Technical setup summary.
- Volume or flow confirmation.
- Invalidation clarity.
- Risk/reward structure.
- False-positive history.

### 11.14 Relationship To PaperOps

Strategy Foundry does not hand off directly to PaperOps.

It produces strategy hypotheses that may later be routed by:

```text
Strategy Router
-> PaperOps Gate Interface
-> existing guarded PaperOps route
```

The Foundry must not:

- Create trade candidates.
- Create risk approvals.
- Create execution approvals.
- Stage paper orders.
- Submit paper orders.
- Write broker records.
- Override idempotency.
- Override duplicate exposure checks.
- Override Q-CTRL paper consultation holds.
- Grant paper proof ledger credit.

The strongest Foundry output is still only:

```text
strategy_hypothesis_ready_for_router_review
```

not:

```text
paper_order_ready
```

### 11.15 Dashboard Representation

The dashboard should show Strategy Foundry as a concise decision table.

Recommended fields:

- Foundry status.
- Hypothesis count.
- Existing-family match count.
- New-family proposal count.
- Rejection count.
- Top hypothesis.
- Top rejection reason.
- Required next gate.
- Paper-review candidate count.
- Authority state.

Recommended decision language:

```text
Foundry decision: new strategy family candidate
Why: ACLED + Kalshi + energy ETF lag beat linear baseline and survived ambiguity review.
Next gate: Akber filter and shadow replay
Authority: strategy hypothesis only
```

The dashboard should avoid paragraphs that sound like generic investment
research. A Fund Manager should be able to see what was found, why it matters,
what gate comes next, and what authority is still blocked.

### 11.16 Authority Boundary

Strategy Foundry must never:

- Create Telegram commands.
- Create trade candidates directly.
- Create risk approvals.
- Create execution approvals.
- Stage paper orders.
- Submit paper orders.
- Write broker records.
- Call broker endpoints.
- Call quantum providers directly.
- Apply strategy mutations automatically.
- Change source weights automatically.
- Change model weights automatically.
- Enable live capital.
- Create paper proof ledger credit.
- Simulate elapsed time in the 30-day paper growth trial.

It may:

- Record strategy hypotheses.
- Record strategy rejections.
- Propose existing-family modifications.
- Propose new strategy families.
- Prepare Akber filter inputs.
- Prepare shadow replay inputs.
- Prepare router inputs.
- Improve dashboard strategy explanations.

### 11.17 Build Contract

Implementation should add:

```text
orchestrator/qsase_strategy_foundry.py
scripts/check_qsase_strategy_foundry.py
```

The module should expose:

```text
build_strategy_hypotheses(settings)
load_strategy_hypotheses(settings)
validate_strategy_hypotheses(payload)
write_strategy_hypotheses(payload, settings)
map_patterns_to_strategy_families(patterns, existing_families)
reject_unfit_strategy_hypotheses(hypotheses)
```

The check script should validate:

- Required upstream artifacts exist.
- Every hypothesis has source-pattern lineage.
- Every hypothesis has source recipe, catalyst class, market expression,
  entry logic, exit logic, invalidation logic, and no-trade conditions.
- Existing-family matches reference known families.
- New-family proposals state why existing families are insufficient.
- Rejections are preserved.
- Akber filter inputs are prepared but not passed.
- Shadow replay inputs are prepared but not executed.
- PaperOps handoff is not direct.
- Trade-candidate authority is false.
- Risk handoff is false.
- Execution authority is false.
- Paper order authority is false.
- Broker-write authority is false.
- Proof credit authority is false.
- Live capital is false.
- Dashboard summary is public-safe.

The first implementation should prioritize contract quality over strategy
volume. One well-formed hypothesis and several clear rejection records are more
valuable than ten vague strategy ideas.

### 11.18 Strategy Foundry Summary

Strategy Foundry is where QSASE turns pattern intelligence into strategy
objects.

It is the bridge between discovery and routing. It maps some patterns into
existing strategy families, proposes new families when the evidence is truly
distinct, prepares downstream Akber and shadow-review inputs, and rejects weak
or unsafe patterns before they reach PaperOps.

It is also a key anti-slop layer. A strategy cannot remain a vague thesis. It
must have lineage, source recipe, market expression, entry logic, invalidation
logic, evidence, required gates, route constraints, and authority flags. If it
does not, Qadam should reject it rather than dress it up as strategy.

## 12. Akber Filter Integration

Akber Filter Integration is the layer that applies Qadam's practical trading
taste to Strategy Foundry hypotheses.

The filter asks:

```text
This pattern may be statistically interesting, but does it actually look like a
tradeable setup that a disciplined operator would want to express through
guarded paper review?
```

This matters because QSASE can discover many patterns that are real but not
tradeable. Some patterns have no clean catalyst. Some have no pricing gap. Some
already repriced. Some have poor volatility shape. Some have weak technical
confirmation. Some have no clean invalidation. Some create ugly risk/reward.

Akber's filter is where Qadam rejects machine-discovered cleverness that does
not survive trading taste.

### 12.1 Purpose

The purpose of Akber Filter Integration is to make Akber's practical judgment
measurable, reproducible, and backtestable.

It should answer:

```text
Does the hypothesis have a real catalyst?
Is there a pricing gap or asymmetric setup?
Is volatility suppressed, expanding, or already exhausted?
Does the technical setup support the thesis?
Does volume or flow confirm the move?
Is the risk/reward clean enough?
Is invalidation clear?
Would this filter have improved historical hit rate or reduced drawdown?
Should this hypothesis pass, hold, or be rejected before routing?
```

The filter is not a universal pattern-search tool. It sits after Strategy
Foundry. Universal discovery must happen first so Qadam does not miss
unexpected source-price relationships. Akber's filter then decides whether a
strategy hypothesis has practical setup quality.

### 12.2 Inputs

Akber Filter Integration should consume:

- Strategy Foundry hypotheses.
- Strategy Foundry rejection records for audit and comparison.
- Linear Pattern Recognition Lab evidence.
- Nonlinear And Quantum Pattern Lab evidence.
- Historical Source-Price Memory records.
- Market snapshots.
- Volatility and liquidity context.
- Technical context where available.
- Options or pricing-gap context where available.
- Volume or flow context where available.
- Existing Signal Integrity Akber filter fields.
- Existing Research Goal `akber_stage` and `akber_stage_score` fields.
- Shadow strategy outcomes where available.
- Paper proof ledger references for attribution only.

The filter should degrade honestly when any context is missing. Missing options
or technical data should not be silently converted into a pass.

### 12.3 Output Artifacts

QSASE should create:

```text
data/runtime/qsase_akber_filter_integration.json
data/runtime/qsase_akber_filter_results.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_akber_filter_threshold_proposals.json
data/runtime/qsase_akber_filter_ablation.json
data/runtime/qsase_akber_filter_integration_history.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_akber_filter_integration_events.jsonl
```

The summary artifact should expose:

```text
status
generated_at
input_hypothesis_count
passed_filter_count
hold_filter_count
rejected_filter_count
missing_context_count
ablation_ready_count
historical_improvement_observed
candidate_for_shadow_replay_count
candidate_for_router_count
execution_allowed
paper_order_allowed
proof_credit_allowed
live_capital_enabled
```

The ablation artifact should compare outcomes with and without Akber's filter
so the filter is not only narrative.

### 12.4 Filter Result Contract

Each filter result should use a schema like:

```json
{
  "akber_filter_result_id": "akber-filter-energy-event-probability-lag-001",
  "strategy_hypothesis_id": "qsase-strategy-energy-event-probability-lag-001",
  "status": "akber_filter_hold",
  "stage_state": {
    "low_volatility": "missing_volatility_context",
    "options_distribution_gap": "pricing_gap_light",
    "catalyst_identification": "pass",
    "technical_setup": "shadow_pass",
    "obv_volume": "missing_volume_confirmation",
    "approval_policy": "not_reached_risk_agent_absent"
  },
  "dashboard_stage_state": {
    "context": "partial",
    "catalyst": "pass",
    "confirmation": "partial",
    "risk": "hold",
    "execution": "not_reached",
    "postmortem_learning": "pending"
  },
  "scores": {
    "catalyst_quality_score": 0.78,
    "pricing_gap_score": 0.56,
    "volatility_setup_score": 0.31,
    "technical_confirmation_score": 0.62,
    "volume_flow_score": 0.22,
    "risk_reward_score": 0.48,
    "invalidation_clarity_score": 0.71,
    "akber_filter_score": 0.53
  },
  "decision": {
    "filter_decision": "hold",
    "reason": "Catalyst and technical setup are present, but volatility and volume confirmation are incomplete.",
    "veto_reason": null,
    "next_required_evidence": [
      "volatility_context",
      "volume_confirmation"
    ],
    "candidate_for_shadow_replay": true,
    "candidate_for_strategy_router": false,
    "candidate_for_paper_review": false
  },
  "ablation": {
    "historical_filter_tested": true,
    "with_filter_hit_rate": 0.57,
    "without_filter_hit_rate": 0.51,
    "with_filter_max_drawdown": -0.028,
    "without_filter_max_drawdown": -0.043,
    "filter_added_value": true
  },
  "authority": {
    "trade_candidate_created": false,
    "risk_handoff_allowed": false,
    "execution_allowed": false,
    "paper_order_allowed": false,
    "broker_write_allowed": false,
    "proof_credit_allowed": false,
    "live_capital_enabled": false
  }
}
```

The result must separate a filter pass from execution authority. Even a perfect
Akber result only means the hypothesis can continue to later review.

### 12.5 Internal Six-Stage Contract

Qadam already exposes internal Akber filter fields in Signal Integrity:

- `low_volatility`
- `options_distribution_gap`
- `catalyst_identification`
- `technical_setup`
- `obv_volume`
- `approval_policy`

QSASE should preserve these fields so the new filter remains compatible with
existing review and dashboard checks.

The internal stages should mean:

`low_volatility`:

```text
Is volatility suppressed, mispriced, expanding from a base, or already too
late?
```

`options_distribution_gap`:

```text
Is there an options, probability, spread, sector-relative, or pricing gap that
makes the setup asymmetric?
```

`catalyst_identification`:

```text
Is there a trusted catalyst rather than a vague narrative?
```

`technical_setup`:

```text
Does market structure support the thesis, or is price action already denying
it?
```

`obv_volume`:

```text
Is volume, flow, attention, or participation confirming the setup?
```

`approval_policy`:

```text
Has the setup reached the later approval chain, or is it still blocked before
risk and execution review?
```

The filter should not force every strategy to use options data. For strategies
without options, the pricing-gap stage can use prediction-market odds,
sector-relative mispricing, volatility gap, spread gap, or event-probability
gap.

### 12.6 Dashboard Six-Stage Lens

The dashboard also represents Akber's lens in a broader six-stage user-facing
sequence:

- Context.
- Catalyst.
- Confirmation.
- Risk.
- Execution.
- Postmortem Learning.

QSASE should map the internal filter to this user-facing lens:

| Dashboard stage | Internal evidence |
| --- | --- |
| Context | Source recipe, market regime, historical memory, route state. |
| Catalyst | `catalyst_identification` and source-quorum evidence. |
| Confirmation | `technical_setup`, `obv_volume`, pricing gap, and market confirmation. |
| Risk | Invalidation clarity, drawdown profile, exposure conflict, risk/reward. |
| Execution | Paper route readiness only after later router and PaperOps checks. |
| Postmortem Learning | Historical ablation, shadow replay, paper proof ledger attribution. |

This keeps the dashboard readable without losing the internal audit fields.

### 12.7 Filter Decisions

Akber Filter Integration should produce one of five decisions.

`pass`:

```text
The strategy hypothesis has enough practical setup quality to continue to
shadow replay or router review.
```

`hold_missing_context`:

```text
The hypothesis is not rejected, but required context is missing.
```

`hold_wait_for_confirmation`:

```text
The catalyst exists, but technical, volume, volatility, or pricing confirmation
has not arrived.
```

`reject`:

```text
The hypothesis fails practical trading taste.
```

`audit_only`:

```text
The hypothesis is useful for learning or dashboard context but should not move
toward paper review.
```

A hold is not a failure. It is a useful state because it prevents Qadam from
turning early pattern discovery into premature trading.

### 12.8 Backtest And Ablation Discipline

Akber's filter must become testable.

For each strategy hypothesis, QSASE should compare:

- Strategy outcomes without Akber filter.
- Strategy outcomes with full Akber filter.
- Strategy outcomes with each Akber stage removed one at a time.
- Strategy outcomes when missing context is treated as hold.
- Strategy outcomes when missing context is treated as pass.
- Strategy outcomes by market regime.
- Strategy outcomes by instrument class.
- Strategy outcomes by catalyst class.

The ablation result should answer:

```text
Did Akber's filter improve hit rate, expectancy, drawdown, false-positive rate,
or learning value?
```

If the filter does not improve outcomes for a strategy family, QSASE should say
so. A human-style filter is only useful if it improves decision quality or
explains why a setup should be held.

### 12.9 Stage Contribution Scoring

The filter should score each stage separately.

Recommended stage scores:

- Catalyst quality score.
- Pricing gap score.
- Volatility setup score.
- Technical confirmation score.
- Volume or flow score.
- Risk/reward score.
- Invalidation clarity score.
- Missing-context penalty.
- False-positive reduction score.
- Drawdown reduction score.

The aggregate Akber score should not hide stage failure. A high catalyst score
with no invalidation logic should still be a hold or reject.

### 12.10 Veto Rules

The filter should be allowed to veto a machine-discovered strategy hypothesis.

Hard vetoes:

- No trusted catalyst.
- No clear invalidation.
- No paperable market expression.
- Source quorum too weak.
- Market already fully repriced.
- Volatility already exhausted.
- Risk/reward structurally poor.
- Duplicate exposure conflict.
- Missing required quantum review.
- Missing required technical or volume confirmation for that strategy type.
- Live-only expression.

Soft holds:

- Technical context missing.
- Volume context missing.
- Options context unavailable.
- Pricing gap not yet visible.
- Catalyst present but not time-sensitive.
- Route state not currently ready.

Vetoes should be recorded as learning evidence, not hidden as generic "no
trade" language.

### 12.11 Relationship To Universal Discovery

Akber's filter must not narrow the universal search too early.

The correct order is:

```text
universal source-price discovery
-> historical memory
-> linear and nonlinear testing
-> Strategy Foundry hypothesis
-> Akber filter
-> shadow/router review
```

If Akber's filter runs before universal discovery, Qadam may miss strange but
valuable cross-source patterns because they do not initially look like familiar
trade setups.

The filter should reject or hold strategy hypotheses after they become
explicit, not prevent the system from discovering them.

### 12.12 Relationship To Strategy Router

The Strategy Router should treat Akber filter output as a hard practical
quality input.

Router effects:

- `pass` can allow router review.
- `hold_missing_context` should route to watch or research.
- `hold_wait_for_confirmation` should route to watch.
- `reject` should block router promotion.
- `audit_only` should route to learning or dashboard context.

The router should not override an Akber hard veto. If a machine-discovered
pattern fails practical trading taste, it should not become a paper-review
candidate unless a future retest explicitly clears the veto.

### 12.13 Relationship To PaperOps

Akber's filter is upstream of PaperOps. It is not PaperOps.

It must not:

- Create trade candidates.
- Create risk approvals.
- Create execution approvals.
- Submit paper orders.
- Write broker records.
- Override Signal Integrity.
- Override Risk Agent.
- Override Execution Policy.
- Override idempotency.
- Override duplicate exposure checks.
- Override daily drawdown checks.
- Override Q-CTRL paper consultation holds.
- Grant paper proof ledger credit.

The strongest Akber result is:

```text
akber_filter_passed_for_router_review
```

not:

```text
paper_trade_approved
```

### 12.14 Dashboard Representation

The dashboard should show Akber's filter as a compact gate record.

Recommended fields:

- Filter status.
- Strategy hypothesis.
- Catalyst state.
- Pricing gap state.
- Volatility state.
- Technical confirmation state.
- Volume or flow state.
- Risk/reward state.
- Decision.
- Missing context.
- Next evidence needed.
- Historical ablation result.
- Authority state.

Recommended decision language:

```text
Akber filter: hold
Why: catalyst is strong, but volume confirmation and volatility context are missing.
Next evidence: volume confirmation
Authority: no trade candidate
```

This is specific enough for the Fund Manager and short enough for the
dashboard.

### 12.15 Telegram Representation

Telegram should receive only short, specific, review-only summaries.

Allowed message shape:

```text
Qadam strategy hold
Setup: Energy event probability lag
Reason: catalyst passed; volume and volatility context missing
Next: watch for confirmation
No paper order approved
```

Telegram must not include repetitive generic strategy prose, live instructions,
broker language, raw scores without context, or anything that implies command
authority.

### 12.16 Authority Boundary

Akber Filter Integration must never:

- Create Telegram commands.
- Create trade candidates directly.
- Create risk approvals.
- Create execution approvals.
- Stage paper orders.
- Submit paper orders.
- Write broker records.
- Call broker endpoints.
- Call quantum providers.
- Apply strategy mutations automatically.
- Enable live capital.
- Create paper proof ledger credit.
- Simulate elapsed time in the 30-day paper growth trial.

It may:

- Pass a strategy hypothesis to router review.
- Hold a hypothesis for more evidence.
- Reject a hypothesis.
- Produce ablation evidence.
- Improve strategy explanations.
- Improve future filter thresholds through proposal-only learning.

### 12.17 Build Contract

Implementation should add:

```text
orchestrator/qsase_akber_filter_integration.py
scripts/check_qsase_akber_filter_integration.py
```

The module should expose:

```text
build_akber_filter_results(settings)
load_akber_filter_results(settings)
validate_akber_filter_results(payload)
write_akber_filter_results(payload, settings)
run_akber_filter_ablation(results, historical_memory)
score_akber_filter_stage(stage, evidence)
```

The check script should validate:

- Strategy Foundry input exists.
- Internal six-stage Akber fields exist.
- Dashboard six-stage mapping exists.
- Each result has pass, hold, reject, or audit-only status.
- Missing context is explicit.
- Hard vetoes block router promotion.
- Ablation artifact is present or explicitly marked unavailable.
- Filter pass does not create trade candidates.
- Risk handoff is false.
- Execution authority is false.
- Paper order authority is false.
- Broker-write authority is false.
- Proof credit authority is false.
- Live capital is false.
- Telegram summary is review-only and command-disabled.
- Dashboard summary is public-safe and concise.

The first implementation should be deterministic. It should use available
market context, Signal Integrity Akber fields, Research Goal stage scoring, and
Strategy Foundry hypotheses before adding any more complex scoring.

### 12.18 Akber Filter Summary

Akber Filter Integration is QSASE's practical trading-taste gate.

It does not discover the universe. It does not create strategies. It does not
approve paper trades. It asks whether a Strategy Foundry hypothesis has enough
catalyst quality, pricing gap, volatility shape, technical confirmation,
volume or flow, invalidation clarity, and risk/reward to continue.

Its value is not just veto power. Its value is measurement. QSASE should learn
which Akber stages improve outcomes, which stages mostly create holds, and
which missing contexts are blocking useful paper setups. That makes Akber's
filter part of Qadam's learning system instead of a subjective afterthought.

## 13. Shadow Strategy Simulator

The Shadow Strategy Simulator is QSASE's what-would-have-happened laboratory.

It tests strategy hypotheses without creating orders.

It asks:

```text
If Qadam had watched, held, rejected, shadowed, or routed this hypothesis under
different evidence, model, quantum, Akber, or route conditions, what would have
happened?
```

This layer is essential because Qadam needs to learn from more than submitted
paper orders. It also needs to learn from rejected strategies, held strategies,
missed strategies, blocked routes, and counterfactual variants. But it must do
that without faking proof. Shadow replay can improve research. It cannot create
paper proof ledger credit.

### 13.1 Purpose

The purpose of the Shadow Strategy Simulator is to evaluate strategy
hypotheses before they reach the Strategy Router or PaperOps gate interface.

It should answer:

```text
Would this strategy hypothesis have improved historical outcomes?
Would Akber's filter have removed false positives?
Would a different source-trust setting have changed the decision?
Would a different model-weight setting have improved or worsened results?
Would quantum ambiguity holds have prevented bad trades?
Would route-readiness constraints have blocked most opportunities anyway?
Did actual paper trades outperform rejected or shadow alternatives?
Which strategy variants deserve router review?
Which variants should be rejected before PaperOps?
```

Shadow simulation is not a replacement for the 30-day paper growth trial. It is
a research and learning layer that helps Qadam decide what deserves real
paper-review attention later.

### 13.2 Inputs

The simulator should consume:

- Strategy Foundry hypotheses.
- Akber Filter results.
- Historical Source-Price Memory records.
- Linear Pattern Recognition Lab results.
- Nonlinear And Quantum Pattern Lab results.
- Quantum Pattern Review records.
- Historical backtest replay records.
- Forward shadow replay records.
- Actual paper order lifecycle records where available.
- Rejected strategy records.
- Strategy update proposals.
- Model weight proposals.
- Source trust score proposals.
- PaperOps route state.
- Paper proof ledger references for comparison only.

Inputs must be point-in-time safe. If a hypothesis, feature, or outcome uses
data that was not available at the decision timestamp, it cannot be used for a
valid shadow replay.

### 13.3 Output Artifacts

QSASE should create:

```text
data/runtime/qsase_shadow_strategy_simulator.json
data/runtime/qsase_shadow_strategy_results.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_shadow_strategy_rejections.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_shadow_strategy_simulator_history.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_shadow_strategy_simulator_events.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_shadow_strategy_variant_matrix.json
data/runtime/qsase_shadow_actual_vs_hypothetical.json
```

The summary artifact should expose:

```text
status
generated_at
input_hypothesis_count
variant_count
active_replay_count
blocked_replay_count
evaluated_replay_count
actual_vs_hypothetical_count
candidate_for_router_count
rejected_variant_count
blocked_reason
trade_candidate_created_count
paper_order_created_count
execution_intent_created_count
broker_write_count
proof_credit_allowed
live_capital_enabled
```

All action counters must remain zero. The simulator may produce research
records only.

### 13.4 Replay Record Contract

Each replay record should use a schema like:

```json
{
  "shadow_replay_id": "shadow-energy-event-probability-lag-baseline-001",
  "strategy_hypothesis_id": "qsase-strategy-energy-event-probability-lag-001",
  "replay_mode": "counterfactual_strategy_replay",
  "replay_state": "evaluated_research_only",
  "variant": {
    "variant_key": "akber_filter_strict",
    "variant_name": "Strict Akber Filter Variant",
    "variant_type": "akber_filter_variant",
    "changed_assumption": "missing volume context treated as hold instead of pass"
  },
  "time_window": {
    "start": "2023-01-03T14:30:00Z",
    "end": "2024-06-01T20:00:00Z",
    "decision_timestamps_point_in_time_safe": true
  },
  "source_refs": [
    "data/runtime/qsase_strategy_hypotheses.json",
    "data/runtime/qsase_akber_filter_integration.json",
    "data/runtime/qsase_historical_source_price_memory.json"
  ],
  "actual_decision": {
    "decision_type": "no_actual_paper_order",
    "paper_order_ref": null,
    "paper_lifecycle_ref": null
  },
  "hypothetical_decision": {
    "decision_type": "would_have_held",
    "reason": "Akber filter held for missing volume confirmation.",
    "would_have_created_trade_candidate": false,
    "would_have_created_paper_order": false
  },
  "outcome": {
    "hypothetical_return_5d": 0.014,
    "hypothetical_max_drawdown_5d": -0.021,
    "hypothetical_hit": true,
    "missed_opportunity": false,
    "avoided_false_positive": true
  },
  "comparison": {
    "baseline_variant_id": "shadow-energy-event-probability-lag-baseline-000",
    "delta_expectancy": 0.003,
    "delta_drawdown": 0.011,
    "delta_false_positive_rate": -0.08,
    "variant_added_value": true
  },
  "decision": {
    "shadow_status": "candidate_for_router_review",
    "reason": "Strict Akber hold reduced false positives and improved drawdown versus baseline.",
    "candidate_for_router": true,
    "candidate_for_paper_review": false
  },
  "authority": {
    "reference_only": true,
    "trade_candidate_created": false,
    "paper_order_created": false,
    "execution_intent_created": false,
    "broker_write_allowed": false,
    "proof_credit_allowed": false,
    "live_capital_enabled": false
  }
}
```

The `hypothetical_decision` field may describe what would have happened. It
must never become an instruction to act.

### 13.5 Replay Modes

The simulator should support several replay modes.

`historical_hypothesis_replay`:

```text
Tests a Strategy Foundry hypothesis across historical point-in-time memory.
```

`forward_shadow_replay`:

```text
Runs a strategy definition forward from a fixed definition date without broker
submission.
```

`counterfactual_strategy_replay`:

```text
Tests a changed strategy rule, filter, threshold, or routing assumption against
historical memory.
```

`actual_vs_hypothetical_replay`:

```text
Compares real guarded paper lifecycle outcomes against shadow alternatives and
rejected candidates.
```

`blocked_route_replay`:

```text
Measures whether a strategy would have been blocked by source quorum, Akber,
quantum ambiguity, duplicate exposure, drawdown, idempotency, or route state.
```

`missed_opportunity_replay`:

```text
Measures whether a held or rejected setup later moved favorably, while keeping
the result research-only.
```

Replay mode must be explicit on every record. A historical replay, forward
shadow replay, and real paper lifecycle are different evidence classes.

### 13.6 Variant Matrix

QSASE should test controlled variants rather than vague alternatives.

Recommended variant families:

- Baseline current strategy.
- Strategy Foundry hypothesis variant.
- Akber filter strictness variant.
- Akber missing-context policy variant.
- Source trust score variant.
- Source quorum threshold variant.
- Model weight variant.
- Frontier model challenge variant.
- Quantum ambiguity threshold variant.
- Strategy-collapse variant.
- Route-readiness variant.
- Idempotency hold variant.
- Duplicate exposure variant.
- Exit timing variant.
- Invalidation rule variant.
- Position sizing assumption for research only.

Each variant should state:

- What changed.
- Why it changed.
- Which artifact proposed the change.
- Whether the change is approved, proposed, or audit-only.
- Whether the replay is historical, forward, or counterfactual.
- Which authority flags remain false.

This prevents the simulator from becoming an uncontrolled model playground.

### 13.7 Actual Versus Hypothetical Comparison

The simulator should compare actual paper lifecycle outcomes against shadow
alternatives when real paper evidence exists.

It should compare:

- Submitted paper setups versus held alternatives.
- Submitted paper setups versus rejected alternatives.
- Actual exit timing versus shadow exit timing.
- Actual Akber filter state versus stricter or looser filter variants.
- Actual quantum ambiguity state versus alternative ambiguity thresholds.
- Actual source trust state versus proposed trust updates.
- Actual route state versus route-ready counterfactuals.

This comparison can support learning attribution. It cannot rewrite the paper
proof ledger. Only real guarded paper lifecycle evidence belongs in the paper
proof ledger.

### 13.8 Blocked Replay Behaviour

The simulator should degrade into blocked records when required evidence or
approval is missing.

Blocked states should include:

- `blocked_missing_strategy_hypothesis`
- `blocked_missing_historical_memory`
- `blocked_missing_akber_filter`
- `blocked_missing_quantum_review`
- `blocked_pending_learning_approval`
- `blocked_missing_paper_lifecycle_reference`
- `blocked_point_in_time_leakage`
- `blocked_authority_boundary_violation`
- `blocked_route_state_unavailable`

Blocked replay is still useful. It tells Qadam why it cannot evaluate a
strategy yet. The artifact should make this visible instead of silently
omitting the replay.

### 13.9 Scoring And Ranking

Shadow replay should score variants with transparent components.

Recommended components:

- Point-in-time safety.
- Sample size.
- Coverage quality.
- Expectancy.
- Hit rate.
- Maximum drawdown.
- False-positive reduction.
- Missed-opportunity reduction.
- Regime durability.
- Akber filter contribution.
- Quantum ambiguity contribution.
- Source trust contribution.
- Route feasibility.
- Learning value.

The simulator should not rank purely by return. A strategy that earns more
hypothetical return but creates worse drawdowns, poor route fit, or many false
positives should be downgraded.

### 13.10 Relationship To Strategy Router

The Strategy Router should consume shadow replay outputs after Akber filter
review.

The simulator may send the router:

- `candidate_for_router_review`
- `hold_for_more_shadow_data`
- `reject_after_shadow_replay`
- `watch_only`
- `audit_only`

It must not send:

- `paper_order_ready`
- `risk_approved`
- `execution_approved`
- `proof_credit_ready`

Router promotion should require shadow replay evidence only when the strategy
policy says shadow replay is required. Some low-confidence hypotheses may need
more shadow evidence before router review. Some strong hypotheses may route
with limited shadow data but must carry lower confidence.

### 13.11 Relationship To Learning And Attribution

Shadow replay is a major input to the Learning And Attribution Ledger.

It should help answer:

- Did the strategy fail because the source recipe was weak?
- Did Akber's filter prevent a bad trade?
- Did quantum ambiguity correctly identify fragility?
- Did the route block a trade that later would have worked?
- Did PaperOps correctly reject a duplicate exposure?
- Did a rejected candidate outperform the submitted paper order?
- Did model weights or source trust weights need proposal-only adjustment?

The simulator should produce evidence for learning proposals, not apply the
learning itself. Weight changes, threshold changes, source promotions, and
strategy mutations must remain proposal-first.

### 13.12 Relationship To Paper Proof Ledger

Shadow replay and paper proof ledger must remain separate.

Shadow replay can say:

```text
This strategy variant would have performed better or worse in replay.
```

It cannot say:

```text
Qadam earned proof credit for this replay.
```

Rules:

- No shadow result can create paper proof ledger credit.
- No simulated fill can enter the paper proof ledger.
- No historical replay can backfill the 30-day paper growth trial.
- No forward shadow replay can count as a submitted paper order.
- No actual-vs-hypothetical comparison can rewrite broker truth.
- Only real guarded paper lifecycle evidence can support paper proof ledger
  review.

This boundary must be visible in artifacts, dashboard summaries, and Telegram
summaries.

### 13.13 Dashboard Representation

The dashboard should show shadow replay as a compact comparison layer.

Recommended fields:

- Shadow simulator status.
- Variant count.
- Active replay count.
- Blocked replay count.
- Top variant.
- Top rejected variant.
- Actual-vs-hypothetical count.
- Candidate for router count.
- Latest blocked reason.
- Proof boundary.
- Authority state.

Recommended decision language:

```text
Shadow replay: candidate for router review
Why: strict Akber variant reduced false positives and drawdown.
Proof: research-only, no paper proof ledger credit
Authority: no order created
```

This makes replay useful without confusing it with real trading evidence.

### 13.14 Telegram Representation

Telegram summaries should be short, specific, and review-only.

Allowed message shape:

```text
Qadam shadow replay
Setup: Energy event probability lag
Result: strict Akber variant reduced false positives
Next: router review candidate
No order or proof credit
```

Telegram must not turn shadow replay into trading instruction, proof status,
or execution approval.

### 13.15 Authority Boundary

The Shadow Strategy Simulator must never:

- Create Telegram commands.
- Create trade candidates.
- Create risk approvals.
- Create execution approvals.
- Stage paper orders.
- Submit paper orders.
- Write broker records.
- Call broker endpoints.
- Call live endpoints.
- Mutate source trust scores.
- Mutate model weights.
- Mutate strategy thresholds.
- Mutate policies.
- Write to the paper proof ledger.
- Enable live capital.
- Grant proof credit.
- Simulate elapsed time in the 30-day paper growth trial.

It may:

- Evaluate historical variants.
- Evaluate forward shadow variants.
- Compare actual and hypothetical outcomes.
- Record blocked replay states.
- Produce router-review candidates.
- Produce learning evidence.
- Produce proposal-only improvement signals.
- Improve dashboard explanations.

### 13.16 Build Contract

Implementation should add:

```text
orchestrator/qsase_shadow_strategy_simulator.py
scripts/check_qsase_shadow_strategy_simulator.py
```

The module should expose:

```text
build_shadow_strategy_replay(settings)
load_shadow_strategy_replay(settings)
validate_shadow_strategy_replay(payload)
write_shadow_strategy_replay(payload, settings)
build_shadow_variant_matrix(settings)
compare_actual_vs_hypothetical(replay_records)
score_shadow_variants(replay_records)
```

The check script should validate:

- Strategy Foundry input exists.
- Akber Filter input exists.
- Historical Source-Price Memory input exists.
- Replay mode is explicit.
- Replay evidence class is explicit.
- Point-in-time leakage is rejected.
- Blocked replays are preserved.
- Actual-vs-hypothetical records do not mutate actual paper lifecycle records.
- No trade candidates are created.
- No paper orders are created.
- No execution intents are created.
- No broker writes occur.
- No source, model, strategy, or policy mutations occur.
- Proof credit is false.
- Live capital is false.
- Dashboard summary is public-safe.
- Telegram summary is review-only and command-disabled.

The first implementation should extend the existing Phase 6 shadow strategy
runner contract. It should preserve the blocked/no-op behavior when approved
learning evidence is missing, then add QSASE-specific variant records as
research-only replay surfaces.

### 13.17 Shadow Strategy Simulator Summary

The Shadow Strategy Simulator lets QSASE learn without pretending to trade.

It compares strategy hypotheses, rejected alternatives, Akber variants, source
trust variants, model-weight variants, quantum ambiguity variants, and route
conditions against historical and forward replay. It helps Qadam decide what
deserves router review and what should be rejected.

Its discipline is the boundary: shadow replay is not paper proof. It can make
Qadam smarter, but it cannot create orders, broker writes, live capital, or
paper proof ledger credit.

## 14. Strategy Router

The Strategy Router is QSASE's decision layer.

It decides the next allowed action for each strategy hypothesis after Strategy
Foundry, Akber Filter, and Shadow Strategy Simulator review.

It asks:

```text
Given Qadam's current self-model, evidence, stack state, route readiness, and
safety boundaries, what is the correct next state for this strategy?
```

The router is not an execution engine. It does not create orders. It does not
approve risk. It does not bypass PaperOps. It only chooses the next strategy
state: watch, research, hold, reject, shadow replay, Strategy Foundry review,
or paper-review candidate.

### 14.1 Purpose

The purpose of the Strategy Router is to rank and route both inherited strategy
families and newly discovered strategy hypotheses.

It should answer:

```text
Which strategy deserves attention now?
Which strategy should stay under watch?
Which strategy needs more research?
Which strategy should be held for missing evidence?
Which strategy should be rejected?
Which strategy needs more shadow replay?
Which strategy should go back to Strategy Foundry?
Which strategy is eligible for PaperOps gate-interface review?
Why is Qadam not trading this strategy now?
```

The router should be deterministic enough to audit but flexible enough to
handle new strategy families produced by QSASE.

### 14.2 Inputs

The Strategy Router should consume:

- QSASE Self-Model.
- Strategy Foundry hypotheses.
- Strategy Foundry rejections.
- Akber Filter results.
- Shadow Strategy Simulator results.
- Linear Pattern Recognition Lab results.
- Nonlinear And Quantum Pattern Lab results.
- Quantum Pattern Review records.
- Historical Source-Price Memory summaries.
- Source health and source quorum state.
- Trading universe route readiness.
- PaperOps route state.
- Open orders and open positions.
- Duplicate exposure state.
- Daily drawdown state.
- Risk budget state.
- Idempotency state.
- Paper proof ledger references for context only.
- Existing Phase 4 strategy-family universe.
- Existing Strategy Update Record proposals.

The router must reject stale or missing inputs explicitly. It should not route
from silent defaults.

### 14.3 Output Artifacts

QSASE should create:

```text
data/runtime/qsase_strategy_router_decisions.json
data/runtime/qsase_strategy_router_decisions.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_strategy_router_decisions_history.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_strategy_router_decisions_events.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_strategy_router_scoreboard.json
```

The summary artifact should expose:

```text
status
generated_at
strategy_input_count
watch_count
research_count
hold_count
reject_count
shadow_replay_count
strategy_foundry_review_count
paper_review_candidate_count
hard_veto_count
top_ranked_strategy
why_not_trading_now
execution_allowed
paper_order_allowed
broker_write_allowed
proof_credit_allowed
live_capital_enabled
```

All authority fields remain false. A paper-review candidate is a candidate for
the PaperOps Gate Interface, not an approved paper order.

### 14.4 Router Decision Contract

Each router decision should use a schema like:

```json
{
  "router_decision_id": "qsase-router-energy-event-probability-lag-001",
  "strategy_hypothesis_id": "qsase-strategy-energy-event-probability-lag-001",
  "strategy_family": {
    "type": "new_strategy_family_candidate",
    "primary_family": "energy_event_probability_lag",
    "secondary_families": [
      "crude_oil_energy_security_disruption",
      "prediction_market_geopolitical_dislocation"
    ]
  },
  "scores": {
    "market_edge_score": 0.64,
    "stack_fitness_score": 0.72,
    "route_readiness_score": 0.48,
    "learning_value_score": 0.81,
    "risk_cleanliness_score": 0.55,
    "router_total_score": 0.64
  },
  "gates": {
    "source_quorum": "pass",
    "catalyst": "pass",
    "akber_filter": "hold_wait_for_confirmation",
    "linear_review": "pass",
    "nonlinear_review": "pass",
    "quantum_review": "pass_for_research",
    "shadow_replay": "candidate_for_router_review",
    "duplicate_exposure": "pass",
    "daily_drawdown": "pass",
    "risk_budget": "not_yet_reviewed",
    "paper_route": "not_yet_reviewed"
  },
  "hard_vetoes": [],
  "soft_blockers": [
    "akber_filter_hold_wait_for_confirmation",
    "paper_route_not_yet_reviewed"
  ],
  "decision": {
    "router_output": "hold",
    "reason": "Evidence is strong, but Akber filter is waiting for confirmation and PaperOps gate review has not started.",
    "next_required_action": "watch_for_confirmation",
    "paper_review_candidate": false,
    "why_not_trading_now": "Akber confirmation hold; not yet eligible for PaperOps gate interface."
  },
  "lineage": {
    "foundry_ref": "data/runtime/qsase_strategy_hypotheses.json",
    "akber_ref": "data/runtime/qsase_akber_filter_integration.json",
    "shadow_ref": "data/runtime/qsase_shadow_strategy_simulator.json",
    "self_model_ref": "data/runtime/qsase_self_model.json"
  },
  "authority": {
    "trade_candidate_created": false,
    "risk_handoff_allowed": false,
    "execution_allowed": false,
    "paper_order_allowed": false,
    "broker_write_allowed": false,
    "proof_credit_allowed": false,
    "live_capital_enabled": false
  }
}
```

The router decision must always include a reason and a `why_not_trading_now`
field. A blank or generic hold reason is not acceptable.

### 14.5 Router Outputs

The router should output one of these states.

`watch`:

```text
The pattern is relevant but not ready for action. Continue monitoring.
```

`research`:

```text
More source, model, historical, or market-context work is required.
```

`hold`:

```text
The strategy is potentially useful, but a required confirmation or route
condition is missing.
```

`reject`:

```text
The strategy fails evidence, safety, explainability, route fit, or practical
trade quality.
```

`shadow_replay`:

```text
The strategy needs additional historical or forward shadow evidence before the
router can decide.
```

`strategy_foundry_review`:

```text
The strategy needs to be remapped, merged, split, renamed, or revised before it
can route cleanly.
```

`paper_review_candidate`:

```text
The strategy is eligible to be handed to the PaperOps Gate Interface for
review by the existing guarded paper route.
```

`paper_review_candidate` is the strongest router output, but it is still not a
paper order.

### 14.6 Scoring Model

The router should use four primary scores.

`market_edge_score`:

```text
How strong is the source-price evidence after historical, linear, nonlinear,
quantum, Akber, and shadow review?
```

`stack_fitness_score`:

```text
How well does this strategy fit Qadam's actual architecture: source coverage,
local model strengths, frontier model review, quantum review, and Python
orchestration?
```

`route_readiness_score`:

```text
How close is the strategy to being safely expressible through the guarded paper
route?
```

`learning_value_score`:

```text
How much would Qadam learn by watching, shadowing, rejecting, or paper-reviewing
this strategy?
```

Recommended secondary scores:

- Risk cleanliness.
- Drawdown tolerance.
- Exposure cleanliness.
- Source freshness.
- Evidence diversity.
- Akber stage quality.
- Quantum ambiguity tolerance.
- Shadow replay support.
- Paper proof ledger relevance.
- Dashboard explainability.

The router should not use a single opaque score. It should expose component
scores so the Fund Manager can see why a strategy was routed.

### 14.7 Hard Vetoes

Hard vetoes must block paper-review candidacy.

Hard vetoes include:

- Failed source quorum.
- Missing trusted catalyst.
- Failed Akber filter.
- Missing quantum review where required.
- Quantum ambiguity hold where required.
- Point-in-time leakage.
- Stale source data.
- Unavailable paper route.
- Duplicate exposure conflict.
- Daily drawdown breach.
- Risk budget breach.
- Open order conflict.
- Open position conflict.
- Idempotency conflict.
- No clean invalidation.
- No paperable market expression.
- Live-only expression.
- Broker-write route required.
- Live-capital route required.
- Telegram-originated command path.

When a hard veto exists, the router may still output watch, research, hold,
shadow replay, foundry review, or reject. It cannot output
`paper_review_candidate`.

### 14.8 Soft Blockers

Soft blockers should prevent premature routing but may be cleared later.

Soft blockers include:

- Technical confirmation missing.
- Volume confirmation missing.
- Options context unavailable.
- Pricing gap not yet visible.
- Historical coverage thin.
- Shadow replay incomplete.
- Strategy family mapping uncertain.
- Source trust degraded but not failed.
- Market session closed.
- Route status temporarily stale.
- Paper account mirror stale.

Soft blockers should produce a precise next action. For example:

```text
Hold for volume confirmation.
Refresh paper account mirror.
Run shadow replay.
Return to Strategy Foundry for family collapse review.
```

### 14.9 Multiple Qualified Strategies

The router must support more than one qualified strategy in a day.

The daily paper-trade target is a discipline target, not a hard ceiling. If
multiple distinct strategy hypotheses become paper-review candidates on the
same day, the router may emit multiple paper-review candidates only when each
one has:

- Distinct Research Goal lineage.
- Distinct candidate identity.
- Distinct idempotency key.
- Distinct exposure profile.
- Passing source quorum.
- Passing Akber filter.
- Required quantum review complete.
- Passing risk-budget precheck.
- No duplicate exposure conflict.
- No daily drawdown breach.
- No live-capital route.
- No broker-write bypass.

The router must also support zero qualified strategies. If nothing qualifies,
the correct output is a precise hold or no-trade reason, not a forced setup.

### 14.10 Family Selection And Strategy Fit

The router should assign each strategy to the right family before later review.

It should output:

- Primary strategy family.
- Secondary strategy family, if any.
- Why the primary family fits.
- Why other families were rejected.
- Whether Strategy Foundry should merge or split the family.
- Whether a new strategy family remains provisional.

This prevents Qadam from treating every opportunity as the same generic
candidate. A semiconductor policy setup, an energy chokepoint setup, a
prediction-market probability gap, and a post-earnings drift setup need
different evidence, timing, invalidation, and risk expectations.

### 14.11 Relationship To PaperOps Gate Interface

The Strategy Router may hand off only `paper_review_candidate` records to the
PaperOps Gate Interface.

The handoff must include:

- Router decision id.
- Strategy hypothesis id.
- Research Goal lineage.
- Candidate identity.
- Proposed strategy family.
- Evidence summary.
- Required gates.
- Current blockers cleared.
- Known residual risks.
- Idempotency seed.
- Exposure summary.
- Why-not-trading-now if not eligible.

The router must not call PaperOps directly. It must not submit orders. It must
not skip Signal Integrity, Strategy Lead review, Head of Quant review, Risk
Agent sizing, Execution Policy, idempotency, broker readiness, or guarded
Alpaca Paper submission.

### 14.12 Relationship To Self-Healing

The router should distinguish market reasons from system defects.

Market reasons:

- No catalyst.
- No confirmation.
- Market already repriced.
- Strategy rejected.
- Risk/reward poor.

System defects:

- Missing artifact.
- Stale source snapshot.
- Failed paper account mirror.
- Missing Akber filter result.
- Missing quantum review.
- Broken route check.
- Validation error.

If the router detects a system defect, it should mark:

```text
self_healing_repair_requested
```

and write a repair request for automation follow-up. It should not hide the
defect as a no-trade market reason.

### 14.13 Dashboard Representation

The dashboard should show router decisions as decision records.

Recommended fields:

- Router status.
- Top strategy.
- Router output.
- Why now or why not now.
- Hard vetoes.
- Soft blockers.
- Score components.
- Paper-review candidate count.
- Rejected strategy count.
- Shadow replay required count.
- Next required action.
- Authority state.

Recommended decision language:

```text
Router decision: hold
Strategy: Energy event probability lag
Why: Akber confirmation hold; PaperOps gate not reached
Next: watch for volume confirmation
Authority: no paper order
```

This keeps the dashboard specific and avoids generic "strategy engine active"
copy.

### 14.14 Telegram Representation

Telegram router summaries should be short and non-commanding.

Allowed message shape:

```text
Qadam router hold
Strategy: Energy event probability lag
Reason: Akber confirmation missing
Next: watch, no paper order
Dashboard: qadam.trade/dashboard/
```

Telegram must not present router output as trading instruction, order approval,
risk approval, or live-capital authority.

### 14.15 Authority Boundary

The Strategy Router must never:

- Create Telegram commands.
- Create trade candidates directly.
- Create risk approvals.
- Create execution approvals.
- Stage paper orders.
- Submit paper orders.
- Write broker records.
- Call broker endpoints.
- Call live endpoints.
- Bypass PaperOps.
- Bypass Q-CTRL review where required.
- Override idempotency.
- Override duplicate exposure checks.
- Override daily drawdown checks.
- Override risk budget.
- Enable live capital.
- Grant paper proof ledger credit.
- Simulate elapsed time in the 30-day paper growth trial.

It may:

- Rank strategy hypotheses.
- Route strategies to watch, research, hold, reject, shadow replay, foundry
  review, or paper-review candidate.
- Emit paper-review candidate handoff records for the PaperOps Gate Interface.
- Emit self-healing repair requests for missing or broken artifacts.
- Explain why Qadam is not trading now.
- Improve dashboard and Telegram strategy clarity.

### 14.16 Build Contract

Implementation should add:

```text
orchestrator/qsase_strategy_router.py
scripts/check_qsase_strategy_router.py
```

The module should expose:

```text
build_strategy_router_decisions(settings)
load_strategy_router_decisions(settings)
validate_strategy_router_decisions(payload)
write_strategy_router_decisions(payload, settings)
score_strategy_for_router(strategy, context)
apply_router_vetoes(strategy, context)
build_paper_review_candidate_handoff(decision)
```

The check script should validate:

- QSASE Self-Model input exists.
- Strategy Foundry input exists.
- Akber Filter input exists.
- Shadow Strategy Simulator input exists or is explicitly not required.
- Every decision has a router output.
- Every decision has a reason.
- Every decision has `why_not_trading_now` or paper-review candidate rationale.
- Hard vetoes block paper-review candidacy.
- Multiple paper-review candidates require distinct lineage and idempotency.
- No trade candidates are created.
- No risk handoff is created.
- No execution authority is created.
- No paper order authority is created.
- No broker-write authority is created.
- No proof credit is granted.
- Live capital is false.
- Dashboard summary is public-safe.
- Telegram summary is short and command-disabled.

The first implementation should route a small number of strategy hypotheses
with explicit scoring and veto records. It should prefer precise holds and
rejections over vague promotion.

### 14.17 Strategy Router Summary

The Strategy Router is QSASE's next-action decision layer.

It converts strategy intelligence into an allowed next state without becoming
an execution engine. It ranks strategies by market edge, stack fitness, route
readiness, risk cleanliness, and learning value. It enforces hard vetoes. It
supports multiple distinct paper-review candidates when genuinely qualified,
and it supports zero trades when no setup qualifies.

Its most important output is clarity: Qadam should always be able to say what
strategy it is applying, why that strategy fits, what action is allowed next,
and why it is or is not trading now.

## 15. PaperOps Gate Interface

The PaperOps Gate Interface is the boundary between QSASE strategy
intelligence and Qadam's existing guarded paper-operation route.

It asks:

```text
Is this router-approved strategy hypothesis eligible to be reviewed by
PaperOps, without QSASE creating a qualified setup, paper order, broker write,
or proof credit by itself?
```

This interface exists because QSASE will become powerful enough to discover,
rank, and route strategy hypotheses. That does not mean it should be allowed to
submit orders. Paper execution remains the responsibility of the existing
guarded PaperOps chain.

### 15.1 Purpose

The purpose of the PaperOps Gate Interface is to convert a router output into a
controlled paper-review handoff record.

It should answer:

```text
Did the Strategy Router output paper-review candidate?
Is the Research Goal lineage distinct?
Is the candidate identity distinct?
Is the idempotency seed distinct?
Is source quorum present?
Is Akber filter passed?
Is required quantum review complete?
Is the paper route available?
Is there a duplicate exposure conflict?
Is there a daily drawdown breach?
Is the paper account mirror fresh?
Is the setup eligible for existing PaperOps review?
If not, exactly why not?
```

The interface does not decide that a trade should be submitted. It decides
whether a QSASE strategy candidate is structured enough to enter PaperOps
review.

### 15.2 Inputs

The interface should consume:

- Strategy Router decisions.
- Strategy Foundry hypotheses.
- Akber Filter results.
- Shadow Strategy Simulator results.
- QSASE Self-Model.
- Historical Source-Price Memory summaries.
- Source health and source-quorum state.
- Quantum Pattern Review records.
- PaperOps route state.
- Paper account mirror state.
- Open orders.
- Open positions.
- Duplicate exposure state.
- Daily drawdown state.
- Risk budget state.
- Idempotency ledger state.
- Q-CTRL paper consultation state.
- Existing guarded Alpaca Paper submit-readiness state.

The interface should reject any router decision that lacks a complete lineage
chain. A strategy without lineage is not paper-review eligible.

### 15.3 Output Artifacts

QSASE should create:

```text
data/runtime/qsase_paperops_gate_interface.json
data/runtime/qsase_paperops_gate_interface.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_paperops_gate_interface_history.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_paperops_gate_interface_events.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_paperops_handoff_records.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_paperops_rejected_handoffs.jsonl
```

The summary artifact should expose:

```text
status
generated_at
router_candidate_count
handoff_record_count
eligible_for_paperops_review_count
held_handoff_count
rejected_handoff_count
duplicate_idempotency_count
duplicate_exposure_count
source_quorum_block_count
drawdown_block_count
qctrl_hold_count
paper_route_unavailable_count
paper_order_created_count
broker_write_count
proof_credit_allowed
live_capital_enabled
```

`paper_order_created_count`, `broker_write_count`, `proof_credit_allowed`, and
`live_capital_enabled` must remain zero or false.

### 15.4 Handoff Record Contract

Each handoff record should use a schema like:

```json
{
  "paperops_handoff_id": "qsase-paperops-handoff-energy-event-probability-lag-001",
  "status": "eligible_for_existing_paperops_review",
  "router_decision_id": "qsase-router-energy-event-probability-lag-001",
  "strategy_hypothesis_id": "qsase-strategy-energy-event-probability-lag-001",
  "research_goal_lineage": {
    "research_goal_id": "rg-energy-event-probability-lag-001",
    "source_pattern_ids": [
      "pattern-acled-kalshi-xle-5d-001"
    ],
    "foundry_ref": "data/runtime/qsase_strategy_hypotheses.json",
    "router_ref": "data/runtime/qsase_strategy_router_decisions.json"
  },
  "candidate_identity": {
    "candidate_id": "qsase-candidate-energy-event-probability-lag-xle-20240601",
    "candidate_identity_hash": "sha256-example",
    "strategy_family": "energy_event_probability_lag",
    "instrument": "XLE",
    "direction": "long",
    "time_horizon": "3d_to_10d",
    "invalidation_summary": "odds reversal, failed market confirmation, or unsafe route state"
  },
  "idempotency": {
    "idempotency_namespace": "qsase_paperops_review",
    "idempotency_seed": "rg-energy-event-probability-lag-001:XLE:2024-06-01",
    "idempotency_key": "qsase-paperops-review-sha256-example",
    "duplicate_idempotency_detected": false
  },
  "gate_state": {
    "router_output": "paper_review_candidate",
    "source_quorum": "pass",
    "akber_filter": "pass",
    "quantum_review": "pass_for_research",
    "shadow_replay": "candidate_for_router_review",
    "duplicate_exposure": "pass",
    "daily_drawdown": "pass",
    "risk_budget_precheck": "pass",
    "paper_account_mirror": "fresh",
    "qctrl_paper_consultation": "not_bypassed",
    "guarded_alpaca_paper_route": "available_for_review"
  },
  "decision": {
    "paperops_gate_output": "eligible_for_existing_paperops_review",
    "reason": "Router candidate has distinct lineage, passing source quorum, passing Akber filter, no duplicate exposure, and no drawdown breach.",
    "next_required_system": "existing_guarded_paperops_route",
    "paper_order_ready": false,
    "qualified_setup_created": false
  },
  "authority": {
    "trade_candidate_created": false,
    "qualified_setup_created": false,
    "risk_approval_created": false,
    "execution_approval_created": false,
    "paper_order_created": false,
    "broker_write_allowed": false,
    "proof_credit_allowed": false,
    "live_capital_enabled": false
  }
}
```

The handoff record can be read by later PaperOps checks. It must not itself
become a submit record.

### 15.5 Candidate States

The interface should output one of these states.

`eligible_for_existing_paperops_review`:

```text
The record is complete enough to be reviewed by the existing guarded PaperOps
chain.
```

`hold_missing_context`:

```text
The router candidate is plausible, but required context is missing.
```

`hold_route_unavailable`:

```text
The strategy may be valid, but the paper route, paper account mirror, market
session, or provider state is not ready.
```

`rejected_before_paperops`:

```text
The candidate fails a hard gate and should not enter PaperOps review.
```

`repair_requested`:

```text
A system defect prevents evaluation, so self-healing should queue repair.
```

The interface should prefer precise holds over vague failure states.

### 15.6 Eligibility Checks

Minimum eligibility checks:

- Router output is `paper_review_candidate`.
- Strategy hypothesis lineage exists.
- Research Goal lineage exists.
- Candidate identity exists.
- Candidate identity hash is stable.
- Idempotency namespace exists.
- Idempotency seed exists.
- Duplicate idempotency check passes.
- Source quorum passes.
- Akber filter passes.
- Required quantum review is complete.
- Shadow replay requirement is satisfied or explicitly waived by policy.
- Paper account mirror is fresh.
- Market expression is paperable.
- No duplicate exposure conflict exists.
- No open order conflict exists.
- No daily drawdown breach exists.
- Risk-budget precheck passes.
- Q-CTRL paper consultation hold is not bypassed.
- Guarded Alpaca Paper route is the only possible submit path.
- Live capital is false.

If any check fails, the interface must record the exact failure reason.

### 15.7 Lineage And Idempotency

The interface must preserve three distinct identities.

Research Goal lineage:

```text
Why this setup exists and which source-price pattern created it.
```

Candidate identity:

```text
What exact setup is being reviewed: strategy family, instrument, direction,
catalyst, horizon, invalidation, and evidence bundle.
```

Idempotency key:

```text
The duplicate-submit guard that prevents the same setup from being submitted
twice through PaperOps.
```

These identities must not be collapsed into one generic strategy id. Two
energy setups may share a family but differ by catalyst, source quorum,
instrument, timing, or invalidation. The interface should allow distinct
qualified opportunities to progress while blocking true duplicates.

### 15.8 Multiple Same-Day Handoffs

The interface must support multiple same-day handoffs when setups are truly
distinct.

Multiple handoffs are allowed only when each has:

- Distinct Research Goal lineage.
- Distinct candidate identity.
- Distinct idempotency key.
- Distinct source evidence.
- Distinct exposure profile or non-conflicting exposure.
- Passing source quorum.
- Passing Akber filter.
- Required quantum review complete.
- Passing risk-budget precheck.
- No daily drawdown breach.
- No duplicate exposure conflict.
- No live-capital route.

This preserves the rule that the daily paper-trade target is a minimum
discipline target, not a hard ceiling, while still blocking duplicate or forced
trading.

### 15.9 Hold And Rejection Reasons

Common hold reasons:

- `missing_research_goal_lineage`
- `missing_candidate_identity`
- `missing_idempotency_seed`
- `paper_account_mirror_stale`
- `market_session_closed`
- `qctrl_paper_consultation_hold`
- `risk_budget_precheck_pending`
- `shadow_replay_pending`
- `route_status_stale`

Common rejection reasons:

- `router_output_not_paper_review_candidate`
- `source_quorum_failed`
- `akber_filter_failed`
- `quantum_review_required_but_missing`
- `duplicate_idempotency_key`
- `duplicate_exposure_conflict`
- `daily_drawdown_breach`
- `risk_budget_breach`
- `live_capital_route_detected`
- `broker_bypass_detected`
- `non_paperable_market_expression`
- `telegram_command_origin_detected`

The rejection ledger should be useful for later learning. A rejected handoff
should explain whether the issue was strategy quality, route state, risk, or
system defect.

### 15.10 Relationship To Existing PaperOps

The interface should hand off to existing PaperOps, not replace it.

Existing PaperOps remains responsible for:

- Qualified setup production.
- Signal Integrity.
- Strategy Lead review.
- Head of Quant review where required.
- Risk Agent sizing.
- Execution Policy.
- Kill-switch checks.
- Q-CTRL paper consultation holds.
- Event Log prewrite.
- Idempotency ledger enforcement.
- Broker-readiness checks.
- Guarded Alpaca Paper submit path.
- Paper lifecycle polling.
- Paper proof ledger review.

QSASE must not duplicate those responsibilities. It produces an upstream
handoff record and then lets the guarded PaperOps chain decide what happens
next.

### 15.11 Guarded Alpaca Paper Route

The interface must name the guarded route explicitly:

```text
existing guarded Alpaca Paper route only
```

It must reject any route that implies:

- Broker live endpoint.
- Live credentials.
- Prediction-market write.
- Crypto-perps write.
- Manual trade-level override.
- Telegram command route.
- LLM direct-order route.
- Quantum direct-order route.
- Any submit path outside PaperOps.

Even when the guarded Alpaca Paper route is available, QSASE still cannot call
it. Only the existing PaperOps submit stage may do that under its own explicit
submit flag and preconditions.

### 15.12 Calendar And Proof Boundary

The PaperOps Gate Interface must preserve the real paper calendar.

Rules:

- No backfilled handoff can count as a current paper setup.
- No historical backtest can create a PaperOps handoff.
- No shadow replay can create paper proof ledger credit.
- No simulated elapsed time can advance the 30-day paper growth trial.
- No simulated fill can enter the paper proof ledger.
- Only real guarded paper lifecycle evidence can support paper proof ledger
  review.

The interface may reference historical and shadow evidence as rationale. It
may not convert that evidence into proof.

### 15.13 Relationship To Self-Healing

The interface should distinguish a valid no-trade state from a system defect.

Valid no-trade or hold states:

- No paper-review candidate.
- Source quorum failed.
- Akber filter held.
- Market closed.
- Duplicate exposure conflict.
- Drawdown breach.

System defects:

- Missing route artifact.
- Broken idempotency ledger.
- Stale paper account mirror.
- Missing Q-CTRL state.
- Missing open-order mirror.
- Validation error.
- Contradictory live-capital flag.

When the problem is a system defect, the interface should write:

```text
self_healing_repair_requested
```

and include the failed artifact or check name. It should not hide an
infrastructure failure as "no setup."

### 15.14 Dashboard Representation

The dashboard should show the interface as a compact handoff gate.

Recommended fields:

- PaperOps gate status.
- Router candidate count.
- Eligible handoff count.
- Held handoff count.
- Rejected handoff count.
- Top handoff candidate.
- Blocking gate.
- Idempotency state.
- Duplicate exposure state.
- Drawdown state.
- Q-CTRL paper consultation state.
- Guarded Alpaca Paper route state.
- Proof boundary.
- Authority state.

Recommended decision language:

```text
PaperOps gate: held
Strategy: Energy event probability lag
Reason: paper account mirror stale
Next: refresh mirror, then recheck
Authority: no order submitted
```

This should be visible enough that users understand why Qadam is waiting, but
short enough to avoid dashboard slop.

### 15.15 Telegram Representation

Telegram summaries should be short, specific, and review-only.

Allowed message shape:

```text
Qadam PaperOps gate hold
Setup: Energy event probability lag
Reason: duplicate exposure conflict
Next: no paper handoff
No order submitted
```

Telegram must not contain operational commands, approval language, broker
instructions, live-capital references, or repetitive generic status text.

### 15.16 Authority Boundary

The PaperOps Gate Interface must never:

- Create Telegram commands.
- Create qualified setups by itself.
- Create trade candidates directly.
- Create risk approvals.
- Create execution approvals.
- Stage paper orders.
- Submit paper orders.
- Write broker records.
- Call broker endpoints.
- Call live endpoints.
- Bypass Signal Integrity.
- Bypass Strategy Lead review.
- Bypass Head of Quant review where required.
- Bypass Risk Agent sizing.
- Bypass Execution Policy.
- Bypass Q-CTRL paper consultation holds.
- Bypass idempotency.
- Bypass duplicate exposure checks.
- Bypass daily drawdown checks.
- Enable live capital.
- Grant paper proof ledger credit.
- Simulate elapsed time in the 30-day paper growth trial.

It may:

- Record paper-review candidate handoffs.
- Hold or reject router candidates before PaperOps.
- Preserve lineage and idempotency material.
- Emit self-healing repair requests.
- Improve dashboard and Telegram visibility.
- Provide existing PaperOps with a clean upstream context packet.

### 15.17 Build Contract

Implementation should add:

```text
orchestrator/qsase_paperops_gate_interface.py
scripts/check_qsase_paperops_gate_interface.py
```

The module should expose:

```text
build_paperops_gate_interface(settings)
load_paperops_gate_interface(settings)
validate_paperops_gate_interface(payload)
write_paperops_gate_interface(payload, settings)
build_paperops_handoff_records(router_decisions, context)
validate_paperops_handoff_record(record)
reject_unfit_paperops_handoffs(records, context)
```

The check script should validate:

- Strategy Router input exists.
- QSASE Self-Model input exists.
- PaperOps route state input exists.
- Paper account mirror input exists.
- Every handoff has Research Goal lineage.
- Every handoff has candidate identity.
- Every handoff has idempotency material.
- Duplicate idempotency is rejected.
- Duplicate exposure is rejected.
- Source quorum failure is rejected.
- Akber failure is rejected.
- Missing required quantum review is rejected.
- Daily drawdown breach is rejected.
- Live-capital route is rejected.
- Broker-bypass route is rejected.
- No qualified setup is created by this interface.
- No trade candidate is created by this interface.
- No paper order is created.
- No broker write occurs.
- Proof credit is false.
- Live capital is false.
- Dashboard summary is public-safe.
- Telegram summary is short and command-disabled.

The first implementation should be conservative. It should produce handoff,
hold, and rejection records before any downstream PaperOps module consumes the
handoff automatically.

### 15.18 PaperOps Gate Interface Summary

The PaperOps Gate Interface is the safety boundary between QSASE intelligence
and paper execution.

It lets Qadam pass only clean, distinct, lineage-backed paper-review candidates
to the existing guarded PaperOps chain. It preserves Research Goal lineage,
candidate identity, idempotency, source quorum, Akber, quantum review, risk
precheck, duplicate exposure, drawdown, and route state before any PaperOps
review begins.

Most importantly, it keeps the roles separate. QSASE can discover and route.
PaperOps can review and, only through the existing guarded Alpaca Paper route,
submit paper orders when all its own gates pass. The interface cannot create
orders, cannot bypass gates, cannot enable live capital, and cannot grant paper
proof ledger credit.

## 16. Learning And Attribution Ledger

The Learning And Attribution Ledger is QSASE's memory of why decisions worked,
why they failed, and which component deserves credit or blame.

It asks:

```text
When Qadam made, held, rejected, shadow-tested, or missed a strategy decision,
which source, model, filter, gate, route, or market condition actually caused
the outcome?
```

This ledger is the bridge between execution evidence and recursive
improvement. It must be strong enough to teach QSASE, but constrained enough
that learning cannot silently mutate production behavior.

### 16.1 Purpose

The purpose of the Learning And Attribution Ledger is to turn outcomes into
auditable learning records.

It should answer:

```text
Did the strategy work?
Did it work for the reason QSASE expected?
Did the source pattern persist after the decision?
Did the local model help or compress away useful detail?
Did the frontier model add useful challenge or add narrative noise?
Did the quantum review surface a nonlinear relationship or merely confirm
ambiguity?
Did Akber's filter improve selectivity or block a valid setup?
Did the router choose the right state?
Did PaperOps block a bad setup or expose a route defect?
Did the outcome come from true edge, regime luck, source noise, execution luck,
or overfitting?
What should Qadam consider changing next?
```

The ledger is not a reward machine that blindly increases weights after a win
and decreases them after a loss. It must preserve causal humility. A profitable
paper trade can be lucky. A losing paper trade can be structurally correct. A
non-trade can be the best decision of the day. A rejected hypothesis can still
contain a useful partial pattern.

### 16.2 Learning Inputs

The ledger should consume:

- Paper proof ledger references.
- Paper lifecycle records.
- Submitted paper order records.
- Open and closed paper positions.
- Paper trade postmortems.
- Non-trade decisions.
- Why-not-trading-now records.
- PaperOps Gate Interface handoffs.
- PaperOps Gate Interface holds and rejections.
- Strategy Router decisions.
- Strategy Foundry hypotheses.
- Akber Filter results.
- Shadow Strategy Simulator results.
- Historical Source-Price Memory records.
- Universal Source-Price Pattern Matrix records.
- Full-Universe Pattern Search records.
- Linear Pattern Recognition Lab results.
- Nonlinear And Quantum Pattern Lab results.
- Quantum Pattern Review records.
- Source health records.
- Source quorum records.
- Model agreement and disagreement records.
- Local Gemma triage records.
- Frontier Gemini critique records.
- Strategy Lead considerations.
- Signal Integrity state.
- Risk Agent state.
- Execution Policy state.
- Duplicate exposure state.
- Idempotency ledger state.
- Daily drawdown state.
- Broker route availability state.
- Self-healing repair records.
- Learning approval records.
- Model-weight proposal records.
- Source-trust proposal records.
- Architect recommendation records.

It should treat these as evidence references, not as unrestricted mutation
authority.
