```text
Order: no new order from this note
```

If there is no new paper lifecycle event, Telegram should not send a paper
trial update just to say the same thing again.

### 19.9 Learning Template

Allowed:

```text
Qadam learning note
State: proposal only
Reason: Akber filter blocked a weak setup correctly
Next: keep threshold unchanged pending review
Order: none submitted
```

Learning messages must include:

- evidence class,
- credited or blamed component,
- proposal state,
- applied state.

They must not say "Qadam learned" unless an attribution record exists.

### 19.10 Repair Template

Allowed:

```text
Qadam repair note
State: repair requested
Reason: paper account mirror stale
Next: rerun canonical PaperOps pass after repair
Order: none submitted
```

Repair messages should include:

- failed artifact,
- failed check,
- repair scope,
- validation command when safe to display,
- forbidden action summary.

For PaperOps, the validation command is:

```text
.venv/bin/python scripts/run_paperops_autonomous_pass.py
```

The source of truth is:

```text
data/runtime/paperops_autonomous_pass_summary.json
```

### 19.11 Quantum Review Template

Quantum messages must be careful. Quantum review is a research and review
surface, not trading authority.

Allowed:

```text
Qadam quantum note
State: nonlinear review complete
Reason: event-probability lag stayed regime-dependent
Next: keep strategy in shadow until market confirmation
Order: none submitted
```

Rejected:

```text
Quantum confirmed the trade.
```

QSASE should never use "quantum confirmed" as a Telegram phrase. It overclaims
what the quantum layer can do.

### 19.12 Notification Frequency

Telegram should send fewer, better messages.

Send when:

- A state changes materially.
- A fresh eligible PaperOps review candidate appears.
- A PaperOps gate state changes.
- A paper lifecycle event closes.
- A hard blocker appears.
- A repair is requested.
- A repair clears.
- A learning proposal is created from new evidence.
- A dashboard mirror becomes stale or recovers.
- Delivery itself fails and needs visibility in the dashboard.

Do not send when:

- The same state repeats.
- Only timestamps changed.
- A generic deploy happened with no user-facing operating impact.
- A stale artifact was reread.
- A shadow hypothesis remains unchanged.
- A dashboard already shows the state and no threshold crossed.

### 19.13 Duplicate Suppression

QSASE must fingerprint outbound messages.

Fingerprint input:

```text
message_class
state
strategy_family
candidate_id
blocker
next_allowed_action
order_state
proof_state
```

Rules:

- Do not send the same fingerprint twice.
- Do not send the same semantic message with different wording.
- Do not bypass dedupe by adding timestamps.
- Allow a repeat only when a material field changes or the cadence window
  expires.
- Store suppressed duplicates in a ledger for audit.

Duplicate suppression should prevent the exact problem where Qadam sends the
same broad update again and again.

### 19.14 Specificity And Human-Style Gates

QSASE should run notification quality checks before a message can be sent.

Specificity requirements:

- Message has a concrete state.
- Message has a concrete reason.
- Message has a concrete next allowed action.
- Message has an order or authority line.
- Message references a backend artifact.
- Message avoids generic fallback phrases.
- Message includes exact blocker text when blocked.
- Message includes exact strategy family when strategy-related.
- Message includes exact repair reason when repair-related.

Human-style requirements:

- No commit hashes unless the message is explicitly a deployment note.
- No long URLs unless necessary.
- No internal schema jargon.
- No "What changed / Why it matters / What to check" essay blocks for QSASE
  messages.
- No multi-paragraph system reports.
- No repeated section headings.
- No broad claims such as "advanced AI improved strategy."

Quality gates should reject messages below the specificity threshold.

### 19.15 Delivery States

QSASE should separate message creation from message delivery.

States:

- `message_candidate_created`
- `message_rejected_generic`
- `message_rejected_duplicate`
- `message_rejected_unsafe`
- `message_ready_for_dashboard_only`
- `message_ready_to_send`
- `message_sent`
- `message_already_sent`
- `message_delivery_failed`
- `message_send_not_allowed`

Delivery failure is not a strategy failure. If Telegram delivery fails after
the content gate passes, QSASE should record a notification-delivery defect and
mirror it on the dashboard Communications panel.

### 19.16 Live Send Gate

QSASE should not assume it can send live Telegram messages.

Live sending requires:

- notification type is allowed,
- bot is configured,
- destination is configured,
- live-notification gate is enabled,
- message passed specificity checks,
- message passed human-style checks,
- message passed safety checks,
- message is not a duplicate,
- message is public-safe,
- message has artifact refs,
- message has no command syntax.

If live sending is not allowed, QSASE may write a dashboard-visible message
candidate and mark it:

```text
message_ready_for_dashboard_only
```

### 19.17 Inbound Intake Boundary

Telegram inbound intake remains read-only.

Inbound records should use this shape:

```text
inbound_record_id
received_at
source_chat_safe_label
message_fingerprint
content_class
world_event_datapoint_created
research_analyst_review_queued
strategy_lead_consideration_created
command_detected
command_ignored
trade_authority_created
broker_write_created
proof_credit_allowed
live_capital_enabled
```

If a message looks like a command, the correct behavior is:

```text
command_detected = true
command_ignored = true
```

not command execution.

### 19.18 Output Artifacts

QSASE should create:

```text
data/runtime/qsase_telegram_notification_boundary.json
data/runtime/qsase_telegram_message_candidates.json
data/runtime/qsase_telegram_message_queue.json
data/runtime/qsase_telegram_message_quality.json
data/runtime/qsase_telegram_dedupe_ledger.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_telegram_delivery_receipts.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_telegram_inbound_readonly_intake.json
data/runtime/qsase_telegram_notification_history.jsonl
```

The summary artifact should expose:

```text
status
generated_at
message_candidate_count
message_ready_count
message_sent_count
message_rejected_generic_count
message_rejected_duplicate_count
message_rejected_unsafe_count
delivery_failure_count
duplicate_suppressed_count
inbound_record_count
inbound_command_detected_count
inbound_command_ignored_count
telegram_live_send_allowed
telegram_command_path_enabled
telegram_trade_command_enabled
paper_order_created_count
broker_write_count
proof_credit_allowed
live_capital_enabled
```

The authority fields must remain false or zero.

### 19.19 Message Candidate Contract

Each candidate should use a schema like:

```json
{
  "message_candidate_id": "qsase-telegram-no-trade-2026-06-28-001",
  "message_class": "qsase_why_not_trading_now",
  "status": "message_ready_for_dashboard_only",
  "title": "Qadam no-trade note",
  "body": "Qadam no-trade note\nState: no fresh eligible submit\nReason: duplicate exposure check is still active\nNext: wait for exposure to clear or new distinct setup\nOrder: none submitted",
  "decision_record_ref": "data/runtime/qsase_dashboard_decision_records.json",
  "source_artifact_refs": [
    "data/runtime/qsase_strategy_router_decisions.json",
    "data/runtime/qsase_paperops_gate_interface.json"
  ],
  "quality": {
    "specificity_status": "specific",
    "specificity_score": 92,
    "human_style_status": "human",
    "generic_rejected": false,
    "duplicate_rejected": false
  },
  "delivery": {
    "telegram_live_send_allowed": false,
    "live_send_attempted": false,
    "live_send_succeeded": false,
    "already_sent": false,
    "delivery_failure_category": null
  },
  "authority": {
    "telegram_command_path_enabled": false,
    "telegram_trade_command_enabled": false,
    "trade_candidate_created": false,
    "risk_approval_created": false,
    "execution_approval_created": false,
    "paper_order_created": false,
    "broker_write_allowed": false,
    "proof_credit_allowed": false,
    "live_capital_enabled": false
  }
}
```

The candidate is evidence of a notification decision, not evidence of a trade.

### 19.20 Dashboard Communications Mirror

Every notification candidate and delivery result should mirror to the dashboard
Communications panel.

The mirror should show:

- latest message class,
- latest message status,
- live send allowed state,
- send attempted state,
- send succeeded state,
- failure category,
- duplicate suppression count,
- rejected generic count,
- inbound record count,
- inbound command ignored count,
- authority boundary.

This prevents Telegram failures from disappearing and prevents operators from
mistaking "not sent" for "nothing happened."

### 19.21 Relationship To Codebase Notifications

Codebase notifications should stay separate from QSASE strategy notifications.

If a codebase or deployment message is sent, it should be:

- short,
- human-readable,
- materially different from the last message,
- about user-facing operational impact,
- explicit that trading authority did not change,
- validated by the same specificity and duplicate gates.

It should not list every changed file, commit, deployment alias, internal hook,
or generic benefit unless the human needs it.

Allowed:

```text
Qadam deploy note
State: dashboard updated
Reason: Communications now shows Telegram delivery failures
Next: review dashboard if messages stop sending
Trading: unchanged, no order submitted
```

Rejected:

```text
Qadam Codebase Upgrade
What changed:
- Production dashboard deploy completed...
- The deploy hook now sends...
- The runtime artifact records...
```

That rejected shape is too long, too repetitive, and not specific enough about
the immediate operating state.

### 19.22 Safety Boundary

Telegram must never:

- Create commands.
- Execute commands.
- Approve trade candidates.
- Approve risk.
- Approve execution.
- Create paper orders.
- Submit paper orders.
- Close paper orders.
- Write broker records.
- Call broker endpoints.
- Call quantum providers.
- Submit Q-CTRL jobs.
- Enable live capital.
- Load live credentials.
- Edit secrets.
- Grant paper proof ledger credit.
- Simulate elapsed time in the 30-day paper growth trial.
- Override the dashboard or canonical runtime artifacts.

Telegram may:

- Summarize state.
- Summarize blockers.
- Summarize learning proposals.
- Summarize repair requests.
- Summarize paper-trial updates.
- Point humans to the dashboard.
- Record inbound context as read-only evidence.

### 19.23 Build Contract

Implementation should add:

```text
orchestrator/qsase_telegram_notification_boundary.py
scripts/check_qsase_telegram_notification_boundary.py
scripts/check_qsase_telegram_message_quality.py
scripts/check_qsase_telegram_dedupe.py
```

The module should expose:

```text
build_qsase_telegram_notification_boundary(settings)
load_qsase_telegram_notification_boundary(settings)
validate_qsase_telegram_notification_boundary(payload)
write_qsase_telegram_notification_boundary(payload, settings)
build_qsase_telegram_message_candidates(context)
validate_qsase_telegram_message_candidate(candidate)
score_qsase_telegram_message(candidate)
dedupe_qsase_telegram_message(candidate, history)
build_qsase_telegram_delivery_record(candidate, delivery_result)
build_qsase_inbound_readonly_record(inbound_message)
```

The check scripts should validate:

- Message candidates are short.
- Message candidates are specific.
- Message candidates are human-style.
- Message candidates have artifact refs.
- Message candidates have state, reason, next action, and order/authority line.
- Generic messages are rejected.
- Repeated messages are suppressed.
- Command-like inbound messages are ignored.
- Telegram command path is false.
- Telegram trade command path is false.
- Live send is gated.
- Delivery failure is recorded separately from strategy state.
- Paper order creation is zero.
- Broker write count is zero.
- Proof credit is false.
- Live capital is false.
- Dashboard Communications mirror is present.

### 19.24 Telegram And Notification Boundary Summary

QSASE should make Qadam's state easier to understand, not louder.

Telegram should tell the human only the important delta: what state changed,
why it matters, what happens next, and whether anything was submitted. It
should suppress repeats, reject generic messages, avoid internal noise, and
mirror delivery failures on the dashboard.

Most importantly, Telegram stays review-only. It can report strategy state,
paper-trial progress, learning proposals, and repair requests. It cannot create
commands, approvals, paper orders, broker writes, proof credit, quantum jobs,
or live-capital authority.

## 20. Governance And Safety Contract

The Governance And Safety Contract is the hard boundary that every QSASE module
inherits.

It asks:

```text
What can QSASE know, propose, display, and repair, and what must it never be
able to do without separate explicit governance and execution authority?
```

This contract is not a cosmetic safety disclaimer. It is a machine-checkable
authority contract. Every QSASE artifact, dashboard row, Telegram candidate,
strategy hypothesis, learning proposal, and repair request must carry the
same boundary: QSASE increases intelligence, not execution authority.

### 20.1 Purpose

The purpose of this section is to prevent a subtle failure:

```text
Qadam becomes better at finding strategies, then accidentally treats better
strategy discovery as permission to trade, mutate policy, or grant proof.
```

QSASE must be able to reason deeply about the full data universe, full trading
universe, local model, frontier model, quantum review, backtesting, strategy
foundry, Akber Filter, PaperOps route, and learning loop. But better reasoning
does not create authority.

The governance contract defines:

- what QSASE may do,
- what QSASE may propose,
- what QSASE may display,
- what QSASE may request repair for,
- what QSASE must never do,
- which flags prove the boundary stayed intact,
- how violations fail closed,
- how approval is separated from application.

### 20.2 Governance Principle

The core principle is:

```text
intelligence expands;
authority does not.
```

QSASE may expand:

- understanding,
- pattern search,
- hypothesis generation,
- historical replay,
- shadow testing,
- causal attribution,
- repair detection,
- dashboard clarity,
- Telegram specificity,
- governance proposals.

QSASE must not expand:

- live-capital authority,
- broker-write authority,
- paper-order authority,
- risk-approval authority,
- execution-approval authority,
- Q-CTRL bypass authority,
- Telegram command authority,
- source-promotion authority,
- model-weight mutation authority,
- strategy mutation authority,
- paper proof ledger credit authority.

The default state is deny. Any authority not explicitly granted to an existing
guarded subsystem remains false.

### 20.3 Authority Classes

QSASE should distinguish five authority classes.

`analysis_authority`:

```text
Allowed. QSASE may read public-safe artifacts, normalize evidence, compare
sources, run research checks, and produce research records.
```

`proposal_authority`:

```text
Allowed. QSASE may propose strategy, source, model, filter, router, dashboard,
Telegram, or repair changes. Proposals are not applied changes.
```

`review_handoff_authority`:

```text
Narrowly allowed. QSASE may create upstream handoff records for existing review
systems such as the PaperOps Gate Interface. A handoff is not a qualified
setup, risk approval, execution approval, or order.
```

`execution_authority`:

```text
Not allowed. QSASE cannot submit paper orders, call brokers, stage orders, or
route live capital.
```

`mutation_authority`:

```text
Not allowed. QSASE cannot silently mutate strategy weights, model weights,
source trust, risk policy, PaperOps gates, proof standards, or dashboard
authority.
```

This classification should appear in QSASE artifacts so validators can reject
authority leaks.

### 20.4 Universal False Authority Flags

Every QSASE artifact should carry a common authority block.

```json
{
  "authority": {
    "live_capital_enabled": false,
    "live_broker_endpoint_allowed": false,
    "broker_write_allowed": false,
    "broker_live_write_allowed": false,
    "paper_order_allowed": false,
    "paper_order_created": false,
    "qualified_setup_created": false,
    "trade_candidate_created": false,
    "risk_approval_allowed": false,
    "risk_approval_created": false,
    "execution_approval_allowed": false,
    "execution_approval_created": false,
    "telegram_command_path_enabled": false,
    "telegram_trade_command_enabled": false,
    "llm_order_authority": false,
    "local_llm_order_authority": false,
    "frontier_llm_order_authority": false,
    "quantum_order_authority": false,
    "quantum_job_authority": false,
    "qctrl_bypass_allowed": false,
    "strategy_mutation_allowed": false,
    "strategy_mutation_created": false,
    "model_weight_mutation_allowed": false,
    "model_weight_update_created": false,
    "source_trust_mutation_allowed": false,
    "trust_score_update_created": false,
    "source_promotion_allowed": false,
    "policy_mutation_allowed": false,
    "policy_mutation_created": false,
    "proof_credit_allowed": false,
    "paper_proof_ledger_write_allowed": false,
    "simulated_elapsed_time_allowed": false,
    "secret_read_allowed": false,
    "secret_write_allowed": false
  }
}
```

If a module needs a narrower set of flags, it may add module-specific false
fields, but it must not omit the universal fields from its summary artifact.

### 20.5 Allowed Actions

QSASE may:

- Build a self-model of Qadam.
- Map the source universe.
- Map the trading universe.
- Build source-price matrices.
- Search historical and current patterns.
- Run linear backtests.
- Run nonlinear and quantum-reviewed research.
- Generate strategy hypotheses.
- Reject weak or unsafe hypotheses.
- Route hypotheses to watchlist, shadow, reject, or paper-review candidate
  states.
- Create PaperOps Gate Interface handoff records.
- Create shadow replay records.
- Create learning attribution records.
- Create proposal records.
- Create repair request records.
- Create dashboard decision records.
- Create Telegram message candidates.
- Create governance review queues.

These actions are information and review actions. They do not create trade
authority.

### 20.6 Forbidden Actions

QSASE must never:

- Create Telegram commands.
- Execute Telegram commands.
- Treat Telegram inbound as command authority.
- Create trade candidates directly.
- Create qualified setups directly.
- Create risk approvals.
- Create execution approvals.
- Stage paper orders.
- Submit paper orders.
- Close paper orders.
- Write broker records.
- Call broker live endpoints.
- Call broker paper endpoints directly.
- Route around the guarded Alpaca Paper path.
- Enable live capital.
- Load live credentials.
- Edit secrets.
- Expose secrets.
- Submit Q-CTRL jobs as a trade shortcut.
- Bypass Q-CTRL paper consultation holds.
- Treat local LLM output as approval.
- Treat frontier LLM output as approval.
- Treat quantum output as approval.
- Apply model weights.
- Apply source trust scores.
- Apply strategy weights.
- Promote sources from degraded or single-source evidence.
- Mutate risk policy.
- Mutate execution policy.
- Mutate PaperOps gates.
- Grant paper proof ledger credit.
- Backfill proof evidence.
- Simulate elapsed time in the 30-day paper growth trial.
- Convert shadow trades into proof.
- Convert backtests into proof.

If QSASE detects any of these attempted actions, it must fail closed and write
an authority violation record.

### 20.7 Paper-Only Boundary

Qadam's current execution boundary is paper-only.

QSASE may reason about:

- equity and ETF paper expressions,
- indirect market expressions,
- source-to-instrument mappings,
- prediction-market context,
- crypto or commodities research context if present,
- future governed market expansions.

QSASE must not convert those research surfaces into live capital.

Rules:

- No live endpoint can be called by QSASE.
- No live credential can be loaded by QSASE.
- No broker write can be created by QSASE.
- No dashboard state can imply live authority.
- No Telegram message can imply live authority.
- No strategy confidence can imply live authority.

The only current paper submission path remains the existing guarded Alpaca
Paper route inside PaperOps. QSASE cannot call that route directly.

### 20.8 Broker Boundary

QSASE can read broker mirror state if exposed through approved runtime
artifacts.

It cannot:

- call broker APIs,
- write broker records,
- submit orders,
- cancel orders,
- close positions,
- alter account state,
- infer fills from simulations,
- claim broker action without PaperOps evidence.

Broker truth must come from existing broker mirror, PaperOps, lifecycle poller,
and paper proof ledger artifacts, not from QSASE inference.

### 20.9 LLM Boundary

QSASE uses two LLM roles:

- local model for triage, compression, classification, and low-latency
  reasoning,
- frontier model for critique, challenge, synthesis, and failure-mode review.

Neither model has authority.

Rules:

- LLM output can create research evidence.
- LLM output can create proposal language.
- LLM output can identify contradictions.
- LLM output can request human review.
- LLM output cannot approve trades.
- LLM output cannot size trades.
- LLM output cannot submit trades.
- LLM output cannot approve risk.
- LLM output cannot mutate strategy.
- LLM output cannot override deterministic checks.

If model output conflicts with deterministic runtime artifacts, artifacts win.

### 20.10 Quantum Boundary

The IBM quantum gates oracle and Q-CTRL consultation surfaces are review and
challenge layers, not authority layers.

Quantum review may:

- examine nonlinear patterns,
- challenge fragile relationships,
- score ambiguity,
- identify path dependence,
- recommend more shadow testing,
- require a hold,
- inform strategy proposals.

Quantum review must not:

- approve trades,
- create paper orders,
- submit broker orders,
- bypass PaperOps,
- bypass Q-CTRL holds,
- grant proof credit,
- imply hardware-confirmed certainty,
- replace source quorum,
- replace risk review.

Allowed dashboard or Telegram phrase:

```text
Quantum review complete; still not trade authority.
```

Disallowed phrase:

```text
Quantum confirmed the trade.
```

### 20.11 Q-CTRL Boundary

QSASE must respect Q-CTRL paper consultation holds.

Rules:

- Missing Q-CTRL state blocks paper-review progression where required.
- Q-CTRL hold state must be visible in dashboard and artifacts.
- Q-CTRL readiness does not create execution authority.
- Q-CTRL unavailability should produce a hold or repair request.
- QSASE cannot submit Q-CTRL jobs to bypass review.
- QSASE cannot mark Q-CTRL as satisfied without the existing gate artifact.

If Q-CTRL state is contradictory, the system fails closed.

### 20.12 Telegram Boundary

Telegram remains review-only and command-disabled.

Rules:

- Inbound Telegram is read-only.
- Command-like inbound messages are ignored and logged.
- Outbound Telegram is notification-only.
- Telegram messages cannot approve trades.
- Telegram messages cannot create candidates.
- Telegram messages cannot grant proof.
- Telegram messages cannot create broker writes.
- Telegram messages cannot enable live capital.
- Telegram delivery failure does not change trading authority.

Telegram should mirror state. It should never become state.

### 20.13 Dashboard Boundary

The dashboard remains read-only.

Rules:

- Dashboard displays structured state.
- Dashboard cannot create commands.
- Dashboard cannot approve trades.
- Dashboard cannot stage orders.
- Dashboard cannot submit orders.
- Dashboard cannot write brokers.
- Dashboard cannot grant proof credit.
- Dashboard cannot enable live capital.
- Dashboard cannot make stale state look current.

Safety Status remains the global authority summary. QSASE cards should inherit
that state rather than redefining authority in every panel.

### 20.14 Source Promotion Boundary

QSASE may propose source-trust or source-promotion changes, but cannot apply
them.

Source promotion requires:

- explicit source identity,
- provenance,
- historical coverage,
- freshness behavior,
- degradation behavior,
- source-quorum contribution,
- false-positive analysis,
- cross-source support,
- human or governance approval,
- audit record.

Forbidden shortcuts:

- promote a source from one good observation,
- promote a source from single-source evidence,
- promote a source while degraded,
- promote a source because an LLM liked it,
- promote a source because a backtest overfit it,
- let a supplemental source satisfy canonical quorum by itself.

QSASE can recommend promotion review. It cannot promote.

### 20.15 Strategy Mutation Boundary

QSASE may propose strategy changes, but cannot apply them.

Strategy mutations include:

- changing strategy weights,
- changing strategy family definitions,
- retiring a family,
- adding a new active family,
- changing router thresholds,
- changing Akber Filter thresholds,
- changing quantum-review requirements,
- changing source-quorum policy,
- changing risk limits,
- changing execution policy.

All strategy mutations require:

- proposal artifact,
- evidence references,
- overfit review,
- paperability review,
- risk review,
- governance approval,
- separate apply module,
- audit record,
- rollback path.

QSASE can create `proposal_ready_pending_review`. It cannot create
`strategy_change_applied` unless a future approved apply module exists and
records it separately.

### 20.16 Learning Approval Boundary

Learning is proposal-first.

QSASE may:

- attribute outcomes,
- propose weight changes,
- propose trust changes,
- propose filter changes,
- propose router changes,
- propose repair,
- queue governance review.

QSASE must not:

- apply model weights,
- apply trust scores,
- apply strategy weights,
- write active policies,
- write proof credit,
- hide failed approvals,
- treat missing approval as approval.

Missing approval means:

```text
blocked_pending_learning_approval
```

not:

```text
ready_to_apply
```

### 20.17 Paper Proof Ledger Boundary

The paper proof ledger is not a QSASE scratchpad.

Only real guarded paper lifecycle evidence can support paper proof ledger
review.

QSASE must not:

- create paper proof ledger credit,
- backfill proof entries,
- convert backtests into proof,
- convert shadow trades into proof,
- simulate fills,
- simulate elapsed time,
- infer closed trades from price paths,
- alter the 30-day paper growth trial calendar.

QSASE may:

- reference paper proof ledger records,
- explain paper proof ledger outcomes,
- attribute real paper outcomes,
- propose future strategy review based on paper evidence.

This prevents QSASE from marking its own homework.

### 20.18 Calendar Boundary

QSASE must preserve real time.

Rules:

- Actual calendar dates are used.
- Same-day reruns are deduped unless material new evidence appears.
- Historical backtests remain historical.
- Shadow replays remain shadow.
- No simulated elapsed time advances the 30-day paper growth trial.
- No stale artifact can be treated as fresh.
- No missed day can be reconstructed as a live paper day.

Calendar integrity is a safety feature, not a reporting detail.

### 20.19 Secret And Credential Boundary

QSASE must not read, write, edit, expose, or request secrets.

Allowed public states:

```text
configured
missing
credential-gated
disabled
degraded
read-only
paper-only
live-capital off
```

Forbidden:

- raw API keys,
- token values,
- broker credential values,
- Telegram bot tokens,
- chat IDs if not needed and safely labeled,
- `.env` edits,
- live credential loading,
- secret names paired with real values,
- screenshots or dashboard output containing secrets.

Credential-gated data should produce degraded or held state, not unsafe
workarounds.

### 20.20 Self-Healing Governance

Self-healing is allowed only for narrow repo and runtime-artifact repair.

Self-healing may:

- detect missing artifacts,
- detect validation failures,
- detect stale dashboard mirrors,
- detect failed checks,
- detect degraded routes,
- create repair prompts,
- rerun approved validation commands,
- write repair outcome records.

Self-healing must not:

- force trades,
- edit secrets,
- load live credentials,
- enable live capital,
- call live broker endpoints,
- bypass Q-CTRL,
- submit outside guarded Alpaca Paper route,
- grant proof credit,
- enable Telegram commands,
- mutate strategy to make a check pass.

If repair would require a forbidden action, the correct state is:

```text
blocked_requires_human_governance
```

### 20.21 Fail-Closed Behavior

QSASE should fail closed on safety uncertainty.

Fail closed when:

- live-capital flag is true,
- broker write flag is true,
- Telegram command path is true,
- proof credit flag is true without paper proof ledger authority,
- simulated elapsed time appears,
- paper order creation appears outside PaperOps,
- source is promoted from degraded evidence,
- strategy mutation appears without approval,
- model weight update appears without approval,
- trust score update appears without approval,
- Q-CTRL state is missing where required,
- artifact freshness is unknown,
- dashboard state contradicts runtime state,
- message quality gate fails,
- validation schema fails.

Fail-closed output should include:

```text
status = blocked_authority_boundary_violation
violation_type
offending_field
source_artifact
recommended_next_action
repair_or_governance_required
```

### 20.22 Violation Taxonomy

Governance violations should use consistent names.

Suggested violation types:

- `live_capital_enabled`
- `live_broker_endpoint_detected`
- `broker_write_authority_detected`
- `paper_order_created_outside_paperops`
- `qualified_setup_created_outside_paperops`
- `risk_approval_created_outside_risk_agent`
- `execution_approval_created_outside_execution_policy`
- `telegram_command_path_enabled`
- `telegram_inbound_command_executed`
- `llm_order_authority_detected`
- `quantum_order_authority_detected`
- `qctrl_bypass_detected`
- `strategy_mutation_without_approval`
- `model_weight_mutation_without_approval`
- `trust_score_mutation_without_approval`
- `source_promotion_without_governance`
- `proof_credit_granted_without_paper_lifecycle`
- `simulated_elapsed_time_detected`
- `secret_exposure_detected`
- `dashboard_write_authority_detected`
- `notification_command_authority_detected`

Each violation should be countable, dashboard-visible, and blocking.

### 20.23 Governance Review Records

When QSASE proposes a change, governance review records should include:

```text
review_id
proposal_id
proposal_type
target_surface
evidence_refs
supporting_evidence_count
contradicting_evidence_count
real_paper_evidence_count
shadow_evidence_count
backtest_evidence_count
overfit_risk
source_noise_risk
regime_bias_risk
paperability_state
risk_review_required
approval_state
approved_by
approved_at
apply_module_required
applied
rollback_plan_ref
authority_flags
```

In the initial QSASE implementation:

```text
approval_state = pending_review
applied = false
```

```json
    "paper_proof_ledger_write_allowed": false
  },
  "authority": {

    "quantum_order_authority": false,
    "qctrl_bypass_allowed": false,
    "strategy_mutation_allowed": false,
    "model_weight_mutation_allowed": false,
    "source_trust_mutation_allowed": false,
    "proof_credit_allowed": false,
    "simulated_elapsed_time_allowed": false
  }
}
```

### 20.26 Dashboard Representation

The dashboard should show governance as one compact Safety Status record.

Recommended display:

```text
Safety Status
Mode: paper-only
QSASE authority: research and proposals only
Dashboard: read-only
Telegram: review-only
Broker writes: no
Live capital: off
Proof: paper proof ledger only
Violations: 0
```

Detailed authority flags should live in Operations, not Overview.

### 20.27 Telegram Representation

Telegram should only mention governance when there is a material state change.

Allowed:

```text
Qadam safety note
State: repair blocked
Reason: requested fix would require secret edit
Next: human governance required
Order: none submitted
```

Allowed:

```text
Qadam safety note
State: authority boundary clean
Reason: PaperOps repaired and live capital remains off
Next: review dashboard
Order: none submitted
```

Do not send repetitive "safety unchanged" messages.

### 20.28 Build Contract

Implementation should add:

```text
orchestrator/qsase_governance_safety_contract.py
scripts/check_qsase_governance_safety_contract.py
scripts/check_qsase_authority_flags.py
scripts/check_qsase_proof_boundary.py
```

The module should expose:

```text
build_qsase_governance_safety_contract(settings)
load_qsase_governance_safety_contract(settings)
validate_qsase_governance_safety_contract(payload)
write_qsase_governance_safety_contract(payload, settings)
build_qsase_authority_flag_audit(context)
detect_qsase_authority_violations(context)
build_qsase_governance_review_queue(context)
validate_qsase_proof_boundary(context)
validate_qsase_calendar_boundary(context)
fail_closed_authority_violation(violation)
```

The check scripts should validate:

- Universal authority flags exist.
- Universal authority flags are false.
- Live capital is false.
- Broker write count is zero.
- Paper order creation outside PaperOps is zero.
- Telegram command path is false.
- LLM order authority is false.
- Quantum order authority is false.
- Q-CTRL bypass is false.
- Strategy mutation is false unless an approved apply module is present.
- Model-weight mutation is false unless an approved apply module is present.
- Source-trust mutation is false unless an approved apply module is present.
- Source promotion from degraded or single-source evidence is rejected.
- Proof credit is false.
- Paper proof ledger write authority is false.
- Simulated elapsed time is false.
- Backtest-to-proof conversion is rejected.
- Shadow-to-proof conversion is rejected.
- Secret exposure is rejected.
- Dashboard remains read-only.
- Telegram remains review-only.
- Violations fail closed.

### 20.29 Governance And Safety Contract Summary

The Governance And Safety Contract is the lock around QSASE.

QSASE is allowed to become much smarter than the current Qadam. It can map the
entire source universe, search the entire trading universe, use local and
frontier LLMs, review nonlinear patterns with quantum tooling, generate new
strategy families, test them historically, run them in shadow, route them
toward PaperOps review, learn from outcomes, and propose improvements.

But it cannot convert intelligence into authority. It cannot create orders,
touch brokers, enable live capital, treat Telegram as commands, treat LLMs or
quantum as approvals, mutate strategy without governance, promote sources from
weak evidence, grant proof credit, or fake time in the 30-day paper growth
trial.

That boundary is what makes the metamorphosis safe: Qadam becomes more
self-aware about its own code, models, data, and markets while still remaining
paper-only, auditable, proposal-first, and governed.

## 21. Implementation Phases

The Implementation Phases turn QSASE from doctrine into executable Qadam
infrastructure.

They ask:

```text
In what order should Qadam be metamorphosed so each layer can be validated
before the next layer depends on it?
```

The implementation must be staged. QSASE should not begin by trying to generate
orders, strategies, or dashboard stories. It should begin by making Qadam's own
state, sources, markets, memory, authority boundaries, and validation contracts
machine-readable.

### 21.1 Implementation Rule

The implementation rule is:

```text
build the introspection and evidence spine before building the strategy spine;
build the strategy spine before building the PaperOps handoff;
build the handoff before building learning;
build learning before exposing recursive improvement.
```

This prevents a common failure mode:

```text
strategy generation appears before the system can prove source coverage,
paperability, authority flags, or outcome attribution.
```

### 21.2 Phase Exit Standard

Every phase must exit with:

- A runtime artifact.
- A history artifact where relevant.
- An event artifact where relevant.
- A validation script.
- A dashboard-safe summary where relevant.
- Explicit blockers.
- Explicit authority flags.
- A fail-closed state.
- No live capital.
- No broker writes.
- No paper orders unless the existing PaperOps route creates them.
- No paper proof ledger credit.
- No simulated elapsed time in the 30-day paper growth trial.

If a phase cannot prove its boundary, it is not complete.

### 21.3 Dependency Map

Recommended dependency order:

```text
QSASE-0 governance and doctrine
-> QSASE-1 self-model
-> QSASE-2 universal source-price matrix
-> QSASE-3 historical source-price memory
-> QSASE-4 full-universe pattern search
-> QSASE-5 linear pattern lab
-> QSASE-6 nonlinear and quantum pattern lab
-> QSASE-7 strategy foundry
-> QSASE-8 Akber filter integration
-> QSASE-9 shadow strategy simulator
-> QSASE-10 strategy router
-> QSASE-11 PaperOps handoff interface
-> QSASE-12 learning and attribution ledger
-> QSASE-13 dashboard visibility
-> QSASE-14 Telegram boundary
-> QSASE-15 end-to-end certification
```

Some implementation work can happen in parallel, but certification should
follow the dependency order.

Before or alongside QSASE-0, Qadam should run the Operational Phase 0 baseline
defined in Appendix A. That baseline does not replace QSASE. It proves that the
existing PaperOps substrate is fresh, explainable, lineage-complete, and
dashboard-consistent enough for QSASE to depend on it.

### 21.4 QSASE-0: Doctrine, Document Hierarchy, And Safety Contract

Objective:

```text
Make QSASE's constitutional premise, source of truth, and authority boundary
explicit before code is added.
```

Scope:

- Confirm QSASE is an evolution of Qadam, not a separate product.
- Define document hierarchy.
- Confirm canonical Qadam specs and user guide references.
- Lock the paper-only boundary.
- Lock the proposal-first learning boundary.
- Lock Telegram and dashboard as non-authoritative.
- Lock no backfilled paper proof and no simulated paper-trial time.

Primary artifacts:

```text
docs/qadam-qsase-implementation-plan.md
data/runtime/qsase_governance_safety_contract.json
```

Primary checks:

```text
scripts/check_qsase_governance_safety_contract.py
scripts/check_qsase_authority_flags.py
scripts/check_qsase_proof_boundary.py
```

Exit criteria:

- Governance and safety contract exists.
- Universal false authority flags exist.
- Safety checks fail closed.
- No execution path is introduced.
- No dashboard or Telegram authority is introduced.

### 21.5 QSASE-1: Self-Model Artifact And Validation

Objective:

```text
Give QSASE a current, machine-readable model of Qadam's own architecture,
capabilities, limitations, and safety boundaries.
```

Scope:

- Python orchestrator role.
- Local Gemma role.
- Frontier Gemini role.
- IBM quantum gates oracle role.
- Q-CTRL paper consultation role.
- Source adapter role.
- Historical memory role.
- PaperOps route role.
- Dashboard and Telegram role.
- Current degraded components.
- Current authority flags.

```text

data/runtime/qsase_self_model_history.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_self_model_events.jsonl
```

Primary module:

```text

```

Primary check:

```text
scripts/check_qsase_self_model.py
```

Exit criteria:

- Self-model is current and public-safe.
- Degraded components are explicit.
- Model roles are separated from authority.
- Quantum review is represented as review, not approval.
- Dashboard and Telegram visibility failures do not alter trading authority.
- All authority flags remain false.

- Specificity checks.
- Human-style checks.
- Duplicate suppression.
- Delivery states.
- Dashboard Communications mirror.
- Read-only inbound intake.
- Command-like inbound ignore records.

Primary artifacts:

```text
data/runtime/qsase_telegram_notification_boundary.json
data/runtime/qsase_telegram_message_candidates.json
data/runtime/qsase_telegram_dedupe_ledger.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_telegram_delivery_receipts.jsonl
```

Primary module:

```text
orchestrator/qsase_telegram_notification_boundary.py
```

Primary checks:

```text
scripts/check_qsase_telegram_notification_boundary.py
scripts/check_qsase_telegram_message_quality.py
scripts/check_qsase_telegram_dedupe.py
```

Exit criteria:

- Telegram messages are short and specific.
- Generic messages are rejected.
- Repeated messages are suppressed.
- Inbound commands are ignored.
- Delivery failure is not strategy failure.
- Telegram cannot create commands, orders, proof, or live-capital authority.

### 21.19 QSASE-15: End-To-End Certification

Objective:

```text
Prove QSASE works as one governed system before any module is treated as an
operational dependency.
```

Scope:

- Full artifact generation.
- Full validation script chain.
- Authority flag audit.
- Pattern search to strategy foundry trace.
- Strategy foundry to router trace.
- Router to PaperOps handoff trace.
- PaperOps handoff to existing PaperOps boundary trace.
- Learning attribution trace.
- Dashboard decision record trace.
- Telegram candidate trace.
- Self-healing repair trace.
- Proof boundary trace.
- Calendar boundary trace.

Primary artifacts:

```text
data/runtime/qsase_end_to_end_certification.json
data/runtime/qsase_end_to_end_certification_history.jsonl
data/runtime/qsase_end_to_end_certification_events.jsonl
```

Primary module:

```text
orchestrator/qsase_end_to_end_certification.py
```

Primary check:

```text
scripts/check_qsase_end_to_end_certification.py
```

Exit criteria:

- Every prior phase artifact validates.
- Every prior phase has a dashboard-safe summary or explicit no-display state.
- Pattern lineage is traceable end to end.
- Strategy lineage is traceable end to end.
- PaperOps handoff does not bypass PaperOps.
- Telegram and dashboard remain non-authoritative.
- Learning proposals are not applied.
- Paper proof ledger credit is not created.
- No simulated elapsed time exists.
- Live capital remains false.

### 21.20 Certification Command Bundle

Once the modules exist, certification should have a single command bundle.

Recommended script:

```text
scripts/check_qsase_certification.py
```

It should run:

```text
scripts/check_qsase_governance_safety_contract.py
scripts/check_qsase_authority_flags.py
scripts/check_qsase_proof_boundary.py
scripts/check_qsase_self_model.py
scripts/check_qsase_universal_source_price_matrix.py
scripts/check_qsase_historical_source_price_memory.py
scripts/check_qsase_full_universe_pattern_search.py
scripts/check_qsase_linear_pattern_lab.py
scripts/check_qsase_nonlinear_quantum_pattern_lab.py
scripts/check_qsase_strategy_foundry.py
scripts/check_qsase_akber_filter_integration.py
scripts/check_qsase_shadow_strategy_simulator.py
scripts/check_qsase_strategy_router.py
scripts/check_qsase_paperops_gate_interface.py
scripts/check_qsase_learning_attribution_ledger.py
scripts/check_qsase_recursive_improvement_contract.py
scripts/check_qsase_dashboard_view_model.py
scripts/check_qsase_dashboard_anti_slop.py
scripts/check_qsase_telegram_notification_boundary.py
scripts/check_qsase_telegram_message_quality.py
scripts/check_qsase_telegram_dedupe.py
scripts/check_qsase_end_to_end_certification.py
```

The bundle should fail on the first authority violation and report the failed
artifact and field.

### 21.21 PaperOps Compatibility Requirement

QSASE certification must include a PaperOps compatibility check.

It should prove:

- QSASE does not alter the canonical PaperOps wrapper command.
- QSASE does not alter the canonical PaperOps summary source of truth.
- QSASE preserves the 30-day paper growth trial calendar.
- QSASE does not backfill paper lifecycle evidence.
- QSASE does not simulate paper proof ledger credit.
- QSASE does not submit outside the guarded Alpaca Paper route.
- QSASE does not load live credentials.
- QSASE does not enable live capital.

For PaperOps-specific validation, the canonical command remains:

```text
.venv/bin/python scripts/run_paperops_autonomous_pass.py
```

and the canonical summary remains:

```text
data/runtime/paperops_autonomous_pass_summary.json
```

QSASE may read this summary as evidence. It must not replace it.

### 21.22 Release Strategy

Recommended release strategy:

`Release A: Foundation`

```text
QSASE-0 through QSASE-3
```

Outcome:

```text
Qadam understands itself, its source universe, its trading universe, and its
historical coverage without generating strategies yet.
```

`Release B: Research Engine`

```text
QSASE-4 through QSASE-6
```

Outcome:

```text
Qadam can search and test full-universe patterns linearly, nonlinearly, and
with quantum review without creating strategy authority.
```

`Release C: Strategy Intelligence`

```text
QSASE-7 through QSASE-10
```

Outcome:

```text
Qadam can map patterns into strategy hypotheses and route them correctly
without creating orders.
```

`Release D: PaperOps Integration`

```text
QSASE-11 through QSASE-12
```

Outcome:

```text
Qadam can hand off paper-review candidates to existing PaperOps and learn from
outcomes without mutating strategy.
```

`Release E: Human Visibility`

```text
QSASE-13 through QSASE-14
```

Outcome:

```text
Dashboard and Telegram show QSASE state concisely, public-safely, and without
authority drift.
```

`Release F: Certification`

```text
QSASE-15
```

Outcome:

```text
The full QSASE chain validates end to end under the governance contract.
```

### 21.23 Implementation Phases Summary

The phases are deliberately ordered from self-knowledge to market knowledge to
strategy knowledge to PaperOps integration to learning to human visibility to
certification.

That order matters. QSASE should not discover strategies before it can prove
what data it used. It should not route strategies before it can prove
paperability and authority flags. It should not learn from outcomes before it
can separate paper, shadow, backtest, non-trade, and system-defect evidence. It
should not notify humans before it can reject generic and repeated messages.

This phased rollout turns QSASE into the next evolution of Qadam without
weakening the existing guarded paper-operation architecture.

## 22. Acceptance Criteria

The Acceptance Criteria define the point at which QSASE can be considered
implemented as Qadam's next operating layer.

They ask:

```text
What must be provably true before Qadam can claim that QSASE exists as a
governed, full-universe, self-aware strategy engine?
```

Acceptance is not satisfied by a document, a dashboard card, a model response,
or a single successful backtest. Acceptance requires artifacts, validation
scripts, authority audits, source-price lineage, dashboard-safe readouts, and
negative tests that prove QSASE cannot bypass Qadam's existing paper-only
boundaries.

### 22.1 Acceptance Standard

QSASE is accepted only when:

- Every required runtime artifact exists.
- Every required validation script passes.
- Every artifact has explicit schema versioning.
- Every artifact has generated_at.
- Every artifact has source references.
- Every artifact has explicit authority flags.
- Every authority flag that should be false is false.
- Every decision record has evidence, reason, blocker, next action, and
  authority boundary.
- Every strategy hypothesis has lineage.
- Every paper-review handoff preserves PaperOps authority.
- Every learning proposal is proposal-only.
- Every dashboard and Telegram summary is public-safe.
- Every negative safety probe fails closed.

The system is not accepted if it only works in the happy path.

### 22.2 Certification Artifact

End-to-end acceptance should produce:

```text
data/runtime/qsase_end_to_end_certification.json
```

The certification artifact should include:

```text
status
generated_at
certification_version
phase_count
passed_phase_count
failed_phase_count
warning_count
required_artifact_count
present_artifact_count
required_check_count
passed_check_count
failed_check_count
authority_violation_count
lineage_gap_count
dashboard_slop_failure_count
telegram_quality_failure_count
paperops_boundary_failure_count
proof_boundary_failure_count
calendar_boundary_failure_count
live_capital_enabled
broker_write_count
paper_order_created_outside_paperops_count
proof_credit_allowed
certified
```

`certified` can be true only when all hard criteria pass.

### 22.3 Capability Acceptance Matrix

QSASE must pass these capability groups.

Self-awareness:

```text
QSASE can explain Qadam's current architecture, model roles, data sources,
trading universe, degraded components, and authority boundaries from artifacts.
```

Source-price universe:

```text
QSASE can build a universal source-price matrix across the full data universe
and full trading universe.
```

Historical memory:

```text
QSASE can align historical source observations with historical market prices
without turning historical replay into proof.
```

Pattern discovery:

```text
QSASE can search across source families, instruments, lags, regimes, and
relationships without prematurely creating strategies.
```

Linear testing:

```text
QSASE can run transparent linear tests with out-of-sample checks and rejection
reasons.
```

Nonlinear and quantum testing:

```text
QSASE can run nonlinear and quantum-reviewed research while labeling quantum
state accurately and preserving non-authority.
```

Strategy creation:

```text
QSASE can map validated patterns into existing or new strategy hypotheses.
```

Filter and routing:

```text
QSASE can apply Akber's filter and route strategies to reject, watchlist,
shadow, hold, repair, or PaperOps review states.
```

PaperOps handoff:

```text
QSASE can create only upstream PaperOps handoff records, not qualified setups
or orders.
```

Learning:

```text
QSASE can attribute outcomes to components and produce approval-gated
proposals.
```

Visibility:

```text
QSASE can show concise dashboard and Telegram decision records without
repetition, generic claims, or command authority.
```

Governance:

```text
QSASE can prove every unsafe authority flag remains false and every safety
probe fails closed.
```

### 22.4 Required Check Bundle

The acceptance command should be:

```text
scripts/check_qsase_certification.py
```

It should require these checks to pass:

```text
scripts/check_qsase_governance_safety_contract.py
scripts/check_qsase_authority_flags.py
scripts/check_qsase_proof_boundary.py
scripts/check_qsase_self_model.py
scripts/check_qsase_universal_source_price_matrix.py
scripts/check_qsase_historical_source_price_memory.py
scripts/check_qsase_full_universe_pattern_search.py
scripts/check_qsase_linear_pattern_lab.py
scripts/check_qsase_nonlinear_quantum_pattern_lab.py
scripts/check_qsase_strategy_foundry.py
scripts/check_qsase_akber_filter_integration.py
scripts/check_qsase_shadow_strategy_simulator.py
scripts/check_qsase_strategy_router.py
scripts/check_qsase_paperops_gate_interface.py
scripts/check_qsase_learning_attribution_ledger.py
scripts/check_qsase_recursive_improvement_contract.py
scripts/check_qsase_dashboard_view_model.py
scripts/check_qsase_dashboard_anti_slop.py
scripts/check_qsase_telegram_notification_boundary.py
scripts/check_qsase_telegram_message_quality.py
scripts/check_qsase_telegram_dedupe.py
scripts/check_qsase_end_to_end_certification.py
```

The bundle should emit one canonical result:

```text
qsase_certification_check=ok
```

or:

```text
qsase_certification_check=failed
```

### 22.5 Self-Model Acceptance

The self-model is accepted when:

- It names the Python orchestrator role.
- It names the local Gemma role.
- It names the frontier Gemini role.
- It names the IBM quantum gates oracle role.
- It names the Q-CTRL paper consultation role.
- It names the source adapter role.
- It names the historical memory role.
- It names the PaperOps role.
- It names the dashboard role.
- It names the Telegram role.
- It lists degraded components.
- It lists missing components.
- It lists current source coverage.
- It lists current trading-universe coverage.
- It carries universal authority flags.
- It says model outputs are not approvals.
- It says quantum outputs are not approvals.
- It says dashboard and Telegram are not authority.

Acceptance failure examples:

- Self-model omits authority flags.
- Self-model calls quantum review "confirmation."
- Self-model treats Telegram inbound as command context.
- Self-model claims live-capital readiness.
- Self-model hides degraded source coverage.

### 22.6 Source-Price Matrix Acceptance

The source-price matrix is accepted when:

- Every source family has a state.
- Every source family has provenance.
- Every source family has freshness state.
- Every source family has trust posture.
- Every source family has source-quorum contribution.
- Every trading-universe instrument has a mapping state.
- Every instrument has paperability state.
- Every source-to-instrument edge has a relationship type.
- Credential-gated sources are marked as credential-gated.
- Degraded sources do not satisfy source quorum.
- Supplemental sources do not become canonical by accident.
- Prediction-market sources are context unless a governed route exists.
- Reddit and Capitol Trades can be represented as source families when plugged
  in, without becoming command or execution surfaces.

Acceptance failure examples:

- A source is present without provenance.
- A trading instrument is evaluated without price data state.
- A source is treated as canonical while degraded.
- A source-to-instrument mapping implies direct trade authority.

### 22.7 Historical Memory Acceptance

Historical memory is accepted when:

- Historical source windows are explicit.
- Historical price windows are explicit.
- Gaps are explicit.
- Replay mode is explicit.
- Source-price alignment is reproducible.
- Same-day observations are deduped.
- Historical records are not confused with current paper lifecycle records.
- Backfilled historical data cannot advance the 30-day paper growth trial.
- Historical replay cannot create paper proof ledger credit.

Acceptance failure examples:

- Historical backfill creates current-day proof state.
- Historical replay lacks provenance.
- Historical price alignment cannot be reproduced.
- Missing data is silently interpolated without a gap record.

### 22.8 Full-Universe Search Acceptance

Full-universe search is accepted when:

- It searches across the full source matrix.
- It searches across the full trading universe.
- It can evaluate cross-source relationships.
- It can evaluate source-price lags.
- It can evaluate cross-asset relationships.
- It can evaluate regime-conditioned relationships.
- It records rejected patterns.
- It records why patterns are rejected.
- It does not create strategy hypotheses before Strategy Foundry.
- It does not create trade candidates.
- It does not promote degraded evidence.

Acceptance failure examples:

- Search is limited to one compartment while claiming full-universe coverage.
- Candidate patterns lack source references.
- Pattern score becomes strategy approval.
- Rejected patterns are discarded without reason.

### 22.9 Linear Lab Acceptance

The Linear Pattern Recognition Lab is accepted when:

- It runs transparent linear tests.
- It separates in-sample and out-of-sample windows.
- It records baselines.
- It records lag selection.
- It records event-study windows.
- It records false-positive checks.
- It records overfit risk.
- It records rejected tests.
- It produces dashboard-safe summaries.
- It cannot create orders.
- It cannot create proof credit.

Acceptance failure examples:

- A linear backtest result is shown as proof.
- In-sample performance is reported as out-of-sample performance.
- Weak results are rounded into confident strategy language.
- Linear success bypasses Akber Filter or Strategy Router.

### 22.10 Nonlinear And Quantum Lab Acceptance

The Nonlinear And Quantum Pattern Lab is accepted when:

- It records nonlinear method type.
- It records regime-dependence.
- It records path-dependence where detected.
- It records quantum review state.
- It records hardware, simulator, or classical fallback mode.
- It records Q-CTRL state where required.
- It records whether quantum review added useful information.
- It records ambiguity instead of hiding it.
- It cannot call quantum providers as execution authority.
- It cannot present quantum review as trade confirmation.

Acceptance failure examples:

- Dashboard says "quantum confirmed."
- Quantum review lacks source-price lineage.
- Quantum state is unknown but presented as ready.
- Q-CTRL hold is bypassed.

### 22.11 Strategy Foundry Acceptance

Strategy Foundry is accepted when:

- Every strategy hypothesis has source-price pattern lineage.
- Every strategy hypothesis has Research Goal lineage.
- Every strategy hypothesis has candidate identity.
- Every strategy hypothesis has strategy family.
- Every strategy hypothesis has instrument mapping.
- Every strategy hypothesis has paperability state.
- Every strategy hypothesis has invalidation concept.
- Every strategy hypothesis has risk concept.
- Every new strategy family is hypothesis-only.
- Weak, unsafe, overfit, non-paperable, or non-explainable hypotheses are
  rejected before routing.

Acceptance failure examples:

- A strategy hypothesis becomes an order.
- A new strategy family becomes active without governance.
- A hypothesis lacks source-price evidence.
- A rejected hypothesis has no rejection reason.

### 22.12 Akber Filter Acceptance

Akber Filter integration is accepted when:

- Every pass has a reason.
- Every veto has a reason.
- Historical filter replay exists.
- Filter contribution can be attributed.
- Filter thresholds are proposal-only.
- Filter state is visible to the router.
- Filter state is visible to dashboard decision records.
- Filter pass does not imply execution approval.
- Filter vetoes become learning evidence.

Acceptance failure examples:

- Filter output is a generic pass/fail with no reason.
- Filter threshold changes are applied silently.
- Filter pass skips PaperOps.
- Filter veto is hidden as "no setup."

### 22.13 Shadow Simulator Acceptance

The Shadow Strategy Simulator is accepted when:

- It can run historical shadow replay.
- It can run forward shadow watch.
- It records no-order state.
- It records counterfactual outcomes.
- It records source-price lineage.
- It records strategy hypothesis lineage.
- It records evidence class.
- It can feed learning attribution.
- It cannot create paper orders.
- It cannot create paper proof ledger credit.

Acceptance failure examples:

- Shadow fills appear in paper account state.
- Shadow results are counted as paper proof.
- Shadow replay lacks timestamp or source references.
- Shadow success bypasses PaperOps handoff.

### 22.14 Strategy Router Acceptance

Strategy Router is accepted when:

- Every hypothesis gets one router state.
- Router states are explicit.
- Holds have specific reasons.
- Rejections have specific reasons.
- Repair-worthy failures are routed to repair.
- No-trade states are distinguished from broken-system states.
- Paper-review candidates preserve lineage.
- Paper-review candidates carry authority flags.
- Paper-review candidates do not become qualified setups.
- Paper-review candidates do not become orders.

Required router states:

```text
paper_review_candidate
shadow_only
watchlist_only
reject
hold_missing_evidence
hold_route_unavailable
repair_requested
blocked_safety_boundary
```

Acceptance failure examples:

- Router emits "trade-ready" without PaperOps evidence.
- Router collapses repair and no-trade into the same state.
- Router creates a trade candidate directly.
- Router omits why-not-trading-now.

### 22.15 PaperOps Handoff Acceptance

PaperOps handoff is accepted when:

- Handoff records are upstream context only.
- Handoff records preserve Research Goal lineage.
- Handoff records preserve candidate identity.
- Handoff records preserve idempotency material.
- Handoff records include source quorum state.
- Handoff records include Akber Filter state.
- Handoff records include quantum review state where required.
- Handoff records include duplicate exposure state.
- Handoff records include daily drawdown state.
- Handoff records include Q-CTRL hold state.
- Handoff records include guarded Alpaca Paper route state.
- Handoff records cannot create qualified setups.
- Handoff records cannot create paper orders.
- Existing PaperOps remains the only guarded paper submit route.

Acceptance failure examples:

- Handoff record is treated as a submit record.
- Handoff lacks idempotency material.
- Q-CTRL hold is missing or bypassed.
- Paper order is created outside existing PaperOps.

### 22.16 Learning And Attribution Acceptance

The Learning And Attribution Ledger is accepted when:

- Real paper lifecycle evidence is separated from shadow evidence.
- Shadow evidence is separated from backtest evidence.
- Non-trade decisions are first-class learning records.
- Rejected hypotheses are first-class learning records.
- System defects are first-class attribution records.
- Component attribution is explicit.
- Causal labels are explicit.
- Proposals have evidence references.
- Proposals have approval-required state.
- No proposal is applied by the ledger.
- No source trust score is mutated.
- No model weight is mutated.
- No strategy weight is mutated.
- No paper proof ledger credit is created.

Acceptance failure examples:

- A winning paper trade automatically changes strategy weights.
- A stale route defect is treated as strategy failure.
- A no-trade decision is ignored.
- A model explanation is treated as learning without attribution evidence.

### 22.17 Recursive Improvement Acceptance

The Recursive Improvement Contract is accepted when:

- Cycle state is explicit.
- Cycle inputs are explicit.
- Cycle outputs are explicit.
- Proposal count is explicit.
- Repair request count is explicit.
- Approval-required count is explicit.
- Applied change count is zero unless a future approved apply module exists.
- Valid no-action is separated from repair-worthy failure.
- Self-healing repair queue is narrow and safe.
- Calendar boundary is preserved.
- Authority flags remain false.

Acceptance failure examples:

- Recursive improvement applies a change by itself.
- Self-healing mutates strategy to pass a check.
- Repair prompt asks to edit secrets.
- A stale artifact is treated as fresh cycle evidence.

### 22.18 Dashboard Acceptance

Dashboard acceptance requires:

- QSASE Snapshot is present.
- Portfolio value line graph is present or explicitly unavailable.
- Current portfolio holdings are present or explicitly empty.
- Trading history rows are present or explicitly empty.
- Strategy Universe rows are present when strategies exist.
- Strategy Universe distinguishes all strategies from strategies currently in
  play.
- Connected data source categories are present.
- Connected individual data source rows are present.
- Source and trading universe rows are present.
- Pattern lab rows are present.
- Linear pattern rows and nonlinear pattern rows are separated or clearly
  labeled.
- Trade intent rows are present when Qadam is considering setups.
- Trade intent rows cannot be labeled as trades, orders, approvals, or
  qualified setups.
- Self-model rows are present.
- Router decision record is present.
- Why-not-trading-now record is present.
- PaperOps gate record is present when relevant.
- Learning ledger rows are present when learning exists.
- Recursive improvement record is present.
- Repair queue rows are present when repairs exist.
- Overview does not contain detailed ledgers.
- Every card has artifact refs.
- Every card has state, reason, blocker, next action, and authority boundary.
- Duplicate headlines are rejected.
- Generic AI prose is rejected.
- Stale state is labeled stale.
- Dashboard remains read-only.

Acceptance failure examples:

- Dashboard says "Qadam learned" without attribution evidence.
- Dashboard says "trade-ready" without PaperOps route evidence.
- Dashboard hides the portfolio value line graph when paper account data is
  available.
- Dashboard shows a trade intent as an order.
- Dashboard collapses connected source categories into a generic source count.
- Dashboard shows a nonlinear pattern without nonlinear or quantum review
  state.
- Dashboard repeats the same safety copy in several places.
- Dashboard hides a repair request as strategy discipline.

### 22.19 Telegram Acceptance

Telegram acceptance requires:

- Message candidates are short.
- Message candidates are specific.
- Message candidates are public-safe.
- Message candidates have artifact refs.
- Message candidates have state, reason, next action, and order/authority line.
- Generic messages are rejected.
- Repeated messages are suppressed.
- Inbound intake is read-only.
- Command-like inbound messages are ignored.
- Delivery failure is recorded separately from strategy state.
- Dashboard Communications mirrors notification state.
- Telegram command path is false.
- Telegram trade command path is false.
- Telegram cannot create orders, approvals, proof, broker writes, or live
  capital.

Acceptance failure examples:

- Same message is sent repeatedly with small wording changes.
- Generic codebase upgrade essay passes quality gates.
- Telegram inbound creates a candidate or command.
- Telegram delivery failure is reported as strategy failure.

### 22.20 Governance Acceptance

Governance acceptance requires:

- Universal authority flags exist.
- Universal authority flags are false.
- Authority violation count is zero.
- Live capital is false.
- Broker write count is zero.
- Paper order creation outside PaperOps is zero.
- Telegram command path is false.
- LLM order authority is false.
- Quantum order authority is false.
- Q-CTRL bypass is false.
- Strategy mutation is false.
- Model-weight mutation is false.
- Source-trust mutation is false.
- Source promotion from degraded or single-source evidence is rejected.
- Proof credit is false.
- Simulated elapsed time is false.
- Secret exposure is false.
- Violations fail closed.

Acceptance failure examples:

- Any unsafe authority flag is true.
- Missing authority flag passes validation.
- Live-capital readiness appears in a dashboard or Telegram output.
- Proof credit appears without real guarded paper lifecycle evidence.

### 22.21 PaperOps Compatibility Acceptance

QSASE must remain compatible with existing PaperOps.

Acceptance requires:

- The canonical PaperOps command remains unchanged.
- The canonical PaperOps summary remains the source of truth.
- QSASE may read PaperOps artifacts as evidence.
- QSASE does not replace PaperOps.
- QSASE does not create qualified setups directly.
- QSASE does not submit paper orders directly.
- QSASE does not call Alpaca directly.
- QSASE does not bypass Q-CTRL paper consultation holds.
- QSASE preserves idempotency.
- QSASE preserves duplicate exposure checks.
- QSASE preserves daily drawdown checks.
- QSASE preserves the 30-day paper growth trial calendar.
- QSASE cannot grant paper proof ledger credit.

Canonical PaperOps validation command:

```text
.venv/bin/python scripts/run_paperops_autonomous_pass.py
```

Canonical PaperOps summary:

```text
data/runtime/paperops_autonomous_pass_summary.json
```

### 22.22 End-To-End Lineage Acceptance

End-to-end lineage is accepted when a reviewer can trace:

```text
source observation
-> source-price matrix edge
-> historical memory record
-> candidate pattern
-> linear or nonlinear lab result
-> quantum review where required
-> strategy hypothesis
-> Akber Filter result
-> shadow result where required
-> router decision
-> PaperOps handoff or rejection
-> PaperOps lifecycle evidence where present
-> learning attribution record
-> proposal or repair request
-> dashboard decision record
-> Telegram candidate where present
```

Any broken link is a certification failure unless the gap is explicitly marked
as unavailable and blocks downstream promotion.

### 22.23 Negative Probe Acceptance

The certification suite must include negative probes.

Required negative probes:

- Live-capital flag true.
- Broker write flag true.
- Telegram command path true.
- LLM order authority true.
- Quantum order authority true.
- Q-CTRL bypass true.
- Paper order created outside PaperOps.
- Qualified setup created outside PaperOps.
- Strategy mutation without approval.
- Model-weight mutation without approval.
- Source-trust mutation without approval.
- Source promoted from degraded evidence.
- Backtest converted to proof.
- Shadow replay converted to proof.
- Simulated elapsed time detected.
- Stale artifact presented as current.
- Generic dashboard card accepted.
- Duplicate Telegram message accepted.
- Secret value present in public-safe output.

Every probe must fail validation.

### 22.24 Certification Statuses

Allowed certification statuses:

`not_started`:

```text
Required QSASE artifacts are not present.
```

`partial`:

```text
Some QSASE phases validate, but end-to-end certification is incomplete.
```

`blocked`:

```text
One or more hard blockers prevent certification.
```

`degraded_research_only`:

```text
Research can run, but source coverage, historical memory, quantum state,
dashboard visibility, Telegram, or PaperOps boundary is degraded.
```

`certified_shadow_only`:

```text
QSASE can research, backtest, route shadow strategies, and display decisions,
but cannot create PaperOps handoffs.
```

`certified_paper_review_handoff`:

```text
QSASE can create upstream PaperOps handoff records while preserving all PaperOps
authority boundaries.
```

`certified_end_to_end`:

```text
All QSASE phases validate, all authority flags are clean, dashboard and
Telegram are public-safe, and PaperOps compatibility is proven.
```

No certification status should imply live-capital readiness.

### 22.25 Acceptance Report Template

The final acceptance report should read like:

```text
QSASE certification: certified_paper_review_handoff
Self-model: pass
Source-price matrix: pass
Historical memory: pass
Full-universe search: pass
Linear lab: pass
Nonlinear and quantum lab: pass
Strategy Foundry: pass
Akber Filter: pass
Shadow simulator: pass
Router: pass
PaperOps handoff: pass
Learning ledger: pass
Dashboard: pass
Telegram: pass
Governance: pass
Authority violations: 0
Paper orders created by QSASE: 0
Broker writes: 0
Proof credit granted by QSASE: false
Live capital: false
```

This report should be generated from artifacts, not written by hand.

### 22.26 Acceptance Criteria Summary

QSASE acceptance means Qadam can prove three things at once.

First, Qadam can understand itself: its code, models, sources, market universe,
quantum review layer, PaperOps route, dashboard, Telegram, and limitations.

Second, Qadam can use that self-understanding to search the full source-price
universe, test linear and nonlinear patterns, generate strategy hypotheses,
route them safely, learn from outcomes, and propose improvements.

Third, Qadam can do all of that without weakening the existing safety model:
no live capital, no broker writes, no Telegram commands, no direct LLM or
quantum order authority, no unapproved strategy mutation, no backfilled proof,
no simulated paper-trial time, and no paper proof ledger credit outside real
guarded paper lifecycle evidence.

If any of those three conditions fails, QSASE is not accepted.

## 23. Current Known Gaps

The Current Known Gaps section records what Qadam already has, what QSASE still
needs, and what must deliberately remain blocked.

It asks:

```text
What prevents today's Qadam from already being QSASE, and which gaps should be
closed without weakening PaperOps, proof, governance, dashboard, or Telegram
safety?
```

This section matters because QSASE is not being created from nothing. Qadam
already has meaningful pieces: source registries, current-state pattern
recognition, edge memory, quantum-reviewed pattern gates, strategy research
intake, shadow learning modules, PaperOps lifecycle checks, proposal-only
learning updates, dashboard visibility, and Telegram quality gates. The gap is
that those pieces are not yet unified into one full-universe, historically
testable, self-aware strategy engine.

### 23.1 Gap Classification

Each gap should be classified as one of:

`implementation_gap`:

```text
The planned QSASE component does not exist yet.
```

`integration_gap`:

```text
The repo has partial pieces, but they are not wired into QSASE lineage.
```

`coverage_gap`:

```text
The needed data, historical window, instrument mapping, or source relationship
is incomplete.
```

`validation_gap`:

```text
The code path may exist, but the required acceptance check does not yet prove
the boundary.
```

`visibility_gap`:

```text
The state exists in runtime artifacts but is not visible clearly on dashboard
or Telegram.
```

`intentional_safety_boundary`:

```text
The capability is deliberately blocked and should not be treated as a missing
feature.
```

This taxonomy prevents Qadam from trying to "fix" safety boundaries that are
supposed to exist.

### 23.2 Existing Strengths To Preserve

QSASE should preserve these inherited strengths:

- PaperOps already has a canonical autonomous-pass wrapper and canonical
  summary source of truth.
- PaperOps already separates ready-idle, blocked, degraded, stale, and
  timeout/no-refresh branches.
- PaperOps already records self-healing repair requests for broken runs.
- Existing pattern recognition already uses broad source scans and quantum
  gate awareness.
- Edge Pattern Ledger already treats quantum review as a core edge gate.
- Existing learning modules already keep model weights, trust scores, strategy
  weights, and architect recommendations proposal-first.
- Existing shadow modules already avoid creating orders when learning approval
  is missing.
- Dashboard work already favors a system-map user journey over a raw module
  dump.
- Telegram quality checks already reject generic messages and separate content
  quality from delivery failure.
- Qadam's current architecture already treats local LLM, frontier LLM, and
  quantum review as intelligence layers, not execution authority.

QSASE should build on these, not bypass them.

### 23.3 Historical Source-Price Coverage Gap

Current state:

```text
Historical backfill exists, but it is not yet the full source-price memory
needed for QSASE.
```

Gap:

- Historical backfill is not yet a universal matrix across every source family
  and every trading-universe instrument.
- Source windows and price windows are not yet uniformly aligned.
- Coverage gaps are not yet represented as first-class QSASE blockers.
- Historical replay is not yet tied to every future strategy hypothesis.
- Historical records are not yet sufficient to support full-universe pattern
  discovery.

Required closure:

- Build `qsase_historical_source_price_memory`.
- Build coverage maps per source family and instrument.
- Store replay windows and missing-window reasons.
- Keep historical replay separate from current paper proof.
- Make backfill unable to advance the 30-day paper growth trial.

Boundary:

```text
Historical coverage can improve research confidence. It cannot create paper
proof ledger credit.
```

### 23.4 Universal Source-Price Matrix Gap

Current state:

```text
Qadam has source registries, source-health artifacts, watched instruments, and
strategy contexts, but not one QSASE matrix that connects all sources to all
tradable and research-only market expressions.
```

Gap:

- The entire data universe is not yet normalized into one QSASE source matrix.
- The entire trading universe is not yet normalized into one QSASE instrument
  matrix.
- Source-to-instrument edges are not yet explicit enough for systematic
  pattern search.
- Paperability is not yet attached to every instrument expression.
- Supplemental, canonical, credential-gated, degraded, and missing sources are
  not yet represented in one QSASE artifact.

Required closure:

- Build `qsase_universal_source_price_matrix`.
- Add explicit source family, freshness, trust, quorum, paperability, and
  instrument mapping fields.
- Represent Kalshi, Reddit, and Capitol Trades as source families when plugged
  in, without granting execution authority.
- Add validation that degraded or credential-gated sources cannot satisfy
  quorum by accident.

Boundary:

```text
Source presence does not imply source authority. Instrument mapping does not
imply trade authority.
```

### 23.5 Pattern Recognition Scope Gap

Current state:

```text
Existing pattern recognition is strongest as a current-state and quantum-oracle
preview surface.
```

Gap:

- It is not yet a full historical strategy-discovery backtester.
- It exposes candidate patterns, but not a complete pattern search over the
  entire historical source-price matrix.
- It is optimized for current feature compression and quantum review readiness,
  not full walk-forward research.
- It does not yet produce the complete rejected-pattern ledger QSASE needs.

Required closure:

- Build `qsase_full_universe_pattern_search`.
- Search source-to-source, source-to-price, cross-asset, lagged, and regime
  relationships.
- Write accepted and rejected candidate patterns with reasons.
- Keep pattern discovery separate from Strategy Foundry.

Boundary:

```text
A pattern is not a strategy. A pattern score is not a trade signal.
```

### 23.6 Edge Pattern Ledger Persistence Gap

Current state:

```text
The edge pattern ledger tracks candidate sleeves under observation and preserves
calendar and quantum-gate boundaries.
```

Gap:

- It does not yet provide full historical persistence for every discovered
  source-price pattern.
- It tracks a focused set of candidate sleeves, not the complete QSASE pattern
  universe.
- It is not yet the universal memory layer that links every candidate pattern
  to later backtest, shadow, paper, and attribution outcomes.

Required closure:

- Treat Edge Pattern Ledger as inherited evidence, not as the full QSASE memory
  layer.
- Build `qsase_historical_source_price_memory` and
  `qsase_component_attribution_ledger` around it.
- Preserve actual calendar behavior and same-day dedupe.

Boundary:

```text
The edge ledger can support QSASE memory. It cannot be stretched into paper
proof or universal historical coverage by assertion.
```

### 23.7 Linear Backtesting Gap

Current state:

```text
Qadam has strategy research notes, backtest requests, and partial replay
concepts, but not a QSASE linear lab that systematically tests the full source
and trading universes.
```

Gap:

- No universal linear backtesting lab exists yet.
- No standard in-sample/out-of-sample split exists for every candidate pattern.
- No standard event-study and lag-test result schema exists for QSASE.
- No standard rejected-linear-test ledger exists.
- No linear baseline exists for every strategy hypothesis.

Required closure:

- Build `qsase_linear_pattern_lab`.
- Add reproducible lag tests, event studies, simple factor tests, walk-forward
  tests, baseline comparisons, and rejection reasons.
- Add validation against overfit and in-sample-only claims.

Boundary:

```text
A strong linear backtest is research evidence, not proof and not execution
approval.
```

### 23.8 Nonlinear And Quantum Pattern Gap

Current state:

